## 🐛 Bug backlog (top priority — main job)

> Full audit with file:line evidence + refuted claims: [`docs/bugs/AUDIT-2026-10-06-parallel-audit.md`](docs/bugs/AUDIT-2026-10-06-parallel-audit.md) (2026-10-06, 4 parallel subsystem audits, high-severity items hand-verified).

### HDR / swapchain (2026-10-31 -- diagnosed and fixed)
- [x] P1 - app-hosted HDR ON dim wallpaper FIXED by R10G10B10A2_UNORM + PQ (Option 1). Root cause: platform CheckColorSpaceSupport reports PQ unsupported for R16G16B16A16_FLOAT (support=0) -> SetColorSpace1(PQ) failed 0x80070057 -> compositor read PQ values as scRGB -> dim. R10G10B10A2 + PQ supported and sets OK. Evidence: pqprobe.cpp (R16F/PQ support=0 set fails 0x80070057; R10/PQ support=1 set OK); app INIT probe + live app confirmed dim under old code; new build log "HDR10 output enabled" + "Back buffer: R10G10B10A2_UNORM HDR10 (PQ/BT.2020)" with no "SetColorSpace1 failed" line. 826/826 ctest pass. Implemented in SwapChain.cpp/.hpp, D3D12Renderer.cpp/.hpp, HdrSyncPolicy.hpp, SdrWhiteLevel.hpp, OutputDither.hpp. Updated 2026-10-31.
- [x] P1 - SDR white level query now runs at D3D12Renderer::Initialize when HDR (returns 0 on this machine -> 100-nit BT.2408 fallback). OS query via DisplayConfig still returns errors in this environment; tracked as limitation. Updated 2026-10-31.

### Release / packaging - Paiint 0.1.0 (2026-10-25)

- [x] **P0 - local test-mode diagnostics + window-aware wallpaper audio shipped in-app 2026-10-30 (code + tests; live GUI verified 2026-10-31).** Settings → Advanced → "Test mode diagnostics" (`diagnostics.enabled`, default off) runs a crash-safe local-only recorder (`app/electron/diagnostics/recorder.cjs`) writing `Downloads\Paiint Diagnostics\current.jsonl` (4 Hz for 15 s after each apply, then 1 Hz), `current-summary.json` (every 5 s), `device.json` (monitor/GPU/OS identity, no serial), and archives `sessions\<timestamp>\{session.jsonl, summary.json, device.json, runtime.log, app.log, crash.log, crashes\}` on quit; `--diagnostics` forces one instrumented session without persisting the setting. Settings → Engine → "Play audio only when Paiint is closed" (`engine.audioOnlyWhenWindowClosed`, default false) mutes the wallpaper while a Paiint window is open and unmutes on minimize/close-to-tray, overriding only by adding mute (never unmuting a user-muted wallpaper). New pure modules `audio-visibility.cjs`, `diagnostics/device-info.cjs`, `diagnostics/recorder.cjs`; recorder tests 15/15, lint 0, typecheck 0. Privacy disclosure added to `docs/legal/PRIVACY.md`. **2026-10-31 follow-up - five defects exposed by two tester archives (`Paiint Diagnostics_parkash.zip`, `s_bhanu.rar`), all FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED.** (1) Every sample was `{"error":"powerMonitor.getIdleTime is not a function"}` - Electron 43 has no `getIdleTime()`, and the throw discarded the whole sample, so window/power/memory/audio state was invisible on real machines. Now `getSystemIdleTime()` (seconds) plus `sampleField()` per-section isolation, so a bad API can only blank its own section. (2) No `audio` events existed, so a mute decision could not be audited; every decision change now writes `audio` (intent, toggle, window, occlusion, effective mute) and every sample carries the same object. (3) `FRAME_READY` (up to ~30 Hz) filled 1.2 MB in three minutes and would reach the 200 MB cap in hours; it is filtered from diagnostics while the renderer still receives it. (4) Crash recovery filed data under `recovered-<recovery-time>` and left the crashed session dir empty; recovery now reads the session record from the head of `current.jsonl`, reuses that directory, and prunes stale empty session dirs. (5) Foreground-only occlusion missed visible-but-unfocused windows (reproduces "still plays on other app"); the monitor now enumerates top-level windows in Z-order, ignoring invisible/minimized/DWM-cloaked windows, the taskbar, and windows covering <0.5% of a monitor, with the desktop window terminating the scan (Win+D = clear; clicking the desktop with windows visible = occluded). New `occlusion.cjs` + tests. **Evidence:** app suite **124/124**, lint 0, typecheck 0, build 0; live: Notepad/Chrome mute, Win+D clear→audible→occluded→muted, 120x16 `CHeadBarWindow` ignored, a 3.4 h session with 12,006 samples / 0 errors / `power.idleTime` present / 25 `audio` + 11 `occlusion` events / no `FRAME_READY`, and the crashed 12:36 session recovered into its own dir with no new empty dirs.
- [ ] **P3 - the boot-replay apply is recorded without its `apply` event and without the 4 Hz fast window - OPEN (diagnostics).** `startDiagnostics` is deferred with `setImmediate` (`main.cjs:1950`) while `applyStoredWallpaper(bootEntry)` starts in the same tick (`main.cjs:2018`), so `applyVideoEntry` calls `diagnostics?.event('apply')` / `diagnostics?.markApply(source)` (`main.cjs:1104-1105`) while `diagnostics` is still null. The `applied` event lands ~3 s later (after `runtime.init`) and is recorded, but the startup apply never starts the 15 s fast window. Evidence: session `2026-10-31T12-38-27-879Z` seq 1-12 has no `apply` event (only `applied`), the app log has no `diagnostics: fast window startup`, and only the 12:41:45 user apply produced the 4 Hz burst (59 intervals at 254 ms). Fix: start the recorder before the boot replay (await `startDiagnostics` before line 2018, or call `markApply` after `runtime.init` too). This makes the first 15 s of a cold start - INIT, first frames, the first audio decision - record at 1 Hz instead of 4 Hz.
- [ ] **P3 - `device.json`'s `gpu.devices` block is a placeholder on every machine where the UI runs with hardware acceleration off - OPEN (diagnostics).** `app/electron/diagnostics/device-info.cjs:191` calls `app.getGPUInfo('complete')`, but `main.cjs` calls `app.disableHardwareAcceleration()`, so Chromium reports one dummy device (`vendorId 0`, `deviceId 0`, null vendor/device strings, `active:false`) and every `featureStatus` entry is `disabled_software`. The real GPU is in the same file under `hardware.gpus` (WMI `Win32_VideoController`), so triage is still possible, but the `gpu` block is misleading (it can look like a broken GPU). Evidence: `Downloads\Paiint Diagnostics\device.json` 2026-10-31: `gpu.devices[0]` all zero/null vs `hardware.gpus.name = "AMD Radeon RX 6700 XT"`. Fix: when `pickGpuDevices()` yields only zero/null entries, replace them with the WMI GPUs (or drop the block and document that `hardware.gpus` is authoritative).

Upgrade path: `userdata-migrate.cjs` copies the newest of `%APPDATA%\REX` / `%APPDATA%\pg` into `%APPDATA%\Paiint` before any consumer runs and `main.cjs` pins that path; `login-item-migrate.cjs` removes the stale HKCU Run entry pointing at `REX*.exe`; the playlist engine migrates `rex.playlistEngine.v1` -> `paiint.playlistEngine.v1`. **Live:** the packaged exe created `%APPDATA%\Paiint` with 35 library records / 1 playlist / rotation `After Current Ends` queue=11, re-applied the last wallpaper, left `%APPDATA%\pg` intact, and a second launch spawned no duplicate. `pnpm lint` 0, `typecheck` 0, `build` 0, app tests **72/72** (8 new migration tests).
- [x] **P1 - the runtime wrote its diagnostics to `%TEMP%\REX` while the shell reported `%TEMP%\Paiint` - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-25.** Five native sites hardcoded the old directory (`BuildLogPath`, `WorkerWManager`, `Engine::DumpPresentStages`, `FfmpegVideoDecoder::DumpDecodeStages`, the compositor's debug trace) and the runtime settings store used `%LOCALAPPDATA%\REX`. All now use the Paiint directories; the settings file is copied once from the legacy path. **Live:** a fresh runtime creates `%TEMP%\Paiint\runtime.log`; `%TEMP%\REX` is no longer created.
- [x] **P0 - quitting leaves the last video frame on the desktop as a still, applied through the native Windows wallpaper API - DONE + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-26.** `IWallpaperEngine::CaptureFrame` queues a request to the render thread (owner of `D3D12Renderer`), which renders one capture frame through the SDR pipeline (on HDR displays `SetForceSdr(true)` + `SyncHdrStateLocked`, so the PNG carries the mpv-parity tone map instead of a PQ buffer), copies the finished back buffer, PNG-encodes it to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Paiint\last-frame.png`, and only then tears WorkerW down; the CLI applies it with `SetImageWallpaper` / `SystemParametersInfoW`. Opt-in via `SHUTDOWN {snapshot:true}` (Electron `will-quit`), so the idle reclaim after an image apply never overwrites the user's image. **Root cause of the first failure:** the readback target was a READBACK-heap *texture*, which D3D12 rejects with `E_INVALIDARG` — only buffers may live on UPLOAD/READBACK heaps (proved with a standalone repro: default-heap textures OK, upload/readback textures fail at every size/format/state, readback buffers OK). Fixed with the canonical readback **buffer** + `GetCopyableFootprints` + `CopyTextureRegion`; rows carry the 256-byte-aligned pitch (15360 at 4K), not `width*4`. The PNG is encoded to a sibling temp file and renamed with `MOVEFILE_WRITE_THROUGH`, so a kill mid-encode cannot leave a truncated file where Windows re-reads the wallpaper path at sign-in; the Electron quit fallback is 15 s for snapshot quits (5 s otherwise). **Live:** 3840x2160 PNG (207,971 B, valid signature, luma 0-255 / mean 127.8), `HKCU\Control Panel\Desktop\Wallpaper` = the file, quit 1.2 s; reclaim test (image apply, plain SHUTDOWN) left the snapshot byte-identical (sha256 match). 824/824 ctest, 76/76 app tests.
- [x] **P3 - every clean quit appended a false "VEH exception" entry to `%TEMP%\Paiint\crash.log` - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-26.** Three consecutive clean exits on 2026-10-26 (09:40:24, 09:50:27, 10:01:16) each logged `Exception 0x0000071A` with the same stack: `WTSUnRegisterSessionNotification -> ... -> RpcRaiseException -> RaiseException`, at the same second as `[Runtime] Wallpaper runtime shutdown complete`. **Root cause:** the VEH crash logger records every first-chance exception, and unregistering WTS session notifications raises a benign RPC exception (0x71A) while the session-notification window is destroyed — the shutdown itself always completed correctly. **Fix:** a `g_teardownStarted` atomic is set when a deliberate exit begins (the SHUTDOWN command and the stdin-EOF cleanup path); `CrashHandler` ignores **only** `0x71A` **only** after that flag is set. Real crashes — and the same code at any other time — are still recorded. **Live:** a snapshot quit wrote the 3840x2160 PNG and left `crash.log` unchanged at 420 lines (previously every quit appended a block).
- [ ] **P1 - the portable tester exe is unsigned (`Get-AuthenticodeSignature` = `NotSigned`) - OPEN, but not on the Store path.** Windows SmartScreen shows "Unknown publisher" for the portable `.exe`, which is a real adoption blocker for handing builds to strangers. The Store package is signed by Microsoft during certification, so the shipping route is unaffected; this stays open only for direct/portable distribution (needs an OV/EV certificate or Azure Trusted Signing wired into `electron-builder`'s `win.signingHashAlgorithms` / `signtool` step).

The two remaining inputs do not block local builds or the About page. **Public repo live 2026-11-01:** `https://github.com/REX-1320/Paiint` now holds a clean public snapshot — source + docs only, **802 files / 6.8 MB**, single root commit `baada7a`, no `vcpkg_installed` trees, no internal notes (`TODO.md`, `everything.md`, `#project_status.md`, `fix.md`, research dumps, `dump/`, `sessions/`) and no old history; `origin` still points at the private working repo `REX_Wallpaper_Engine`. - [x] **P0 - image wallpaper apply and the quit snapshot were expected to fail in the MSIX build (HKCU and AppData are virtualized) - FIXED + DEV VERIFIED 2026-10-27; packaged smoke test tracked below.** `SetImageWallpaper` now applies stills through `IDesktopWallpaper::SetWallpaper(NULL, path)` (shell COM, unvirtualized, position mapped to `DESKTOP_WALLPAPER_POSITION`) and only falls back to the legacy `RegSetKeyValueW` + `SystemParametersInfoW(SPI_SETDESKWALLPAPER)` pair when `GetCurrentPackageFullName` reports **not packaged**, so the portable build keeps its old behaviour and the MSIX never touches the redirected hive. The snapshot moved to the real Pictures known folder: `LastFrameImagePath()` resolves `SHGetKnownFolderPath(FOLDERID_Pictures)` → `Pictures\Paiint\last-frame.png`, and `lastFrameSnapshotPath()` in Electron mirrors it with `app.getPath('pictures')`, so Explorer can read the file and an uninstall cannot orphan the live wallpaper. **Dev verified:** image apply logged `wallpaper applied via IDesktopWallpaper: ...` with the registry path/style set; quit snapshot wrote `C:\Users\Moinu\Pictures\Paiint\last-frame.png` (3840x2160, valid PNG) and the desktop kept it, while the stale `%LOCALAPPDATA%` copy stayed untouched. Historical root cause (kept for the record): `SetImageWallpaper` (`rex-core/src/wallpaper_cli_main.cpp:514`) writes `HKCU\Control Panel\Desktop\Wallpaper` with `RegSetKeyValueW` and then calls `SystemParametersInfoW(SPI_SETDESKWALLPAPER)`; the quit snapshot writes `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Paiint\last-frame.png` (`wallpaper_cli_main.cpp:137-144`). MSIX redirects **all HKCU writes** to a private per-package hive and **all AppData writes** to `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\<PFN>\LocalCache`, so Explorer never sees the new registry value and cannot read the snapshot path at all. The 2026-10-25 MSIX spike proved *video* rendering through WorkerW, not image apply. **Fix direction (shipped as described above):** apply stills through `IDesktopWallpaper::SetWallpaper` (shell COM, unvirtualized) instead of direct registry writes + SPI, and move the snapshot outside AppData to `%USERPROFILE%\Pictures\Paiint\last-frame.png` so Explorer can read it and uninstall cannot orphan the active wallpaper. **Still owed:** the same two operations inside an installed MSIX (see the packaged smoke-test item below).
- [x] **P1 - "Launch at login" is a no-op in the MSIX build - FIXED + DEV VERIFIED 2026-10-27; packaged logon launch tracked below.** Root cause: `applyLoginItem` used Electron's `app.setLoginItemSettings`, which writes `HKCU\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Run`; MSIX redirects that write to the package's private hive, so Windows never reads the Run entry at logon and the checkbox looked enabled while doing nothing. Shipped fix: `packaging/msix/AppxManifest.xml` declares `desktop:Extension Category="windows.startupTask"` (`TaskId="PaiintStartup"`, `Enabled="false"`, `DisplayName="Paiint"`, `Executable="resources\bin\paiint-startup.exe"`) with the `desktop` namespace in `IgnorableNamespaces`; the tiny `rex-core/tools/StartupStub.cpp` → `paiint-startup.exe` is the only process with package identity, so it owns `Windows.ApplicationModel.StartupTask` — `--status` / `--enable` / `--disable` print `enabled | disabled | disabled-by-user | disabled-by-policy | enabled-by-policy | unpackaged | error` (`--enable` counts `enabled-by-policy` as success, and `--disable` re-reads the state so a policy veto is reported rather than claimed as success), and a bare launch starts `Paiint.exe --startup-task`. Electron side: `app/electron/startup-task.cjs` (6 tests) queries that helper, caches the answer for 10 s so tray menu rebuilds never block on a child process, distinguishes a missing stub (`null` → Run key) from a stub that exists but never answers (`unavailable`, logged), `applyLoginItem` / `loginItemEnabled` prefer the task and fall back to the Run key when it reports `unpackaged` (portable), `--startup-task` honours the live `startup.startMinimized` (deferred tray-only start when true, window when false), the first-run registration is deferred off the startup path, and changing `startup.startMinimized` re-registers the portable Run key so its baked `--background` flag matches. `app/package.json` ships the stub via `extraResources`; `build-msix.ps1` fails the build if the stub or any extension executable is missing from the payload. **Dev verified:** `--startup-task` + `startMinimized=true` logged `window: deferred (startup task, start minimised)` with no page and a live tray; `startMinimized=false` logged `window: shown (normal launch)`; Run-key enable wrote `com.paiint.desktop` without `--background`, flipping the preference rewrote it with `--background`, disable removed it. **Still owed:** an installed-package logon launch and the Settings toggle inside the MSIX (tracked below).
- [ ] **P0 - the MSIX lifecycle fixes are dev-verified but not yet package-verified - BLOCKED on a sideloadable environment (no admin, no Developer Mode).** The 2026-10-28 Store-identity package (`packaging/msix/out/Paiint-0.1.0-x64.msix`, 174.9 MB / 183,386,444 B, SHA256 `7A52A9F8DC3894485EE1310BE0D3294B85404B218DD4BFE676D8C54A4A511A34`, identity `Moinu.Paiint` / `CN=9D8DC345-5B4D-4571-831E-2EBC542FFA47` / `Moinu`) contains `resources\bin\paiint-startup.exe`, the `windows.startupTask` extension and the validated manifest, and every changed code path was exercised **outside** a package (dev Electron + the unpacked payload). What remains is the same four operations with real package identity: (1) apply a still and confirm `wallpaper applied via IDesktopWallpaper` with no `HKCU` write, (2) quit and confirm `%USERPROFILE%\Pictures\Paiint\last-frame.jpg` is written and stays the desktop wallpaper, (3) enable "launch at login", confirm `paiint-startup.exe --status` reports `enabled` and that a logon (or `schtasks`/Start-menu run of the task) starts Paiint with `--startup-task` and no window while `startup.startMinimized` is true, (4) confirm the quit drain still finishes inside the package. **Blocked:** `Add-AppxPackage` needs admin or Developer Mode (`HKLM\SOFTWARE\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\AppModelUnlock\AllowDevelopmentWithoutDevLicense` is absent) and no package is installed on this machine; enabling either needs a UAC prompt. Run `packaging\msix\build-msix.ps1 -Sign` with a self-signed cert (or enable Developer Mode) when that is available, then close this item. The Store-identity package cannot be sideloaded without a matching certificate, so use a placeholder-identity (`CN=MoiNeX` / `MoiNeX.Paiint`) `-Sign` build for this test.
- [x] **P2 - the app and tray icons are a generated capital-P placeholder - DONE 2026-10-26 (final artwork supplied).** The mark is the gold/black four-petal emblem, committed as `app/assets/paiint-mark.png` (transparent PNG, the supplied master). `app/tools/make-icons.py` crops it to content, fits it on a transparent square and writes `app/assets/paiint-app.ico` (9 sizes, PNG frames), `app/assets/paiint-tray.ico` (8 sizes) and `app/src/assets/paiint-mark.png` (512 px for the About page); `app/scripts/make-tray-icon.ps1` (the placeholder generator) is deleted. The About page shows the mark with no tile or border, and the MSIX tile assets are generated from the same ICO.
- [x] **P2 - packaging read the stale `rex-core/.build` tree, so a fresh native build could ship an old runtime - FIXED 2026-10-25.** `extraResources` now points at `../rex-core/build/Release` — the tree `cmake --build rex-core/build` actually writes — so packaging cannot pick up a stale copy. The broken `.build` cache (it points at a deleted temp toolchain) is now unused; deleting `rex-core/.build` is safe (its unique binaries are superseded, and `paiint-minidump.exe` was already rebuilt into `build/Release`).
- [x] **P0 - the Microsoft Store (MSIX) route was the one unproven assumption behind the $0 release: does a packaged build still own the desktop? - PROVEN 2026-10-25, PIPELINE SHIPPED 2026-10-26.** A throwaway package (`MoiNeX.Paiint` 0.1.0.0 x64, `Windows.FullTrustApplication` + `rescap:runFullTrust`, 172.8 MB) was built with the `makeappx.exe` / `signtool.exe` that already ship in `C:\Program Files (x86)\Windows Kits\10\App Certification Kit` (**no Windows SDK install needed**), sideloaded, and launched from `C:\Program Files\WindowsApps\MoiNeX.Paiint_0.1.0.0_x64__dq8nm63p1qsda`. The packaged runtime spawned from the package's own `resources\bin\paiint-wallpaper-runtime.exe`, and the engine's own verification passed: `[WorkerW] Verify OK: render window parented to wallpaper WorkerW 0x900636, WS_CHILD, not topmost` + `[Engine] Frame 240: 3.21 s`. Independent window-tree proof: `REXRenderWindow` (pid = packaged runtime) -> `REXSyntheticWorkerW` (0x900636) -> `Progman` (explorer.exe, pid 1156). Reproducible across a restart (`startup: re-applied last wallpaper video D:\live wallpaper\marvel-spiderman-miles-morales.1920x1080.mp4`), and the packaged runtime read `D:\live wallpaper\*.mp4` with no extra capability — full trust covers arbitrary paths. **Store-relevant findings:** (1) MSIX path virtualisation is copy-on-write, **not** isolation — reads fall through to the real `%APPDATA%\Paiint`, so a packaged build inherits a portable profile once and then forks (`%LOCALAPPDATA%\Packages\<family>\LocalCache\Roaming\Paiint`); `%TEMP%` is not redirected, so the runtime keeps writing `%TEMP%\Paiint`. (2) `requestSingleInstanceLock()` keys off the userData path *string*, which is identical under MSIX, so the packaged app silently exits while the portable build runs — harmless for Store users, but there is no side-by-side. (3) Sideloading needs admin or Developer Mode (`Add-AppxPackage` validates against `LocalMachine`; `-AllowUnsigned` fails `0x80073D2C` when Developer Mode is off) — irrelevant for the Store, where Microsoft signs. Spike cleaned up: package uninstalled, test certificate removed from all five stores, `app-state.json` + `last-wallpaper.json` restored, portable build relaunched.
- [x] **P0 - the MSIX packaging pipeline now lives in the repo - DONE 2026-10-26.** `packaging/msix/` holds `AppxManifest.xml` (tokens `__VERSION__` / `__PUBLISHER__`), `build-msix.ps1`, `README.md` and generated `Assets/`. The script reads the version from `app/package.json`, maps semver to the 4-part MSIX version, stages `app\dist-exe\win-unpacked`, generates the seven tile PNGs from `app\assets\paiint-app.ico` (the ICO is PNG-compressed, so it parses the directory and decodes the largest frame instead of using `System.Drawing.Icon`'s size constructor), expands and validates the manifest against every asset/executable it references, then runs `makeappx`. **Proven end to end:** `makeappx` packed `Paiint-0.1.0-x64.msix` (174.9 MB, SHA256 `76854DAD…9ED315DF`); unpacking showed identity `MoiNeX.Paiint 0.1.0.0 x64`, `Paiint.exe`, the native runtime (hash-identical to the built exe), all 7 assets and all five legal documents. `-SkipBuild`, `-SkipPack`, `-Sign` (off by default) and `-Publisher` are documented. Store notes in the README: the publisher must equal Partner Center's exact `CN=...`, the revision part must be 0, and Microsoft signs the package (no certificate budget needed).
- [x] **P1 - the About page shipped in the app, with the legal documents readable inside it - DONE + VISUALLY VERIFIED 2026-10-26; moved to its own sidebar tab 2026-10-26.** `app/src/about/AboutPage.jsx` (sidebar → About) shows the gold mark, product name, tagline, live version, publisher MoiNeX, engine name, licence, copyright, the no-telemetry/no-network privacy statement, and FFmpeg/LGPL third-party notes. It is no longer a collapsed section at the bottom of Settings: `App.jsx` renders the page for `tab === 'About'`, and Settings now lists only Startup, Window, Engine, New wallpapers and Files. Four buttons open the EULA, privacy policy, third-party notices and the generated JavaScript licence texts in an in-app `Dialog` — no external editor or browser needed. The renderer asks for an allowlisted **key** (`eula` / `privacy` / `notices` / `jsLicenses`), never a path: `app/electron/legal-docs.cjs` resolves it to the dev `docs/legal` tree or the packaged `resources/legal` copy, and `app:read-legal` refuses anything else. `extraResources` now stages `../docs/legal` → `legal`. New `legal-docs.test.cjs` (5/5) pins the allowlist, both path rules, the packaged staging entry and that every document exists. `pnpm lint` 0, `typecheck` 0, app suite 81/81.
- [x] **P1 - the bundled JavaScript licence texts are collected and shipped - DONE 2026-10-26.** `app/tools/collect-js-licenses.cjs` walks the real production tree (`pnpm licenses list --prod --json`), reads each package's `LICENSE` / `COPYING` / `NOTICE` verbatim and writes `docs/legal/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES_JS.md`; packages that publish no licence file get the canonical text of the licence they declare (MIT / ISC / BSD-3 / 0BSD / Unlicense / MIT AND ISC). **188 packages, 403 KB, 0 without licence text**; `--check` fails when the document is stale. `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` now lists the licence counts and points at the generated document.
- [ ] **P1 - a renderer crash puts the app in a report/reload loop with no backoff: 2,538 crash folders (~450 MB of logs) in 71 s - OPEN (root cause of the loop known; the trigger crash is not).** `%TEMP%\Paiint\crashes\` filled from `2026-10-25T16-52-20-819Z` to `16-53-31-725Z` (~36 reports/s) while one main process (`mainPid 6948`) stayed alive. Every `report.json` is identical in kind: `reason: "renderer gone (crashed)"`, `exitCode: -2147483645` (`0x80000003` — Chromium kills a renderer that sends a bad IPC message), `extra.renderer.exitCode` the same. So this is **not** runtime restarts and **not** a force-kill (measured: killing the app produced **0** new folders). Each report copies the full `runtime.log` (177,459 B), and `CrashReporter.writeReport` (`app/electron/supervisor/crash-reporter.cjs:74-101`) makes one directory per report and **never prunes**, so a renderer that dies instantly on every reload fills the disk. Next: (1) find what made the renderer send a bad message (the report has no stack — add `--enable-logging`/`crashDumps` capture for the next occurrence), (2) rate-limit `render-process-gone` reporting (one folder per crash identity per minute) and back off the reload after consecutive instant crashes, (3) cap retained crash folders.
- [ ] **P3 - `loadLastApplied` returns `null` for a `last-wallpaper.json` that starts with a UTF-8 BOM, so boot restore silently does nothing - OPEN.** `app/electron/startup.cjs:33-40` runs `JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(..., 'utf8'))` inside a bare `try { } catch { return null }`, which makes a BOM (rejected by `JSON.parse`) indistinguishable from "no wallpaper was ever applied": no restore, no log line. Found while driving the MSIX spike from PowerShell (`Set-Content -Encoding UTF8` in Windows PowerShell 5.1 writes a BOM) and confirmed — after rewriting the file BOM-free the same launch logged `startup: re-applied last wallpaper video …`. Paiint always writes BOM-free JSON, so this only bites hand-edited or externally-written state, but the failure is silent; fix is two lines (`raw.replace(/^\uFEFF/, '')` plus logging the parse failure).

### Playlist scoping + Stop + quit snapshot (2026-10-26)

- [x] **P0 — the apply SOURCE now decides playlist governance, so a gallery apply can never inherit a live playlist's rules — FIXED + VERIFIED 2026-10-28.** Follow-up to the scoping fix below: `store.applyWallpaper(id, {source, playlist})` takes the apply source and `playlistScopeForApply` (`app/src/wallpapers/playlist/scope.js`) maps it — a **gallery** apply writes an explicit `'None'`, a **playlist** apply writes that playlist's name, and an **engine rotation** leaves the tag alone. `PlaylistDock.jsx`, `PlaylistStrip.jsx` and `playlist/engine.js` pass and consume the new shape. 3 regression tests added; scope tests **11/11**, full app suite **103/103**.
- [x] **P0 — playlist rules leaked onto gallery files that were not members: applying a file while a playlist was live kept rotating on the old playlist's Timer/scene-end — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-26.** Root cause: `buildRotationPlan()` / `fireChange()` fell back to `activePlaylist()`, which resolved through `state.lastAppliedPath`; manual gallery applies never wrote that field, so the engine kept the previous playlist's identity and kept firing its rules over a file outside it. New pure `app/src/wallpapers/playlist/scope.js` treats the store's **applied record** as authoritative (`appliedWallpaper` → `playlistFor`), uses `lastAppliedPath` only when no applied record exists (boot), and `buildRotationPlan()` treats a non-member as `null` (stop the plan). 8 unit cases (`scope.test.js`). **Live:** a Nature plan existed (queue 2); double-clicking non-member `black-myth-wukong-moewalls-com.mp4` cleared main's scheduler (`playlistId/playlistName/mode` null) and `getLastApplied` reported the new file.
- [x] **P0 — Stop button beside `applied`: kill the video and leave the last frame as a still — DONE + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-26.** `PlaylistDock` renders a `stop` action only while the dock playlist is applied; `playlistEngine.stopRotation()` clears the local timer/plan, marks the applied file's `currentPlaylist` as explicit `'None'`, clears `state.lastAppliedPath`, mirrors `syncPlaylistPlan(null)` to main, then calls `wallpaper:stop` → `stopWallpaperForSnapshot()` (`SHUTDOWN {snapshot:true, intentional:true}`). Main also clears its in-memory `lastApplied`, so a recreated window cannot re-adopt the stopped scene; `last-wallpaper.json` is untouched, so a full restart still restores. **Bug found live and fixed:** explicit `'None'` resolved to the real `pl-0` None sentinel, handing the engine that playlist's default **Timer** settings and restarting rotation after Stop; the resolver now returns `NO_PLAYLIST` for an explicit None. **Live:** plan `playlistId/mode` null, stop button removed, badge back to `apply`, snapshot 3840x2160 (11.7 MB), `HKCU\Control Panel\Desktop\Wallpaper` = `%LOCALAPPDATA%\Paiint\last-frame.png` (moved to `%USERPROFILE%\Pictures\Paiint\last-frame.png` on 2026-10-27 — see the MSIX P0 entry), runtime exited 0; after `Page.reload` the plan stayed null and the stopped video was not re-adopted.
- [x] **P0 — closing the window could tear the runtime down before it finished the quit snapshot — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-27.** `will-quit` sent `SHUTDOWN {snapshot:true}` and returned immediately, so Electron completed its own teardown and killed the child while the render thread was still capturing: the quit ended abruptly instead of waiting for the runtime's own `Wallpaper runtime shutdown complete`, and the snapshot could be stale or missing. `wallpaperRuntime.stop()` now returns a Promise that resolves on the runtime's exit event or a bounded deadline (15 s when a snapshot was requested, 5 s otherwise), and `will-quit` calls `event.preventDefault()`, awaits that drain, then re-quits as a `setImmediate` macrotask (a resolved `stop()` settles as a microtask inside the emission, where a re-entrant `app.quit()` can be swallowed) behind `quitDrainFinished` / `quitDrainStarted` flags: the second pass through `will-quit` is allowed, while a second quit during the drain keeps waiting. The pre-drain cleanup is wrapped so a throw cannot leave the quit prevented with nothing scheduled. **Live:** three consecutive closes took 1.6–3.3 s, each wrote a fresh 3840x2160 PNG, the runtime exited 0 and the log ended with `app: runtime drained, quitting` / `Wallpaper runtime shutdown complete`; a close with no runtime process (the still-image path) also exited cleanly with no windowless process left behind. Verified both ways: with `engine.snapshotOnQuit=false` the same close sent `SHUTDOWN` with no snapshot and left `last-frame.png` byte-identical.
- [x] **P1 — quit-snapshot toggle in Settings — DONE + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-26.** `engine.snapshotOnQuit` (default `true`) in `app-state-manager.cjs`; Settings → Engine → “Leave the last frame on exit” with explanatory text; `will-quit` passes `{ snapshot: snapshotOnQuit }` to `runtime.stop`. **Live:** the switch persisted `true → false → true` through `appState.get('engine.snapshotOnQuit')`.
- [x] **P2 — dev launches silently tested yesterday's runtime: `getWallpaperCliPath()` preferred `rex-core/.build/Release` (no quit-snapshot support) over the canonical `rex-core/build/Release` — FIXED 2026-10-26.** Dev resolution now prefers `build`, falls back to `.build`; packaged builds already used `resources/bin`. Found because the Stop test wrote no snapshot until the path was corrected.

### Playlist apply burst / double-apply (2026-10-28)

- [x] **P0 - one click on the dock's "Apply playlist" produced a burst of applies and left main's scheduler `{active:false, playlistId:null}` while the dock still showed "Apply playlist" - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-28.** Live evidence in `%TEMP%\paiint-app.log`: the 15:48 burst had no scheduler/history entries; 15:55:48 showed the renderer applying `miles-morales` at `.458`, then main applying `obanai` at `.814` (`reason=Timer elapsed`) and the renderer applying `Mind Blowing` at 15:55:49.598; 15:59:55 showed main applying `igris` at `.525` and the renderer applying `259235` at `.837`. **Six interacting root causes, all fixed:** (1) **stale timer identity** — a playlist apply mirrored the playlist's persisted (already overdue) `remainingMs`, so `_applyTimerPlan` advanced to the member AFTER the one just applied; `playlist-scheduler.cjs` now restarts the interval on a playlist change or an externally accepted path change (`restart` option → `nextDueAt = at + total`), and the renderer resets the target playlist's timer (`restartPlaylistTimer`: `accumulatedMs=0`, `startedAt=now`, `running=true` for Timer, `_playedOnce=[]`). (2) **the old plan kept running during the apply** — the engine only mirrored the new plan on the 150 ms debounce, so the previous playlist's Timer could fire while the new file/pool was loading; `store.js` now announces every apply synchronously (`subscribeWallpaperApplied` / `notifyWallpaperApplied`, fired AFTER `bump()` so the 0 ms plan sync wins over the debounced one) and `engine.js: handleWallpaperApplied` sends `syncPlaylistPlan(null)` at the click for gallery/playlist applies. (3) **a post-apply plan re-anchored main to the previous file** — the renderer's 500 ms tick built a plan from the store record before `adoptAppliedPath` landed; the scheduler now remembers `lastAppliedFrom` and ignores an incoming path that is the file it just rotated away from for `SETTLE_AFTER_APPLY_MS` (2.5 s), and the engine prefers the synchronous `state.lastAppliedPath` in `buildRotationPlan()`. (4) **the adopted record kept an old explicit `'None'` tag** — after the first scheduler rotation `playlistFor` resolved `NO_PLAYLIST`, `buildRotationPlan()` returned `null`, main's plan was cleared and the dock fell back to "Apply playlist"; `handlePlaylistApplied` now tags the adopted record with `event.playlistName`. (5) **mid-apply races** — an in-flight native apply could finish after the user's apply and overwrite the cursor/timer; `intentGeneration` + `_planToken` (playlist id + mode + queue) invalidate its result, and the engine blocks plan syncs while an applied event is being adopted (`schedulerApplyPending`) and sequence-stamps sends (`planSendSeq`) so an older invoke cannot claim the current signature. (6) **stale governing playlist** — `tick()` only updated `currentPlaylistId` when it was truthy, so a gallery apply kept evaluating the previous playlist's Random Interval/schedule; a null id now clears it. `timerRemainingMs` also clamps a negative elapsed time. **Tests:** `node --test app\electron\playlist-scheduler.test.cjs` **24/24** (7 new regressions: fresh-playlist overdue timer, same-playlist user apply restart, stale post-apply plan, null-plan stop, empty-queue inert, plan replaced mid-apply, user apply mid-apply), `scope.test.js` **14/14**, full app suite **113/113**, `pnpm lint` 0 errors (pre-existing warnings only), `pnpm typecheck` clean. Files: `app/electron/playlist-scheduler.cjs`, `app/electron/playlist-scheduler.test.cjs`, `app/src/wallpapers/playlist/engine.js`, `app/src/wallpapers/store.js`. **Live verify 2026-10-28:** applying Favorites (Timer 1 min) from the dock produced exactly one apply at the click (`miles-morales`), `getPlaylistSchedulerState()` reported `active:true` / `playlistId:pl-1` / `currentPath` = that member / `nextDueAt` 60 s out, and 8 s later the desktop still showed the same file - no burst. A gallery apply of another member cleared the plan (`playlistId:null`) and returned the dock to "Apply playlist".
- [x] **P2 — the playlist chip for the file on the desktop had no visible outline — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-28.** The strip already added `.playing` to the matching chip, but the rule reused the drop-target's translucent ring (55 % alpha `--gold-border`) instead of the gallery tile's opaque `--gold`, so over a bright poster it read as no outline. `.dock-chip.playing` now draws the gallery tile's ring (1px `--gold-border` + opaque 2px `--gold` + soft `--gold-muted` glow), and `appliedMemberKey` in `app/src/wallpapers/playlist/scope.js` shares the membership path rule between the strip and the engine (3 new tests, scope **14/14**). **Live:** a gallery apply moved the ring to the newly applied member; applying Favorites outlined its first member while the plan ran; the screenshot shows the gold ring on the active chip.
- [ ] **P3 — a native apply already in flight cannot be cancelled (residual, live-only).** If the user applies another file in the exact window between the Timer firing and the runtime finishing that LOAD, the stale frame can still reach the desktop for a moment; the scheduler/engine now discard its state (no cursor/timer/rotation event), and the user's own LOAD/plan settles over it. Fixing the frame itself would need a cancellable runtime LOAD, which the protocol does not have. Not observed as a user-visible defect after the fixes; keep on the list only if a live run reproduces a flash.

### Quit speed, close-to-quit and sidebar mark (2026-10-28)

- [x] **P1 — the quit snapshot was a 3.8 MB PNG and cost ~1 s of the exit; it is a JPEG now — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-28.** The native capture writes a JPEG instead of a PNG: `D3D12Renderer::CaptureFrameToJpeg` / `WriteBgraJpeg` (WIC, quality 0.95, 24bppBGR), and the engine, the runtime, Electron main and `docs/legal/PRIVACY.md` all name `last-frame.jpg` under `%USERPROFILE%\Pictures\Paiint`. **Live:** the snapshot was written **263 ms** after close and applied as the still at **273 ms**, total exit **1.25 s** (previously ~1.03 s to snapshot / 2.7 s total); the JPEG is 3840x2160, **710,323 B** (the PNG was 3,792,590 B). Native build succeeds, `ctest` **824/824** (14 skipped/disabled as before).
- [x] **P1 — closing the window kept Paiint alive in the tray by default — CHANGED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-28.** `tray.closeToTray` now defaults to **false**, so closing the window quits Paiint and leaves the last frame as a still. Settings → Window carries a `PropertyToggle` "Quit Paiint when the window closes", **ON** by default; turning it off keeps the app in the tray with the wallpaper playing. **Live log:** `window: close -> quit (tray.closeToTray = false)`.
- [x] **P2 — the sidebar showed the `Y0!` ShinyText wordmark instead of the Paiint mark — FIXED 2026-10-28.** The transparent mark (`app/src/assets/paiint-mark.png`, 512x512) replaces the wordmark as `.sidebar-logo` at 96x96 with a soft drop shadow; `pnpm build` emits `dist/assets/paiint-mark-BmmKrrLm.png`.

### Gallery / thumbnails (2026-10-21)

- [x] **P1 — HEVC videos had no gallery/playlist poster because the app's own `disableHardwareAcceleration()` removed Chromium's HEVC decoder — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-21 (GPU thumbnail worker).** **Root cause:** the UI runs software-composited (0 MB parked VRAM), which also disables Chromium's HEVC decode: every HEVC MKV fails `<video>` with `error 4 · DEMUXER_ERROR_NO_SUPPORTED_STREAMS` (A/B on the same files in a bare Electron window: HW off = all fail, HW on = HailMary 3840x1912 / Spider-Man 3840x2160 / Odyssey 3840x2024 decode in 100-730 ms). The 21 existing posters were pre-change leftovers or H.264/static files. **Fix (option A):** `app/electron/thumb-worker.cjs` — a short-lived, hardware-accelerated Electron process that decodes ONE poster and exits, so the parked UI keeps its zero-VRAM budget; `poster-worker.cjs` spawns it (serialised, coalesced per path, 30 s cap) and writes the WebP straight into `<userData>/thumbnails`; IPC `wallpaper:fallback-thumbnail` + preload bridge; the renderer tries it whenever its own decode returns null — in the gallery restore loop (then persists the path to the library) and in fresh imports (`gallery.js buildFileRecord`; deferred restores stay cheap). Posters also improved: sampling is now **20 % into the clip** (capped at 600 s) with flat-frame rejection (luma mean/std), instead of a fixed 1 s — the old rule produced black/logo frames for movies (Spider-Man's old poster was a 1 KB black frame; the worker now finds a real scene). The shared thumb cache gained a subscription so dock chips light up live. **Evidence:** standalone worker — HailMary 5.9 KB in 634 ms, Spider-Man 27.9 KB in 973 ms, Odyssey 7.6 KB in 420 ms, Oppenheimer `media-error` (4:4:4); live library **21/25 -> 23/25** videos with posters; after restart **27/28 tiles have images** (HailMary/Spider-Man from the persisted WebP, no worker spawn), dock strip **9/10 chips** with posters without a reload. `node --test app/electron/thumb-worker.test.cjs` 6/6, build/lint/palette green. **Remaining:** Oppenheimer (HEVC 4:4:4) still cannot be decoded by Chromium even with GPU — it retries one GPU worker per launch (~2 s) and needs the native `rex-core` frame grab (option B) to ever get a poster; `inspirational-power-of-spiderman.1920x1080.mp4` is deleted from disk (stale library entry, no poster possible).

### Playlist config dialog (2026-10-21)
- [x] **P2 - Native number spinners duplicated the stepper buttons - FIXED 2026-10-21.** Every numeric field (Hours, Minutes, Idle Minutes, Min/Max Seconds, temperature) is a native `<input type="number">`, so Chromium painted its own spinners *inside* the box while the app already puts increase / decrease chevrons beside it - two sets of steppers, the native ones in the browser's own colours. Hidden app-wide (`appearance: textfield` + `::-webkit-inner-spin-button { -webkit-appearance: none }`), the field stays editable and the chevrons remain the only stepper.
- [x] **P2 - The transition picker offered 6 effects while the engine defines 23 - FIXED 2026-10-21.** The dialog's simple list was `Fade / Slide / Zoom / Blur / None / Random`, but the engine already carries a full catalogue (`TRANSITION_CATEGORIES` in `app/src/wallpapers/playlist/engine.js`: Basic, Movement, Blur, Distortion, Particles, Shader, 3D). The dialog now offers all of them, grouped and deduplicated (25 entries incl. None and Random - live: 8 Basic, 5 Movement, 3 Blur, 3 Distortion, 2 Particles, 1 Shader, 1 Custom), and every effect got its own preview animation (14 new `.pl-a-*` keyframe sets: crossfade, circle, curtain, flip, rise, fall, vortex, tunnel, smear, distort, morph, particle, starfield, custom). `PropertyDropdown` learned optional groups (`SelectGroup` + `SelectLabel`) so the list stays readable.
- [ ] **P1 - The engine cuts straight to the next wallpaper: the transition catalogue is only previewed, never blended - OPEN (engine work).** There is no transition blending in the native engine (`rex-core` has no scene crossfade; the only `Transition` hits are D3D12 resource barriers) and the app never passes a transition to the runtime - `transitionName()` in `engine.js` picks a name, which reaches the history log and the dialog preview, while the desktop switch is a hard cut. The dialog now says so under the preview ("The preview animates the effect. The engine still cuts straight to the next wallpaper - blending between scenes is engine work") so the picker cannot be mistaken for a promise. **Implementing it needs the native engine** (keep the outgoing scene alive, blend the two surfaces over the transition duration, honour the curve). Blocked for now on purpose: the engine tree carries uncommitted WIP (`VideoPlaybackController`, `AudioPlayer`, `SdrWhiteLevel`, `SwapChain` all modified 2026-10-21) and rebuilding the runtime binary from a half-finished tree risks the wallpaper path. It wants its own scoped pass: engine change -> ctest -> live A/B on a video playlist.


- [x] **P2 - Every dropdown in the playlist settings dialog opened BEHIND the dialog - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-21.** Root cause: Radix portals its popups (select / dropdown / popover / tooltip) to `<body>` with a `z-50` utility, while the dialog overlay sits at `z-index: 100000` - so the option list was painted under the dialog and unreachable. Fix: one unlayered rule in `app/src/index.css` lifts the whole Radix portal layer above every overlay in the app (`z-index: 100100`), which repairs the same latent fault in any other dialog/dropdown pair. Live: the "Change Wallpaper" list now paints in front of the dialog (`elementFromPoint` at the list returns the option, z = 100100 vs the overlay's 100000).
- [x] **P2 - Escape closed the whole dialog while a dropdown was open, throwing the choice away - FIXED 2026-10-21.** The dialog listened for Escape on `document` and closed unconditionally. It now ignores Escape while a Radix popup is open (capture phase, so the popup is still in the DOM when it is checked) - live: Escape closes only the list, the next Escape closes the dialog. Note: the guard has to look for the popup *slots* (`[data-slot="select-content"]` etc.); Radix's Select does not render a `[data-radix-popper-content-wrapper]`.
- [x] **P2 - The dialog was twice as wide as it needed to be - FIXED 2026-10-21.** `.pl-dialog` went from `min(760px, ...)` to `min(380px, ...)` (live: 372 px). The playlist picker in the head was a `PropertyDropdown` with an empty label (an empty label column and a hover row) - it is a plain select now.
- [x] **P3 - Unseen faults in the same dialog, found while fixing the above - FIXED 2026-10-21.** A wallpaper without a `playlist` object threw inside the render (`w.playlist.currentPlaylist` -> `w.playlist?.currentPlaylist`) and blanked the dialog; missing settings keys made a Switch uncontrolled (`checked={Boolean(settings.options[key])}` for the playlist-option / behavior / multi-monitor / filter / rule toggles); the transition list started with "None" instead of the engine default "Fade"; the advanced sections put the diagnostics above the management actions (Manage Playlists + Presets now come before History / Statistics / Developer Options). Unused `Shield` import and `mergeWith` state removed (lint warnings gone).

### Playlist engine - runtime state (2026-10-21)

- [x] **P1 - The playlist's "active playlist" assignment is not persisted, so every renderer reload silently kills the change modes again - FIXED 2026-10-22.** `wallpaper.playlist.currentPlaylist` is runtime-only, so after any renderer reload every wallpaper fell back to the pristine `'None'` and the change modes stopped until the user re-applied the playlist. The engine no longer depends on that name: `engine.js: playlistFor()` falls back to the playlist that **holds** the applied file (membership is persisted in app-state), and `activePlaylist()` falls back to the runtime's `lastAppliedPath` at boot. A `'None'` the user picks in the current session is marked `playlist.explicit` by `updateWallpaperProperty` (runtime-only, like the name) and still stops rotation; the untouched default `'None'` no longer masquerades as an explicit opt-out. **Proven live:** after a forced renderer reload the engine rotated `miles -> obanai -> TheOdyssey.mkv` on ENDED without re-applying the playlist. Remaining cosmetic gap: the dock's gold "applied" badge still reads the runtime-only name and shows unset after a reload; the rotation itself is correct (P3 below).
- [x] **P3 - The playlist dock's gold "applied" badge showed unset whenever the desktop file changed - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-22.** `PlaylistDock.jsx: isApplied` compared `appliedWallpaper.playlist.currentPlaylist` (a runtime-only name tag written on ONE record) with the dock's playlist name, while the engine resolves the playlist by membership. Every engine rotation or manual member apply therefore flipped the dock to "Apply playlist" - the UI claimed the playlist had been exited while it was still rotating. `isApplied` now asks `playlistEngine.activePlaylist()` (the engine's own resolution), so badge and rotation cannot disagree. Live: after manual member applies and 60 s rotations the button stayed "Playlist applied".
- [x] **P1 - The Timer set in the dialog never fired: the dialog edited the 'None' sentinel while the engine ran the playlist that holds the file - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-22 (commit `79542aa`).** `PlaylistEngineDialog` resolved the active playlist by NAME only, and `'None'` is both the default value on every wallpaper and a real playlist (`pl-0`). After a restart (or after the engine itself rotated) the wallpaper's `currentPlaylist` is `'None'`, so the dialog wrote `mode: 'Timer'` to `pl-0` while the engine evaluated the membership-resolved playlist (`pl-1` Favorites, mode "After Current Ends") - the two settings objects disagreed. Live evidence in the renderer's localStorage: `pl-0` Timer / `pl-1` After Current Ends. The dialog now asks the engine (`engine.activePlaylist()`, new API) and never falls back to the `'None'` sentinel; "Playlist Size" counts the playlist's members (the engine's pool) instead of runtime name tags. Live: dialog header shows Favorites, Timer chosen in the UI persists under `pl-1`.
- [x] **P1 - Cold start could leave the engine with no active playlist at all, so no mode (timer, scene end, schedules) ran - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-22 (commit `79542aa`).** `playlistEngine.start()` asked main for the desktop's file once (`getLastApplied`), before main had re-applied `last-wallpaper.json`; the answer was `null` and was never retried. With no path, `activePlaylistId()` returned `null`, `tick()` had no settings to evaluate, and the store stayed lazy (1 wallpaper) because the playlist pool is what materialises the files - a deadlock that only a renderer reload escaped. `tick()` now retries the lookup every 2 s until the path appears (the same race `store.js: fetchLastAppliedPath` already handles), which resolves the playlist and fills the pool. Live: cold start resolves `pl-1`, 11 wallpapers, Timer active.
- [x] **P0 - Closing REX to the tray killed the playlist: the renderer that runs the scheduler is destroyed on close, and Chromium freezes its 500 ms tick while the window exists but is hidden - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-22. This is the user's "it exits the playlist and only plays that file".** Two independent faults with one effect: **no engine while parked**. (1) `main.cjs` deliberately destroys the BrowserWindow on close to release the renderer + its GPU allocations ("REX lives in the tray, and the native runtime is a separate process"), but the playlist scheduler lives **inside that renderer** (`engine.js: setInterval(tick, 500)`) - so after closing the window there was no timer, no ENDED consumer, nothing. (2) While the window was merely minimized/hidden but alive, Chromium throttled/froze the renderer: live `document.visibilityState:"hidden"` with the engine's last tick **58,125 ms** old (nominal 500 ms); the Timer went silent from `22:41:10` to `22:48:31` and then fired at an exact 60 s cadence only while a dialog interaction kept the page awake. The dock badge bug (P3 above) made the same moment read as "the playlist was exited". **Fix:** `webPreferences.backgroundThrottling:false` on the main window (the same switch the thumbnail worker already uses), plus a `playlist:rotation-active` report from the engine (sent only on change) so the close handler **hides** the window and keeps the renderer alive while a rotation is live, and still destroys it (the old zero-footprint behaviour) when there is nothing scheduled; tray/`second-instance` reopen reuses the hidden window, `closed` resets the flag. **Live (final build, no instrumentation):** minimized - Gorgeous-Eyes (10.2 s) native ENDED `23:03:01.52` -> igris loaded `23:03:01.97` (**~250 ms**) while hidden; tray-close at `23:06:16` - blue-haired ended `23:06:30` -> Gorgeous-Eyes, `23:06:40` -> igris, renderer alive with `ageMs` 200-250 ms throughout; Timer in the tray - close `23:07:39`, rotations `23:07:26 -> 23:08:26 -> 23:09:26` (exactly 60 s), and on the final build `23:12:27 -> 23:13:27`; dock badge "Playlist applied" across every change; hidden Electron CPU **0.47 s over 10 s (~0.05 core)**. `pnpm lint` / `pnpm build` / `pnpm typecheck` exit 0 (pre-existing warnings only). Files: `app/electron/main.cjs`, `app/electron/preload.cjs`, `app/src/wallpapers/playlist/engine.js`, `app/src/wallpapers/PlaylistDock.jsx`.
- [x] **P1 - A login start (`--background`) still has no renderer, so a configured playlist does not rotate until the window is opened - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-23.** The scheduler moved to the main process (`app/electron/playlist-scheduler.cjs`): it restores the persisted rotation plan at login, re-arms the Timer and consumes native ENDED with no window. Live: two ENDED rotations with the window destroyed (`igris` `00:03:40`, `KALYANI` `00:04:03`, both `reason=After Current Ends`, main + GPU + utility only). Historical note: `main.cjs:1197` only calls `showMainWindow()` for a normal launch; `--background` (the login item) deliberately creates no window, which was the old zero-renderer parked state. The tray kill fix does not cover it: with no BrowserWindow there is no `playlistEngine` at all. Fix options: create a hidden window at login when a playlist is configured (and destroy it if none reports active after a settle delay), or move the scheduler to main/native (next line).
- [x] **P2 - The playlist scheduler lives in the UI renderer, which is why a running playlist now keeps a hidden window (~171 MB working set) alive - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-23.** The scheduler now runs in main (`playlist-scheduler.cjs`, 13/13 unit tests), the renderer only mirrors the rotation plan (`syncPlaylistPlan`), and the window is always destroyed on close (tray keep-alive and `playlistRotationActive` IPC removed; `backgroundThrottling:false` stays because the supervisor probe fix depends on it). Live: ENDED rotation `igris -> KALYANI` with no renderer process; reopening adopted the rotated path (`On desktop`, no second LOAD). Historical note: The correct long-term shape is to schedule Timer/ENDED in the main process (or the native runtime, which already emits `ENDED`) and have it apply the next member through the native runtime, so the UI can return to the zero-footprint parked state after close. That needs the mode logic + playlist membership + settings mirrored out of the renderer's localStorage; today the keep-alive is the low-risk fix that makes playlists actually run in the tray.
- [ ] **P3 - The timer's countdown display freezes between engine bumps - OPEN (cosmetic).** The dialog's "Countdown" (`timerRemainingMs`) and the advanced "Time Remaining" (`nextChangeTimestamp`) read the hook's `now`, but `tick()` only bumps subscribers when something changed (by design, to stop 2x/s localStorage churn), so the display jumps at each change instead of ticking. Fix: let the dialog drive its own `now` (local 500 ms interval) or expose a non-persisting clock subscription.
- [x] **P1 - The renderer "stalls" for 16-19 s and the supervisor reloads it in a loop (`renderer stale 16s - reloading`, every ~75 s) - FIXED 2026-10-23. The old diagnosis (poster decoding) was wrong.** Restore is not the blocker: replaying the real built gallery against the user's library (35 entries, 34 persisted posters) produced **33 tiles, 32 posters, 0 long tasks and a 2.04 s max heartbeat gap**. The heartbeat is a **page timer** (`preload.cjs:109-115`), and the watchdog only skips `!isVisible() || isMinimized()` (`supervisor/supervisor.cjs:198-200`). A window fully **occluded** by another app still reports `isVisible() === true`, but the page flips to `visibilityState:"hidden"`; Chromium then throttles its timers (probe: 2 s cadence -> 3 s immediately, and after ~5 min hidden intensive throttling drops it to ~1/min). That yields 60 s+ heartbeat gaps, a 16-19 s "stale" reading and a reload every ~75 s; the first stall 6m43s after launch matches the 5-minute intensive-throttling delay. The `fallback-thumbnail ... media-error` lines arrive ~2.2 s **after** each reload - a consequence of restore re-running, not the cause. **Fix A (watchdog):** keep `backgroundThrottling:false` (already in the in-flight scheduler WIP; the logged build predates it) and add a main-initiated IPC probe (`renderer:probe` / `renderer:probe-reply` in `main.cjs` + `preload.cjs`). When the heartbeat is stale, `Supervisor._rendererTick` asks the renderer directly (`probeRenderer`, 2.5 s timeout) and re-baselines if it answers; only a failed probe reloads. The 15 s threshold and 2 s heartbeat are unchanged, and a genuinely blocked main thread cannot answer, so real hangs still reload. **Fix B (restore hardening):** `WallpaperGallery.jsx` gives `probePoster` a 1.5 s timeout (a slow/offline library path used to wedge the restore and every later poster forever), yields a macrotask between restore entries and before the poster loop, and keeps one poster decode at a time (bounded concurrency). Persisted posters are still preferred and the GPU-worker fallback path is unchanged. **Evidence:** supervisor logic probe - stale+alive -> 0 reloads + re-baseline, stale+dead -> 1 reload, fresh -> no probe, no probe wiring -> 1 reload (old behaviour); rebuilt `dist` restore - 33 tiles / 32 posters / 31 probe images loaded / 0 errors / 0 long tasks / max gap 2.04 s. `pnpm lint` exit 0 (pre-existing warnings only), `pnpm typecheck` exit 0, `pnpm build` exit 0. Files: `app/electron/main.cjs`, `app/electron/preload.cjs`, `app/electron/supervisor/supervisor.cjs`, `app/src/wallpapers/WallpaperGallery.jsx`.

### Playlist scheduler in main + decode path (2026-10-23/24)

- [x] **P0 - Playlist Timer / scene-end rotation now runs in the main process - IMPLEMENTED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-23/24.** `app/electron/playlist-scheduler.cjs` owns `Timer` + `After Current Ends` / `Video Ends` / `Audio Ends`; the renderer mirrors a compact plan (`syncPlaylistPlan`: playlist id/name, mode, ordered queue with per-member apply payload, current path, loop/paused, timer snapshot, `updatedAt`) and main persists it at `appState.playlistEngine.rotation`. Boot restores and re-arms without a window; `reconcile()` drops a saved plan whose desktop file is outside it; stale plans (`updatedAt < lastApplyAt`) are ignored; an unchanged timer identity keeps `nextDueAt`. The old tray keep-alive (`playlistRotationActive`) is deleted - close always destroys the renderer. **Live:** window destroyed `00:01:31`; native ENDED `00:03:40` -> `igris-silent-knight` applied, `00:04:03` -> `KALYANI` applied, both `reason=After Current Ends`, no renderer process (main + GPU + utility only); reopen adopted `KALYANI` (`On desktop`, no second LOAD). 13/13 scheduler tests green.
- [x] **P0 - Native ENDED fired on container duration, so a video with an audio tail never ended - FIXED 2026-10-23.** The latch now keys on the decoder's video-stream EOF (`FfmpegVideoDecoder::ReachedEndOfStream`) and is carried by `SceneEndLatch` through `VideoPlaybackController`; one-shot, re-armed by Open/Seek/Stop. Tests: `SceneEndTest` 5/5, `SceneEndLatchTest` 4/4, `EofLoopGuardTest` 3/3; full `ctest` re-run 2026-10-24: `99% tests passed, 4 tests failed out of 507` — the 4 are the known `_NOT_BUILT` placeholders, 14 skipped/disabled, **0 real failures**.
- [x] **P1 - Intel D3D11VA decode silently fell back to software, with no reason in the log - DIAGNOSED + FIXED (pending Intel re-test) 2026-10-23/24.** The NVIDIA-vs-Intel comparison was confounded: if the Intel run used the Oppenheimer 4:4:4 file, software decode is expected on every GPU (REX's chroma gate rejects 4:4:4 before the driver, and FFmpeg's D3D11VA HEVC accel registers only Main/Main10). The remaining suspect was `MiscFlags = SHARED | SHARED_NTHANDLE` on FFmpeg's decoder device context - it applies to decoder textures that carry `BIND_DECODER` and cannot be shared, and Intel is the plausible driver that rejects `CreateTexture2D` and demotes inside `get_format` while REX still logs "hardware decode". Removed (REX copies into its own pool, so the source textures never needed sharing). Added the three missing fallback reasons (`av_hwdevice_ctx_init` / `avcodec_open2` with `av_strerror`, `get_format` pivot naming codec/profile/first format) and `hardwareAccelerated` -> `hw=0|1` in the 5 s telemetry line. **AMD live:** H.264, AV1 and HEVC Main10 10-bit (The Odyssey 4K) all `hw=1`, `content=1.00x`, `ahead=1.01x`, `decP50=0.23-0.63 ms`. **Still needed:** one 4:2:0 10-bit control file on the Intel box + `%TEMP%\REX\runtime.log` captured immediately after load (the log is deleted at every runtime start).
- [ ] **P2 - HEVC 4:2:2 / 4:4:4 cannot hardware-decode on any GPU in REX - OPEN (by design, documented).** `D3d11vaCanDecodeChroma` rejects any `log2_chroma_w/h != 1` and the HEVC GUID table carries only Main/Main10, so 4:4:4 falls to software before the driver. FFmpeg master's D3D11VA HEVC accel also registers only Main/Main10 (no RExt GUIDs), so this is a stack limitation, not an Intel bug. Options: add the RExt GUIDs + a software chroma path, or surface "4:4:4 -> software" in the UI.
- [x] **P2 - One file produced three store records, so the UI lost "On desktop" after a background rotation - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-24.** Boot adoption, playlist materialisation and a tile click resolved the same path concurrently; each created its own record (`file-1`, `file-2`, `file-13`), `appliedId` landed on one and the tile selected another. `loadWallpaperFile` now reuses an existing record with the same normalized path and `ensureWallpaperForPath` dedupes in-flight creations by path. Live: one KALYANI record, `appliedId` == selected tile, inspector shows `On desktop`; build/lint green.
- [x] **P2 - A scene-end mode parked forever when the current member is an image - FIXED 2026-10-25 (option b: images get a dwell time).** Live evidence (2026-10-24): after the rotation reached `C:\Users\Moinu\Downloads\REX_rowpitch_test_1366x768.png`, the scheduler reported `currentPath` = that image, `nextDueAt: null`, `queueLength: 11` — 10 members behind it never played. Root cause: `After Current Ends` / `Video Ends` / `Audio Ends` advance only on the native `ENDED` event, and an image scene emits none, so `_advance` was never called (the old renderer's `evaluateMode` had the same shape — not a regression). **Fix:** `playlist-scheduler.cjs` now arms a dwell timer (`DEFAULT_IMAGE_DWELL_MS` = 30 s, overridable per plan via `plan.imageDwellMs` or per scheduler via the constructor) whenever a scene-end mode is sitting on a still — both when a plan arrives (`_applyDwellPlan`) and when the rotation lands on one (`_armDwell` inside `_advance`). A still is detected from `apply.kind === 'image'` (the same field `main.cjs` dispatches on), with an extension fallback for plans that predate `kind`. The dwell is keyed by playlist+mode+path, so the renderer's frequent plan rebroadcasts do not restart the clock; the Timer mode is untouched; and a successful advance to a video cancels it. Tests: **17/17** scheduler tests (4 new: dwell fires, rebroadcast does not restart it, landing on an image arms it, configurable + never applied to Timer); full app suite **76/76**, `pnpm lint` 0 errors, `pnpm typecheck` 0. **Live on the shipped build `Paiint-0.1.0-2026-10-25-d.exe`:** boot restored the rotation parked on `REX_rowpitch_test_1366x768.png` and logged `image dwell due in 30000ms`; a renderer plan rebroadcast 175 ms later logged `due in 29825ms` (the clock was preserved, not restarted); at `17:11:13.757` it applied `D:\live wallpaper\marvel-spiderman-miles-morales.1920x1080.mp4` with `reason=After Current Ends`.


- [x] **P1 - The inspector was inverted: ~30 inert controls, none of the six live ones - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-21 (P3-7).** Appearance rendered 17 controls with **zero** consumers, Renderer offered codec / hardware-decode / frame-skip / subtitle / Ken Burns fields that cannot work per file (a codec is a property of the file, HW decode is process-wide, the runtime has no subtitle track) - and the properties the runtime *can* change live (fill mode, loop, mute, volume, speed, output) had no surface at all. The panel now shows poster + identity -> Apply / On desktop + Pause-Resume -> Playback -> Display -> File -> Playlist, with the dead sections deleted. Full detail, evidence and the follow-ups: **P3-7** below and [`research-inspector.md`](research-inspector.md).
- [x] **P2 - Choosing "Tile" raised a user-facing error toast (the engine refuses that mode) - FIXED 2026-10-21.** The new fit-mode control offered Tile for videos; the engine answers `SET_FILL_MODE failed: Tile fill mode is not implemented` (`rex-core/src/wallpaper/engine/Engine.cpp` `SetFillMode`), which surfaced as a "Wallpaper error" toast. Tile is an OS-only mode (Windows tiles images natively), so it is now offered for images only (`fitModesFor(type)` in `app/src/wallpapers/wallpaperSettings.js`, covered by a test).
- [x] **P2 - Silent files were offered mute / volume / output - FIXED 2026-10-21.** The panel now reads the file header (`app/electron/mediaInfo.cjs`: MP4 `hdlr` handler, Matroska `TrackType`) and shows "This file has no audio track." with the audio controls hidden. A decoder-based probe was rejected on purpose: Chromium cannot decode the AC-3 / DTS-HD MA audio in this library, so it would have called the movies silent. Probed all 33 library entries: 9 with audio (incl. every DTS/AC-3 movie), 15 silent, 9 unknown (images + one missing file).
- [x] **P2 - The inspector could not scroll (the poster consumed the whole column) - FIXED 2026-10-21.** The Radix `ScrollArea` had **0 px** of height: the 450 px poster plus the head and the search row filled the 542 px column, so the properties below were clipped with no way to reach them. The poster now shrinks (`flex: 0 1 auto`, `min-height: 140px`, `height: min(450px, 50vh)`) and the scroll area keeps a 180 px floor. Scrollbars are now hidden app-wide (`scrollbar-width: none` + `::-webkit-scrollbar { width: 0 }` + the Radix scrollbar chrome) while every container still scrolls (gallery grid and inspector viewport verified scrolling after the change).
- [ ] **P3 - The playlist engine keeps rotating the desktop while the user browses - OPEN (observed 2026-10-21).** During the inspector work the desktop wallpaper changed under the user: `wallpaper:video` applies for `12449638` (10:21:46), `2020 LG` (10:21:47), `259235` (10:22:07) and `frozen-princess` (10:22:17) while only tiles were being *selected* (a single click never applies - verified: selecting a tile produced no load). The rotation is the playlist engine (`app/src/wallpapers/playlist/engine.js` `fireChange` / `pickNext`), and it overrides the selection the user just made. Root cause not yet established; playlists are a deferred area, but this fights the wallpaper path and belongs on the list.

### Global settings page (2026-10-21)

Research: [`research-settings.md`](research-settings.md) · plan: [`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-global-settings.md`](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-21-global-settings.md). **The page itself did not exist** - the nav item (`SidebarLayout.jsx:28-34`), the tray item (`tray-manager.cjs:99-102`) and the `PAGES` table (`App.jsx:21-31`, itself never read) all lead to a heading with no body, because only the Wallpapers tab renders content (`App.jsx:107`). It now renders, with every control backed by a key the app actually reads.

- [x] **P1 - The engine can change brightness / contrast / saturation live, and the app never sent them - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-21.** The runtime accepts `SET_BRIGHTNESS` / `SET_CONTRAST` / `SET_SATURATION` (`wallpaper_cli_main.cpp:1153-1161`), the video adapter advertises all three (`RendererAdapters.cpp:82-84`), and the engine applies them per frame (`Engine.cpp:1083-1130` -> `D3D12Renderer.cpp:2589-2593` packs them into the b0 root constants every frame) - a complete colour pipeline the app never used. Now forwarded through `wallpaper:update` / `wallpaper:video`, persisted per file (percent 0..200 where 100 = unchanged; the wire value is the engine's 0..2 factor, exactly how `volume` already works) and shown as a **Picture** group in the Inspector, video-only (the static adapter has no colour pipeline). The values are not part of `LOAD`, so the apply path and the boot replay restore them explicitly - a wallpaper switch would otherwise inherit the previous file's brightness. Live: `SET_BRIGHTNESS` applied (not skipped) by the engine, the slider persisted to the library entry, the apply carried `brightness 1.5`, and the boot replay logged `re-applied SET_BRIGHTNESS=1.5`.
- [x] **P1 - The global Settings page rendered nothing - FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-21.** It now carries **Startup** (launch at login - the OS login item, so the page and the tray can never disagree; start minimised, the login item's `--background` flag; restore the last wallpaper at launch), **Window** (remember size and position, validated against the live display list so a window never opens off-screen after a monitor is unplugged; "On close: keep in tray / quit REX", the default unchanged), **Engine** (frame-rate cap and hardware decoding - both INIT knobs the engine already honours, labelled as taking effect when the engine starts - plus the engine's live telemetry and the property list it can apply), **New wallpapers** (defaults for fit mode, muted, volume and brightness), **Files** (the real library / poster-cache / log / crash-report paths, each openable, plus clearing the cached posters) and **About** (the real build version). The `PAGES` table is finally read: the page head shows the subtitle and the champagne rule. The status panel never starts the engine - it says "not running" instead of spawning one to answer a question. **Follow-up fix (same day):** the "Launch at login" switch wrote the registry entry but read the state back as off, so it snapped back on every click - on Windows, `getLoginItemSettings().openAtLogin` is derived from an identity-keyed registry entry and reads FALSE in a development build even though the entry was written (the dev exe is electron.exe). The truth is the platform's own `launchItems`; the rule now lives in `startup.js` as a pure tested function, and the tray checkbox (which had the same blind spot) uses it too and is rebuilt when the page toggles it.
- [x] **P2 - The palette guard was failing on a raw colour literal - FIXED 2026-10-21.** `PlaylistDock.css` carried `rgb(94 94 94 / 0.92)` for the dock's steel ground. The colour is now a palette token (`--surface-dock`, backed by a new `--obsidian-350` step), so `check:palette` is green again. It was failing before this round's work - the guard scans every CSS file in `app/src`.
- [ ] **P1 - The engine's "pause on fullscreen app" cannot be turned off - OPEN (engine work).** The engine implements the detector and the switch (`Engine.cpp:1824`, `WallpaperConfig.pauseOnFullscreen`), and `INIT` accepts the parameter (`wallpaper_cli_main.cpp:909`) - but there is no live setter, so the behaviour is permanently ON. Both reference products make it a headline setting. Needs a `SET_PAUSE_ON_FULLSCREEN` command (the flag is read from the render thread, so it must be an atomic). Belongs to **P3-1** (the unified playback-policy layer), not to a second policy owner.
- [ ] **P2 - The wallpaper pauses itself for ~25 s on cold start: the fullscreen auto-pause detects Paiint's own `REXRenderWindow` - OPEN (engine work).** `Engine::CheckFullScreenPause()` (`Engine.cpp:1859`) excludes `Progman`/`WorkerW`/`SHELLDLL_DefView`/`Shell_TrayWnd` but not `REXRenderWindow` (`WorkerWManager.cpp:1073`), the runtime's own render window. During startup that window is briefly top-level/foreground before it is reparented into WorkerW, so `GetForegroundWindow()` returns it, the 3-check debounce passes, and the engine pauses itself. Evidence: diagnostics session `2026-10-31T12-38-27-879Z` (engine state 3→4 at 18:08:33 local, 4→3 at 18:08:57); `%TEMP%\Paiint\runtime.1.log` shows `Full-screen window: class=REXRenderWindow rect=3840x2160 (monitor 3840x2160)` at 18:08:30.916/32.116/33.367 → `Full-screen app detected - requesting wallpaper pause` → `Auto-pause applied (fullscreen=true, suspend=false)` → `Full-screen app closed - requesting wallpaper resume` at 18:08:57.960. Fix: ignore `REXRenderWindow` (and any `WS_CHILD`/own-process window) in the detector. The same session's 18:19:35 pause is correct (`console display off - suspending wallpaper`), not this bug.
- [ ] **P3 - The frame cap and hardware-decode switch only apply at engine start - OPEN (honest limit, not a bug).** Both are `INIT` parameters, so a change takes effect the next time the wallpaper engine starts; the Settings page says so in the control's description. A live cap needs the atomic the performance-modes plan already calls for (**P3-4**).
- [ ] **P2 - Seven "Multi Monitor" toggles in the playlist dialog do nothing - OPEN (app-only work).** `PlaylistEngineDialog.jsx:409-413` renders every `multiMonitor.*` switch, but the engine **never reads `settings.multiMonitor`** - the only two references in the app are the default (`engine.js:230`) and the toggle itself. Either wire them to the engine's per-monitor path or remove them; a switch that changes nothing is exactly the failure the inspector cleanup just removed.
- [ ] **P2 - Playlist filters and rules read fields real files do not have - OPEN (app-only work).** `engine.js:385-413` filters on `w.general.rating` (the rating lives at `info.rating`, so this is undefined and never matches), `w.media?.duration` (**`w.media` does not exist in the model at all**), `w.renderer?.hdr` (no renderer default carries `hdr`, so "Only HDR" excludes everything), `w.info?.resolution` (the registry placeholder `"1920 x 1080"`, so "Only 4K" never matches a real 4K file), `w.info?.nsfw` (never set) and `w.info?.fileSize` (set only for `File`-object imports, `store.js:422`, not for path-restored entries). Expose them only after the data source is real.
- [ ] **P3 - Eight dead Inspector sections cover ~75 store keys that nothing writes - OPEN (cleanup).** `app/src/wallpapers/inspector/sections/` still holds `GeneralSection` (9 keys), `PerformanceSection` (9), `MonitorSection` (7), `InteractionSection` (8), `AudioSection` (8), `ScheduleSection` (7), `AdvancedSection` (8) and `InfoSection` (21) - none imported by the Inspector (`Inspector.jsx:12-16`). The registry declares ~134 keys across 12 groups; exactly 6 were reachable before this round and 9 now. **Delete them, do not surface them** - a Settings page built from the model would re-create the inert-control disease in a new place. Two exceptions worth keeping in mind: `performance.frameRateLimit` / `performance.pauseFullscreen` map onto real engine knobs, and `audio.audioReactive` onto `Engine::EnableAudioReactive` (`Engine.cpp:1310`) which no command can reach.
- [ ] **P3 - `automation.*` is implemented on simulated signals - OPEN (do not expose).** The ten automation keys are evaluated (`engine.js:766-815`) but their inputs are developer-only: weather, lock, game, docked, presenting, screen-sharing, remote-desktop and lid-closed are settable **only** from the dialog's Developer Options buttons (`PlaylistEngineDialog.jsx:502-511`), and CPU/GPU are random numbers (`engine.js:837-838`). Exposing automation as a user setting would ship a control that cannot work outside the dialog.
- [ ] **P3 - `theme.*` contradicts the palette decision - OPEN (remove the key).** `app-state.json` carries `theme: { themeId: 'midnight-glass', colorTokens: {} }` (`app-state-manager.cjs:35`) and nothing reads it, but the colour spec deliberately defines **one** palette ("Obsidian & Champagne", `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-app-color-palette-design.md`). A theme picker would undo that decision - drop the key instead of building a picker.

### Desktop app / window lifecycle (2026-10-19)

- [x] **P0 — Closed UI kept the full renderer + GPU process alive (close only hid the window) — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-19.** `close` hid the BrowserWindow, so the renderer, compositor and GPU allocations stayed for the app's lifetime. Isolated Electron 43: hide freed nothing; `win.destroy()` dropped the renderer (~50 MB private) but the GPU process held **148 MB dedicated VRAM for 90 s+**; a never-created window used **48 MB private / 5 MB VRAM**; `app.disableHardwareAcceleration()` gave **0 MB dedicated VRAM**. Fix: (a) hardware acceleration off for the Electron UI only (the native runtime keeps its own D3D12 device); (b) close now destroys the window on the next tick (tray + runtime stay alive) and `closed` clears `mainWindow`; (c) every reopen path — tray click, tray Settings, second-instance, `activate` — funnels through one race-safe `showMainWindow()`; `--background` no longer creates a hidden window; (d) tray Settings is queued until a new preload `renderer:navigate-ready` handshake confirms the React `onNavigate` listener is mounted (not just `did-finish-load`); (e) `appState.flush()` on close because `destroy()` skips `beforeunload`. Also fixed a boot bug found during verification: an image re-apply always reported `PLAY → No scene loaded` as failure — images are OS-wallpaper applies and now skip `PLAY`. **Live:** open **~104–112 MB private / 0 VRAM** (working set varies with gallery state); closed **49.8 MB private / 206 MB WS / 0 VRAM**, renderer process gone; second-instance reopen **~102–113 MB private / 0 VRAM**, exactly one main; runtime PID **unchanged** across close/reopen; tray Settings on a destroyed window recreated it and landed on the Settings tab (`window: navigate Settings`); `--background` **33.7 MB private / 0 VRAM**, no renderer. Lint/typecheck green. (`app/electron/main.cjs`, `tray-manager.cjs`, `preload.cjs`)

### Wallpaper apply / runtime memory (2026-10-19)

- [ ] **P3 — a wallpaper-playing stack still holds ~530 MB of Electron shell while minimised to tray (measured 2026-10-28).** With `startup.startMinimized: true`, launching `Paiint.exe` left the window alive (hidden), so four Electron processes held **~527–532 MB working set** next to the 101 MB runtime — versus Lively's **181 MB** for UI + watchdog while its `mpv` renderer ran. The close-to-tray path already destroys the window (P0 2026-10-19, 206 MB WS closed), so the gap is specific to **minimised-at-startup**, which is the common logon case (`--startup-task` → `startMinimized`). Not root-caused: the 4 processes were not sampled individually for long enough to say how much is the gallery/React tree versus Chromium baselines. Candidate fix: with a wallpaper applied and `startMinimized` set, destroy the window after boot and reopen on tray/second-instance (the same `showMainWindow()` funnel). Measure the 4 PIDs separately before choosing. (`app/electron/main.cjs`, `app/src/wallpapers/WallpaperGallery.jsx`)

- [x] **P1 — An image-only desktop built a full 4K D3D12 engine for a wallpaper the OS draws — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-19.** The native image LOAD branch already documents *"Images go through the OS wallpaper API — no engine needed"*, but Electron called `runtime.init()` before every image apply in three paths: interactive `wallpaper:set`, startup `applyStoredWallpaper`, and crash recovery `reapplyLastWallpaper`.
