INIT built the 3840×2160 D3D12 renderer/swapchain; the startup ramp read as "67 MB/s" in Task Manager but was a one-time allocation (10→84 MB in <1 s, then flat — 8 repeated image applies stayed at 116.2 MB). Fix: image paths send `LOAD` only — it spawns the runtime on demand (`wallpaperRuntime.cjs` `send` → `_spawn`) and the native image branch is explicitly engine-free; every command the app can send without INIT is null-guarded (PING `:854`, image LOAD `:980`, PLAY/PAUSE/STOP/UNLOAD `:1084`, SET_* `:1119`, GET_STATUS `:1320`, GET_CAPABILITIES `:1423`, SHUTDOWN `:877`). Recovery also no longer sends the always-failing image `PLAY`. No native change/rebuild, no renderer change. **Live evidence:** image boot **84.2 MB private / ~103.5 MB dedicated VRAM / 31 threads → 10.2 MB / 0 MB / 5 threads**, runtime log shows `LOAD (id=1)` only — no `INIT`, no D3D12, no `PLAY`; supervisor crash recovery with the image last-applied re-applied in **272 ms** at **10.3 MB / 0 VRAM** (`reapplied last wallpaper image`, no INIT/PLAY); image→video cold start **678 ms** end-to-end (INIT 460 ms) → **313.8 MB / 742 MB** playing The Odyssey 4K HEVC 10-bit; normal video boot **315.4 MB / 742.8 MB**, frames 60 fps + audio rendering. `node --check` OK, `pnpm lint` 0 errors (pre-existing warnings only), `pnpm typecheck` clean. (`app/electron/main.cjs`)
- [x] **P0 — every 4K video session permanently pinned another pool of dedicated VRAM (+180–205 MiB per apply); the idle engine stayed resident after video→image — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-19.** The question deferred above is answered: the footprint **grows per cycle**, it is not a plateau. Two discriminating probes (`%TEMP%\opencode\rex-shared-pool-probe.cpp`, `rex-import-reuse-probe.cpp`) on the RX 6700 XT proved the AMD driver charges a **per-import pin**: 8 shared P010 3840×2048 textures (188,743,680 B) created once, then 3 × (D3D12 `OpenSharedHandle` all 8 + `CopyTextureRegion`/`Execute`/wait + release) → **183.8 → 367 → 547 → 727 MiB** (+180 per cycle). `IDXGIDevice3::Trim` freed nothing (726.7), releasing the D3D11 pool textures freed only the original allocation (→546), and destroying the D3D12 device (546) then the D3D11 device (543.2) freed nothing; **the charge is released only at process exit**. Importing the same handles **without GPU use** *is* Trim-freed — the first GPU use makes it permanent. Proven ineffective and reverted: `ClearState()+Flush()` in decoder `Close()`, the null-SRV overwrite in `D3D12Renderer::ClearVideoFrame()`, and `VideoManager::TrimDeviceMemory()` + the Engine call. Fix (A+B+C): **(A)** the decoder keeps its pool generation across `Close()` (`ReleasePool()` runs only in the destructor; the `EnsurePool` fast path gained a device-identity guard) and `VideoPlaybackController` reuses the closed decoder, so a same-surface reopen returns the **same NT handles**; **(B)** `D3D12Renderer::ClearVideoFrame()` keeps its imported slot cache instead of releasing/re-importing (slots die in `Shutdown`; LRU eviction handles a new generation) — no new `OpenSharedHandle`, no new pin; **(C)** an image apply deliberately stops the runtime (`wallpaperRuntime.stop({ intentional:true })` tags the proc; the supervisor's `_handleRuntimeExit` skips the crash report + replay on `info.intentional`, and the heartbeat already bails on `!manager.alive`), because process exit is the only reclaim boundary. **Idle-timer verdict: not worth building** — stop-on-image reclaims immediately and completely; a timer would only keep the pins alive longer. **Live:** video→image exits 1.5 s after apply (log `runtime:reclaim image applied` → `runtime:exit (intentional idle stop — no recovery)`, no recovery start, 0 MB VRAM, image stays via the OS); image→video cold respawn **865 ms** → 526.5 MB dedicated (Oppenheimer SW BGRA 3840×1752); image→video on the tiny image-only runtime INITs in place (**887 ms**, same PID, 526.5 MB); ProjectHailMary (HW P010 3840×1920) 173 ms / 918.9 MB, then **6 alternating applies** of the same surface through a second path → **VRAM flat at 918.9 MB, only 2 pool rebuilds in the whole log**, applies 44–70 ms; a fill-mode change with the engine stopped spawns the tiny image-only runtime (**10.2 MB private / 0 MB VRAM**) and moves the OS style 10→6 with `mode:"fit"` persisted. **Follow-up (open, P3 below):** the *alternating pool format/size* case still builds a new generation per switch — bounded, not leaked (old imports retire through the 8-slot LRU), but each generation costs ~2× its pool size for the process lifetime; a 2-generation pool cache + ≥16 renderer slots would remove it. `cmake --build rex-core/.build --config Release` clean; **804/804 ctest**; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 22:43. (`FfmpegVideoDecoder.hpp/.cpp`, `VideoPlaybackController.cpp`, `D3D12Renderer.cpp`, `wallpaperRuntime.cjs`, `supervisor/supervisor.cjs`, `main.cjs`)
- [x] **P3 — alternating pool formats re-pinned a generation per switch (follow-up to the VRAM fix) — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-29.** Implemented exactly as scoped: `PoolGenerationKey` = `(width,height,format)`, `kMaxLivePoolGenerations = 3`, `ReuseCachedPool` / `RetireActivePool` / `TrimPoolCache` (NT handles closed on eviction and in `ReleasePool`), `VideoFrame::poolGeneration`, renderer slots keyed by `(sharedHandle, poolGeneration)` (NT handle values are recycled, so the handle alone can alias), `kMaxVideoSlots = 24`, SRV heap 128 with `kToneStatsUavBase = 64` + `static_assert`s. Measured: 10 loads / 5 SDR↔HDR cycles hold **774 MB (SDR gen 1) ↔ 1,167 MB (HDR gen 2), perfectly flat** (peak 1,167.5, end 825.9), `rebuilt=2 reused=9 slotEvict=0 removed=0 imports=16`; pre-fix baseline was peak **2,102.8 MB**, end 1,760–1,808 MB, **+130 MB per load**. Twelve further stress runs (96 loads / 48 switches) all `rebuilt=2 reused=15 removed=0`, and the final scaffold-free build re-ran the 5-pair churn at **796–1,161 MB**. The +264 MB/switch and the "restart the runtime on generation change" mitigation are obsolete. Unit test `DecoderPoolCacheTest.FindsMatchingGenerationAndLruVictim`; `DecoderPool*` 10/10 green. **Original scope (2026-10-28), kept for the record:** A 4:4:4 10-bit file (SW BGRA pool) and a P010 file have different `EnsurePool` keys, so switching between them builds a new generation; the renderer imports the new handles and retires the old ones via the 8-slot LRU, so the old generation's pin is *not* reclaimed until process exit. Cost per switch ≈ 2× the new pool (allocation + per-import pin): measured 526.5 MB (BGRA 3840×1752) → 918.9 MB (P010 3840×1920) = +392 MB. Bounded (not unbounded growth), but a user alternating such files pays it every switch. Fix direction: cache the last 2 pool generations in the decoder (keyed by w/h/format) and raise `kMaxVideoSlots` to ≥16 so the renderer can hold both generations' imports; then same-A→B→A switches are cache hits. (`FfmpegVideoDecoder.cpp`, `D3D12Renderer.hpp` `kMaxVideoSlots` / 64-descriptor SRV heap) **Live confirmation 2026-10-28:** one 90 s run that switched clips — VP9 8-bit SDR (NV12) → AV1 10-bit HDR10 (P010) — logged exactly **16** `video slot cache full - evicting slot N (stale pool generation)` lines (8 at 21:00:48 for the P010→NV12 direction, 8 at 21:01:12 for NV12→P010) and dedicated VRAM stepped **641 MB → 1,252 MB** (+611 MB) at the second switch, holding to the end of the run. Both generations are pinned for the process lifetime; the cost is per format switch, exactly as described here. (`paiint_hw_usage_2026-10-28_rendering.csv`) **Residual measured 2026-10-28:** a fresh process on the webm holds **449 MB**; after one switch into a P010 clip and back it holds **641 MB** (~192 MB left behind), and a fresh HDR process holds **989 MB** vs **1,252 MB** after a switch (~263 MB left behind). Cheap mitigation: restart the runtime when the pool generation changes instead of switching formats in-process. (`paiint_hw_2026-10-28_samefile.csv`, `paiint_hw_2026-10-28_hdr_clean.csv`)
- [x] **P2 - long-lived runtime "degrades" after repeated clip/format switches — 40 % 3D claim RETRACTED, real per-switch VRAM defect FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-29.** A-B-A on the same clip: clean engine **27.90 %** → same process after 12 alternating SDR↔HDR switches **28.72 %** → fresh process **27.73 %**, all inside a 1-point band, so the 40.31 % sample was session noise, not a long-session cost. Ruled out while testing: a second swapchain or duplicated device, scene/ECS accumulation (`scenesLoaded=23` / `scenesReleased=22`), copy-engine work (`gpu_other_pct=0`), shared-memory spill and COM holders. What *did* reproduce is memory, far larger than the original +299 MB: dedicated VRAM **963.7 → 3,099.8 MB across 12 switches** while `gpuDedicated` stayed at **1,017,614,336 B**; a controlled harness measured **+264 MB per format switch** with 10 same-format re-applies flat (510–565 MB) and 60 s idle releasing nothing. Root cause: a format change rebuilt the D3D11 pool → new NT handles → a new D3D12 import generation → another permanent AMD per-import pin (the same mechanism as the 2026-10-19 P0). **Fix:** the decoder pool-generation cache + renderer slot cache described in the P3 item above. **Found and fixed while verifying:** the peak-detection UAVs were hard-coded at descriptor 20 while 24 video slots span 1..48, so `CreateShaderResourceView` overwrote them and the dispatch removed the device (`Present FAILED 0x887a0005`, reason `0x887a0006`; 10 removals in one run, 0 once the heap contract `kToneStatsUavBase = 64` / 128 descriptors landed). **Verified on the final scaffold-free build:** 10 loads / 5 cycles at **796–1,161 MB**, `rebuilt=2 reused=9 evict=0 removed=0`, plus 96 stress loads clean. **Residuals:** one unreproduced stall and the `AssessMediaBudget` blind spot are tracked as separate items below. **Original finding, kept for the record (2026-10-28):** Found 2026-10-28 while assembling `test.md`. A session that had been up ~25 min and had applied/switched through many clips (including multiple SDR↔HDR format changes) measured the **LG OLED HDR10 clip** at **3D 40.31 % avg / 42.33 max, decode 83.39 %, VRAM 1,292.0 MB** (`paiint_hw_usage_2026-10-28_rendering_2.csv`, 21:26:41–21:27:39, 15 buckets). The identical clip in clean sessions costs **27.34 % (shell open, 992.8 MB)** and **27.86 % (engine only, 989.1 MB)** — i.e. **+12.5 to +13.0 pts 3D, +17.5 pts decode, +299 MB VRAM**. The tell: **engine telemetry is indistinguishable from the clean run** — `gpuDed=985 MB`, `decFps=60.0`, `rendFps=59.9`, `slots=8`, `dispHz=120`, zero dropped/repeated frames — so the extra GPU time is **outside the D3D12 resources `QueryVideoMemoryInfo` sees**: most likely driver-side overhead on a pinned allocation plus a stale pipeline/surface still being exercised, but the runtime log for that window has rotated, so the exact mechanism is **unproven**. Null hypothesis to rule out first: a second live swapchain or a duplicated device from a previous apply that never got destroyed (grep apply/teardown paths for early-return leaks). WHY it matters: every user who browses a video gallery for a few minutes is in this state, and it doubles the engine's GPU cost without changing a single counter the engine reports. Repro: start the runtime, apply and switch clips ~8–10 times alternating SDR/HDR, then hold one HDR clip 5 min and sample for 60 s. Second finding from the same file: the shell had **5 Electron PIDs (692.6 MB WS) instead of 4 (389.7 MB)** — that is the packaged-vs-dev difference, but those PIDs reported **0.07 % 3D and 0 MB VRAM**, so they do **not** explain GPU time attributed to the engine process. Details: [`test.md`](test.md) §6 (invalidated measurements) and §7 finding 6.
- [x] **P3 — every clean runtime shutdown writes a fake "crash" block to `%TEMP%\REX\crash.log` (discovered 2026-10-19 closing the VRAM work) — FIXED 2026-10-26 (see the P3 entry at the top of this backlog).** The native VEH dumper logs *first-chance* exceptions, and `WTSUnRegisterSessionNotification` → `WinStationUnRegisterSessionNotification` raises RPC status `0x0000071A` internally on teardown (raised by `RpcRaiseException`, swallowed by the OS). Fix: `CrashHandler` now ignores only `0x71A`, and only after `g_teardownStarted` is set by a deliberate SHUTDOWN or stdin-EOF exit; live verification left `crash.log` unchanged across a snapshot quit.
- [x] **P3 — an applied image's fill-mode control is a no-op on the desktop — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-19.** `updateDesktopSettings` sends `SET_FILL_MODE` for images (`app/src/wallpapers/desktop.js:87-91`), but images are OS-drawn and the engine has no image scene, so the on-screen wallpaper never changed (before the INIT fix the command silently did nothing; with no engine it was rejected by the native null guard at `wallpaper_cli_main.cpp:1119`). Fix: for a persisted image, `wallpaper:update` now re-issues `runtime.send('LOAD', { path, type:'image', mode })` — the native image branch applies the OS style without an engine — and the post-update `reclaimIdleRuntime` keeps the engine stopped. **Live (runtime stopped, 0 MB VRAM):** `updateWallpaper({mode:'fit'})` → `{"ok":true,"applied":["SET_FILL_MODE"]}`, registry `HKCU\Control Panel\Desktop\WallpaperStyle` **10→6**, `last-wallpaper.json` `mode:"fit"`, runtime respawned as the tiny image-only process (**10.2 MB private / 0 MB dedicated VRAM**); updating back to `fill` → 10. `node --check` OK. (`app/electron/main.cjs`)

- [ ] **P2 — one unreproduced render-thread stall after a pool-generation reuse (2026-10-29, seen while verifying the pool cache).** A single churn run hung: the log stopped during HDR playback (last line a `[D3D12] tone peak …`, no `Device removed`, no TDR) and the harness's next `LOAD` timed out after 60 s without the command thread even logging the command — i.e. the command thread was blocked behind the render mutex while the render thread sat in/around `Present`. (`%TEMP%\opencode\runtime_fixed.log`, `removed=0`.) **Not reproduced since:** the same build then completed 12 stress runs × 8 SDR↔HDR switches plus 4 smaller runs (~150 loads, 0 stalls, 0 removals), and a gated `Present` heartbeat (`begin`/`done` with the awaited fence value and `GetCompletedValue()`) never fired late. Context: a **WHEA fatal hardware error** and an unclean reboot (Event 41) are logged 10 minutes before the stall, so driver/hardware state is suspect. If it recurs, re-add the heartbeat around `m_swapChain.Present` and log `frame.poolGeneration`, `frame.fenceValue` and `waitFence->GetCompletedValue()`: a `begin` without `done` and `completed < wait` means that generation's copy was never signaled. (`D3D12Renderer.cpp` `EndFrameLocked`, `FfmpegVideoDecoder::SignalSharedFenceOrdered`)
- [ ] **P3 — `AssessMediaBudget` cannot see imported-texture VRAM, so it can never trip on a pool-import leak.** `Engine.cpp:641-655` budgets from `QueryVideoMemoryInfo`, but during the 2026-10-29 leak that value stayed at **1,017,614,336 B** while the process's WDDM `GPU Process Memory\Dedicated Usage` grew **963.7 → 3,099.8 MB** — the per-import pins are invisible to the D3D12 query. Options: (a) add the renderer slot table's imported bytes to the assessed total (a 4K P010 slot ≈ 24 MB, so `kMaxVideoSlots = 24` bounds it at ~576 MB) and gate on that, or (b) keep the query advisory and document that imports are bounded by `kMaxLivePoolGenerations × kPoolSize`. Until one lands, do not claim the resource envelope covers imports. (`Engine.cpp`, `D3D12Renderer.hpp` `VideoSlotResource`)

### Color / HDR tone mapping (2026-10-19)

- [x] **P0 — HDR10 video rendered with only the red channel ("someone messed with colors", the 2020 LG OLED 4K HDR clip) — ROOT-CAUSED + FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-29.** The clip is AV1 Main 3840×2160 `yuv420p10le`, tv range, `bt2020nc`/`smpte2084`/`bt2020`, hardware-decoded to a **P010** pool; the SDR VP9/BT.709 webm always looked right. Symptom on the mint-cocktail frame: `meanR 41.4 / meanG 3.9 / meanB 3.0`, `redDominantPct 29.2` — a red-only image. **Root cause (a): descriptor collision.** The pool-generation cache raised `kMaxVideoSlots` to 24 (SRVs 1..48) while the tone-stats UAVs were still hard-coded at descriptor **20**; creating them overwrote slot 9's UV SRV and slot 10's Y/UV SRVs. With luma valid and chroma reading a UAV descriptor as zero, BT.2020 YCbCr→RGB collapses to `(Y, ~0, ~0)` — red. The HDR pool is generation **2** (NV12 gen 1 came first), so its slots sit at indices 8..15 and land exactly on the clobbered descriptors. **Root cause (b): stale `m_inputFormat`.** The global was written only on the re-import branch, so a cached generation (switch back to SDR) kept the previous content's format constant and shaded NV12 planes as P010. **Fix:** `kSrvHeapSize = 128` / `kToneStatsUavBase = 64` with `static_assert`s pinning the layout, `kVideoSrvBase`/`kVideoSrvPerSlot` single-sourced for the SRV base, renderer slots keyed by `(sharedHandle, poolGeneration)`, and `m_inputFormat` derived from the bound slot's `GetDesc().Format` on **every** draw (cache hit included). **Verified on the exact repro path** — SDR NV12 gen 1 → HDR P010 gen 2 in one runtime, then the quit snapshot: the same mint-cocktail frame now reads **`meanR 18.7 / meanG 22.0 / meanB 22.7`, `redDominantPct 0`**, green mint leaves and neutral glass visible, matching the source frame (`22.8 / 23.9 / 24.7`); a fresh HDR open reads `18.9 / 22.2 / 22.9`; the SDR control still renders correctly (`41.0 / 23.6 / 13.1`). **Re-run on the shipped runtime** (`paiint-wallpaper-runtime.exe` SHA256 `FB7AD253…F35D660B`, the binary inside the rebuilt MSIX): `18.8 / 22.1 / 22.8`, 0 % red-dominant, no device removal. Evidence: `%TEMP%\opencode\color3-switch.jpg` + `color3-switch.log` (`pool rebuilt ... NV12 gen=1` → `pool rebuilt ... P010 gen=2`). (`D3D12Renderer.cpp/.hpp`)

- [x] **P1 — HDR video wallpapers render with crushed shadows ("too much black") on an SDR display — user-reported 2026-10-19; root-caused; TARGET = mpv/libplacebo parity (unit 20) — IMPLEMENTED + VERIFIED 2026-10-20.** Measured on the Spider-Man DV-P8.1 test frame (t=180 s, SDR desktop): engine ACES path **27 % of pixels below code 4** vs VLC 3.0.23 **9 %**; per-pixel codes at 1 cd/m²: engine **5** vs VLC **20** vs BT.2390 **15**; at 100 cd/m²: **205** vs 149 vs 186. Root cause: ACES filmic toe (slope 0.214) on ÷203-normalized linear + sRGB encode. **User decision: match mpv, not BT.2390/VLC** (ground truth = installed mpv 0.41.0 / **libplacebo v7.360.0**). **Shipped:** (1) `graphics/renderer/HdrToneMapping.hpp` — libplacebo's anchors (SDR white 203, target black 203/1000), ST.2094 knee pick, single-pivot polynomial spline (`Pa`/`Qa`/`Qb`, `slope_tuning 1.5`, `slope_offset 0.2`, `spline_contrast 0.5`, knee 0.4/0.1/0.8) and the 20-frame IIR + 1 %/3 % scene hysteresis; (2) a **GPU peak-detection compute pass** (libplacebo's `pl_shader_detect_peak`: Rec.2020 Y → PQ → 14-bit, 1 % black cutoff, max + mean, sampled every 2 pixels, re-measured only on a new decoded frame) with per-frame-context readback; (3) the pixel shader now tone-maps the **IPT intensity** (Ebner-Fairchild, hue preserved, libplacebo's hull clamp) instead of per RGB channel, and outputs black-scaled (`(x-0.001)/0.999`) with gamma 2.2; (4) the source stays in BT.2020 until the tone map (the old 2020→709→2020 round trip also mis-tagged the HDR-output path as BT.2020 — fixed as a side effect). **Evidence:** the CPU port reproduces mpv's own 16-bit captures of a 24-band PQ ramp to **0.0996 codes/255** (mean) / 0.39 (max) — pinned by 4 new unit tests; the engine's **GPU** detector measures the same ramp at max 4002.6 cd/m² / avg 0.42177 PQ (model: 4000 / 0.42184) and builds the identical curve (srcPivot 0.42177, dstPivot 0.37455, slope 0.85737); and the **on-screen** wallpaper (4K desktop capture) matches the model to **±1 code, median 0.30** over the 24 bands. New in-repo tools: `tools/color/parity_check.py` (measures a capture, prints the table, exits non-zero on drift) and the rewritten `tools/color/shader_port.py` (mirror of the new path + the mpv reference table). Suite **808/808** green.
- [ ] **P2 — the engine has no GPU peak-detection pass; adding one must not cost mpv's 73 % compute.** Measured 2026-10-20 on the 4K AV1/PQ clip (RX 6700 XT, per-GPU-engine counters): mpv gpu-next = **3D 27 %, compute 73 %, video-decode 74–76 %** (the compute load IS libplacebo's full-resolution peak-detection pass, re-run every rendered frame); REX today = **3D 14 %, compute 0 %, video-decode 72–78 %** (identical decode path, half the pixels of mpv's 1080p window, trivial shader). Plan: run the stats pass on a **sampled grid (stride 2)** and only when the **decoded frame changes** (the IIR still advances every rendered frame) → same curve, ~10× cheaper than mpv. Acceptance: stats pass ≤2 % of one GPU frame at 4K; no measurable fps change. **Status: DONE — re-measured 2026-10-20 on the new build (4K HEVC 10-bit clip): GPU compute reads 0 % (below the 0.4 % counter threshold), GPU 3D 1.4–2.1 %, video-decode 50 % — vs mpv gpu-next's compute 73 % / 3D 27 %.**
- [x] **P2 — engine stalls found while measuring utilisation (2026-10-20, 4K AV1/PQ clip).** (a) `late frame: total=45.00 ms target=16.68 ms` on clip start (one 2.7× frame overrun during decoder/pool warm-up); (b) `slow housekeeping: CheckDisplayChanges took 8.15 ms on the render thread` — the display-change probe ran **on the render thread** and blocked a frame (measured at 415 ms and 1084 ms on a contended desktop). **FIXED 2026-10-20 (unit 23):** the probe now runs on a **housekeeping worker thread** (`Engine::HousekeepingLoop`); the render thread only raises a request flag and never waits for it, so no probe can stall a frame. The worker is stopped and joined before the renderer/video teardown. **Evidence (the 4K HEVC 10-bit + 5.1 clip that produced 242 late frames):** **0 late frames, 0 slow-housekeeping warnings, exactly 24.0 fps** (60 frames per 2.50 s). Suite **809/809**.
- [ ] **P2 — output transfer differs from mpv by ~1–2 codes in the shadows.** mpv's swapchain is `RGB_FULL_G22_NONE_P709` (gamma 2.2) while REX writes sRGB-encoded values into an sRGB/BT.709 swapchain; the difference is small but systematic below code ~16. Decide once (G22 to match mpv's window, or keep sRGB for the OS's benefit) and document it.
- [x] **P2 — the wallpaper is upscaled with a single bilinear blit; mpv uses Lanczos by default — FIXED 2026-10-20 (unit 26).** The renderer's sampler is `D3D12_FILTER_MIN_MAG_MIP_LINEAR` and the video is drawn as one fullscreen quad, so a video smaller than the desktop was magnified with the sampler's bilinear filter — visibly soft next to mpv (whose gpu-next default is `--scale=lanczos`). **Fix:** the shader now resamples the **luma with a Catmull-Rom (bicubic) kernel** — the "at minimum a Catmull-Rom pass" this item asked for — and the chroma keeps the bilinear tap (it is subsampled anyway, and doubling the tap count for it costs more than it shows). 16 taps (4x4), and **only when the source is actually magnified**: the magnification factor is derived from the UV derivatives (source texels per output pixel), so it needs no extra root constants (the budget is exactly 64/64 DWORDs) and a 1:1 source keeps the single exact tap. **Evidence (2026-10-21):** on the 24-band ramp (1920x1080 on a 3840x2160 desktop, a 2x magnification) the band-edge transition measures **1.37 screen px** (10-90 %, median over 9 edges) — a 2x bilinear upscale cannot go below 2 px, so the resampler is provably active; GPU 3D rises from 1.4-2 % to **8.3 %** with the 16 taps, and the content still plays at exactly its source rate (25 fps, 30 fps). The shader's compile diagnostics are now logged too (a failed compile used to print no reason at all). **Remaining:** a true separable **Lanczos** (mpv's `--scale=lanczos`, 6 taps per axis) needs two passes (horizontal + vertical) or 36 taps in one — a separate item, not done here. **Analysed 2026-10-21 (unit 32):** it is *not* a shader-local change. A separable scale needs the horizontal and vertical passes separated, which means an intermediate texture — and the intermediate would have to hold the **decoded RGB**, because the current shader does YUV→RGB, the tone map and the output transfer in ONE pass. So a two-pass Lanczos is a colour-path refactor (pass 1 = decode into an intermediate, pass 2 = scale + tone map + output), not a kernel swap: the 36-tap single-pass variant would cost ~2.25x the Catmull-Rom's GPU time (measured 11 % GPU 3D at 4K → ~25 %) for a kernel that is still not separable. The Catmull-Rom now runs for real and is mpv's own `--scale=catmullrom` preset; the separable Lanczos stays a deliberate follow-up, and the colour path it would rewrite is the one the mpv parity check pins.
- [x] **P2 — the Catmull-Rom kernel never ran: the magnification test was inverted — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 32).** The shader picks between the 16-tap kernel and the sampler's bilinear tap from the UV derivatives: `scale` is *source texels per output pixel*, so a **magnified** source (the video is smaller than the desktop) has `scale < 1` and a 1:1 or minified source has `scale >= 1`. The early-out tested `scale <= 1.0001` — the other way round — so the kernel was skipped on every upscale (the only case it exists for) while a minified source paid for 16 taps it did not need. The code's own comment ("1:1 or minified") already described the intended test, so the condition had drifted from its documentation. **Fix:** the early-out now tests `scale >= 1.0001` in both axes. **Evidence (the 24-band ramp, 1920x1080 on a 3840x2160 desktop, the same capture method before and after):** the band-edge 10-90 % transition goes from a **median 2.20 px** (bilinear) to **1.74 px**, the worst edge from 6.2 px to 2.2 px, and the on-screen parity against the mpv model is unchanged (**median 0.67 codes, max 3.17**) because the band interiors are flat. GPU 3D at 4K rises from **1.4-2.1 % to ~11 %** (the 16 taps) and the content still plays at exactly the source rate (**25.07 fps** on a 25 fps clip, 0 late frames). A 1:1 source keeps the single exact tap, so a 4K clip on a 4K desktop pays nothing. (The unit-26 claim "the band-edge transition is 1.37 screen px — a 2x bilinear upscale cannot go below 2 px, so the resampler is provably active" was **wrong on both counts**: 1.37 px is what bilinear measures at 2x, and the resampler was *not* active.)
- [x] **P2 — the parity fixture and the parity model had drifted apart, so the on-screen check compared the clip against the wrong band values — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 31).** The 24-band PQ ramp is generated by `tools/color/make_bands.py` and measured against `tools/color/shader_port.py`'s `MPV_BANDS` — the list mpv 0.41.0's own 16-bit captures were taken on. Unit 22 (`e18de18`) changed the **generator's** copy of that list to `[… 200, 300, 500, 800, 1200, 2000, 3000 …]` and left the model on `[… 203, 300, 400, 600, 800, 1000, 2000 …]`, so from then on the fixture really did carry 500/800/1200/2000/3000 cd/m² in the upper bands while the model expected 400/600/800/1000/2000 — a **+7…+17 code "regression" on the bright bands that the engine never had** (the clip was wrong, not the renderer). The generator's own comment ("the values match MPV_BANDS in shader_port.py") had become false, and the earlier "the DWM's SDR→HDR mapping lifts the highlights" reading was a red herring. **Fix:** the generator now **imports** `MPV_BANDS` from `shader_port.py` — one list, so the fixture, the model and the mpv reference cannot drift apart again. The clip was rebuilt (`.yuv` + lossless HEVC, FULL range) and its per-band codes now match the nominal values within **2.7 codes** (the encoder's 10-bit rounding). **Evidence:** the on-screen capture now matches the model to a **median of 0.67 codes** (max 3.17 — the 0.01 cd/m² band, below one code of the darkest step) across all 24 bands, with the bright bands within 1.5 codes. The engine's own peak detector agrees with the fixture: **4087 nits** for the 4000-nit band (the clip's top code is 926), avg 0.42287 PQ (model 0.42184).
- [x] **P3 — chroma siting is never read (`chroma_sample_location` is absent from the whole tree) — RESOLVED as not actionable (2026-10-21).** MPEG-1 (centre) vs MPEG-2/H.264 (left) chroma siting shifts the chroma by up to half a chroma pixel. **Findings:** (1) the practical impact is ~zero - swscale's default siting is the MPEG-2 one (left), which is what H.264 and HEVC use (the overwhelming majority of wallpaper content), so only MPEG-1 video would differ; (2) swscale has **no public setter** for the position: `src_h_chr_pos`/`src_v_chr_pos` live in `SwsFormat`, which is only reachable through `sws_alloc_context` + `sws_init_context` with hand-built `SwsFilter` chains (`sws_getContext` and `sws_setColorspaceDetails` take no chroma position - verified in the shipped `libswscale/swscale.h`, vcpkg x64-windows). Reaching it would mean rebuilding the scaler by hand for a format that is essentially extinct. **Left as documented, not implemented** - if a user ever hits MPEG-1 content, the fix belongs in the decoder's own chroma placement, not in the scaler setup.
- [ ] **P3 — ICC profiles are ignored (no lcms2 in the tree); mpv has lcms2 enabled.** SDR videos that carry an ICC profile (camera/DRI masters) are rendered as if untagged (BT.601/709 matrices) → a colour cast vs mpv. Low impact for wallpaper content, but it is a correctness gap and cheap to state.
- [x] **P2 — no output dithering on the 10-bit → 8-bit SDR path** (research B/C): truncation bands dark gradients; add ordered/blue-noise dither at the SDR encode, measure with CAMBI. **FIXED 2026-10-20 (unit 22):** the SDR back buffer is now **10-bit** (`R10G10B10A2_UNORM` — the exact format mpv's window uses, measured on mpv 0.41.0) and the pixel shader **dithers** before the write: Interleaved Gradient Noise (Jimenez) at **one code of the output depth**, added to the ENCODED signal (after the transfer function, where the quantisation happens). The amplitude is compiled into the shader per pipeline state (`TARGET_BITS`: 10 / 8 / 0 for the FP16 HDR output, which is not quantised) and the rule lives in `graphics/renderer/OutputDither.hpp` so the shader and the tests cannot drift (`ColorPipeline.OutputDitherMatchesTheTargetDepth` green). The swapchain falls back to 8-bit UNORM if the driver refuses the 10-bit buffer (at creation **and** on a later resize; the refusal is remembered so it is not retried every refresh) and the SDR pipeline state is rebuilt when the format changes (a PSO's RTV format must match the buffer it renders into). **Live:** `Back buffer: R10G10B10A2_UNORM SDR 10-bit (sRGB/BT.709)`. On an 8-bit *desktop* the DWM's own dither handles the final 10→8 step, so the visible banding is gone either way. Suite **809/809**.
- [x] **P2 — the SDR back buffer was 8-bit while mpv's window is 10-bit** (research B/C). **FIXED 2026-10-20 (unit 22, the same change as the dither above):** `R8G8B8A8_UNORM` → `R10G10B10A2_UNORM`, colour space unchanged (`RGB_FULL_G22_NONE_P709` — the same space mpv's window declares). Windows 11 also has a per-monitor "Desktop mode: 8-bit / 10-bit" setting, so with this change the engine can deliver true 10-bit to the compositor on a 10-bit desktop. The HDR path is untouched (`R16G16B16A16_FLOAT`).
- [x] **P2 — SDR-white handling is fixed constants (paper 203 / SDR 100); no OS query — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 28).** On an HDR display the OS knows how bright "SDR white" should be — it is the Windows "SDR content brightness" slider — and exposes it as a fixed-point multiplier of the 80-nit reference (`DISPLAYCONFIG_SDR_WHITE_LEVEL`, nits = (level/1000)·80). The engine now reads it: `SdrWhiteLevel.hpp` (header-only — a new .cpp would need a CMakeLists edit, which the repo forbids) walks the active display-config paths, matches the DXGI output the window sits on (`SwapChain::GetOutputDeviceName()`) and returns the level; the renderer takes it when the output is in an HDR mode and otherwise keeps the 100-nit fallback (the SDR diffuse white of ITU-R BT.2408). The constant lane was already correct (the HDR branch carries the SDR white, the SDR branch carries the tone curve's peak) — only the value was missing. The conversion is pure and pinned by a new unit test (`ColorPipeline.SdrWhiteLevelConversion`). Live: the engine starts and plays with the query in place; on this SDR display the OS reports nothing and the fallback holds. Suite **814/814**.
- [ ] **P3 — Dolby Vision Profile 5 renders garbage; detect and refuse** (research C): P8.x (compat id 1/2/4/6) = HDR10/HLG base layer — fine; P5 IPTPQc2 has no compatible base — flag unsupported instead of decoding nonsense (`dvcC`/`dvvC` side data).
- [ ] **P3 — chroma upsampling is plain bilinear (`MIN_MAG_MIP_LINEAR`)** (research C): acceptable today; upgrade to chroma-aware (catmull-rom/spline) at the next color-path touch.
- [ ] **P3 — verification harness still lives in `%TEMP%`** (research D): port the metric stack (trusted reference chains + CAMBI/ΔEITP/XPSNR, offscreen readback) into `rex-core/tools/color/` so "file == wallpaper" checks are reproducible in-repo; commit a 10-bit gradient-ramp fixture.

### Video decode path (2026-10-20)
- [x] **P1 — the pre-sized hardware frames context broke D3D11VA: 4K clips fell back to SOFTWARE decode and the wallpaper stuttered — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 34).** The `get_format` callback (unit 29/30) pre-allocated the decode surfaces (`av_hwframe_ctx_alloc(ctx->hw_device_ctx)` + `av_hwframe_ctx_init`) so FFmpeg would not re-allocate them when the resolution changes mid-stream. **FFmpeg's D3D11VA decoder creates the decode texture itself** (it keeps it in the frames context's `user_opaque`) and its `pool_init()` refuses a caller-allocated context: `AVD3D11VAFramesContext.texture not set` → `Failed setup for format d3d11` → the hwaccel never initialises → **software decode**. Measured on the user's own 4K 60 fps clip (`D:\live wallpaper\ishtar-fate.3840x2160.mp4`): `software frame conversion (CPU upload) active decodePath=SW_BGRA8` and **14 late frames of 150–180 ms in 35 s** (a visible hitch every 2.7 s). The 10-bit ramp did not show it because its pre-sizing *failed* and fell back to FFmpeg allocating the frames — which is exactly what now happens always. **Fix:** the pre-sizing is gone; the callback only selects `AV_PIX_FMT_D3D11` (and still skips chroma layouts D3D11VA cannot decode ✓). **After:** the same clip decodes on the GPU (`decoder surface: 3840x2176 fmt=NV12 ... pool=NV12`), **0 late frames**, **0 A/V corrections**, and exactly **60.03 fps** on a 60 fps source (2040 frames in 33.98 s). The allocation churn the pre-sizing avoided is a far smaller cost than a 4K software decode.

- [x] **P1 — concurrent Seek/Close on a frame-threaded decoder aborts the runtime (FFmpeg `av_assert0(fctx->async_lock)`) — FIXED + VERIFIED 2026-10-20.** Found as a **flaky test**: `DecoderLifetimeTest.ConcurrentSeekCloseAndReadCompletes` failed ~1 run in 3 (and 1 in 4 under load) with `Assertion fctx->async_lock failed at libavcodec/pthread_frame.c:174` — an FFmpeg **abort**, i.e. the whole wallpaper runtime dies. **Root cause:** the software path runs a *frame-threaded* decoder (`m_decCtx->thread_count = 0` → FFmpeg spawns frame workers, `FfmpegVideoDecoder.cpp:255`), and that machinery holds an internal async lock which `avcodec_receive_frame()` releases while it runs — `avcodec_flush_buffers()` (Seek) and `avcodec_free_context()` (Close) release it too, so an overlap **releases it twice** and trips `av_assert0` in `async_unlock()` (`libavcodec/pthread_frame.c`). The overlap was reachable: `VideoPlaybackController::Close()` calls `decoder->Close()` (which frees the codec context) **outside** `m_decodeMutex`, while a concurrent `Seek()` → `avcodec_flush_buffers()` runs under it — a use-after-free on the context as well. **Fix:** a codec-exclusion mutex inside `FfmpegVideoDecoder` (`m_codecMutex`, recursive because `DecodeFrame` calls `SeekInternal` for the EOF loop wrap) taken by `DecodeFrame`, `Seek`/`SeekInternal`, `Flush`, `SelectStreamByResolution` (which also frees/reopens the context) and `Close`; plus a null-context guard in `SeekInternal` (a flush on a freed/null context is its own FFmpeg assert). **Evidence:** 6 rounds × 4 concurrent copies of the test = **24/24 green** (it failed ~1 in 4 rounds before), full suite **808/808**.
- [x] **P2 — `SpeedClockTest.PositionAdvancesAtPlaybackRate` fails whenever the console display sleeps (ambient, not a rate bug) — FIXED 2026-10-20.** The engine suspends the wallpaper when the OS reports `GUID_CONSOLE_DISPLAY_STATE` as off (`WorkerWManager` power broadcast → "console display off — suspending wallpaper"), and this box turns the monitor off after **300 s of idle** — i.e. after any multi-minute build/test cycle. The engine then correctly auto-pauses and the rate measurement reads 0.0009 s instead of ~2 s (the same ambient failure the unit-9 run hit earlier). **Fix (test-only):** the test holds the display awake with `SetThreadExecutionState(ES_CONTINUOUS|ES_SYSTEM_REQUIRED|ES_DISPLAY_REQUIRED)` (which also wakes an already-sleeping monitor), waits up to 3 s for the engine to reach `Running`, and **skips** — with the cause named — if it never does (no live display → the rate is unmeasurable). The suspend/resume arbitration itself stays covered by the engine's own tests.

### Audio decode / playback path (2026-10-20 audit)

Audited the whole audio path against mpv/libav behaviour on the hardest in-tree case (TheOdyssey IMAX: 4K HEVC Main10 + **DTS-HD MA 5.1**, 48 kHz). **The path is healthy**: decode = FFmpeg software decoder on a second demuxer of the same file; the endpoint is opened at the *source* rate with `AUDCLNT_STREAMFLAGS_AUTOCONVERTPCM` (Windows does the rate conversion, so no 44.1↔48 drift); 6→2 ch downmix by a hand-rolled matrix (centre feeds both, surrounds −3 dB, LFE dropped — as documented); all the common decoder sample formats are converted correctly (`ReadSample`, `AudioPlayer.cpp:844`); the health log is objective (`audio out: N fps decoded, N fps rendered, N underruns, peak …`).

- [x] **P2 — the audio decode runs even when MUTED, and it costs more CPU than the entire video pipeline — FIXED 2026-10-20 (unit 25).** Measured 2026-10-20 (same clip, 25 s steady-state, process CPU as % of one core): **no audio track 0.7 %** → **5.1 DTS-HD MA unmuted 4.5 %** → **muted 3.0 %** (was 7.7 % before the fix). The wallpaper is muted by default (protocol LOAD defaults to `mute=true`), so the common case was burning CPU on audio nobody heard. **Fix:** while nothing can be heard (muted, or volume 0) the AudioPlayer does **not decode at all** — the demuxer simply stops being read, so it keeps its position, and unmuting re-seeks the audio to the *video's* position (`VideoPlaybackController::SetMute` asks for the seek BEFORE the decode resumes, and the seek is request-based so it is consumed on the audio thread) so the sound comes back in sync with the picture. The underrun counter no longer counts the deliberate silence (it reported hundreds of "underruns" for a muted wallpaper — the health line now reads `0 fps decoded, 33782 fps rendered, 0 underruns`). Also added: the CLI gained `--no-fullscreen-pause` (the engine pauses the wallpaper when a TRUE full-screen app owns the foreground — correct behaviour, but it made every measurement run on this box pause, because the terminal here runs full-screen).
- [x] **P2 — the tempo resampler is a naive linear interpolation (aliasing when speed ≠ 1) — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 27).** `AudioPlayer::ResampleLinear` stretched srcFrames→dstFrames with no anti-aliasing filter, so speeding the content up folded the spectrum back over itself (mpv/libav resample with a polyphase FIR). **Fix:** the tempo path now uses `AudioPlayer::ResampleWindowedSinc` - an 8-tap Blackman-windowed sinc with the cutoff at **0.5·min(1, 1/ratio)** cycles per source sample, so speeding the content up lowers the cutoff and filters the aliasing band out instead of folding it in. **Two new unit tests** (`test_audio_resample.cpp`): a smooth signal passes through within 0.09 % (an 8-tap kernel cannot reconstruct content that sits at Nyquist, so the property is pinned on a signal the kernel can reproduce), and a tone **above** the new Nyquist is filtered out where the linear version aliased it back. The second test earned its keep immediately: it caught a real bug in the first draft, where the cutoff was normalised to Nyquist but used as cycles-per-sample and so was 2× too high - the anti-aliasing was not actually happening. The linear resampler is kept for the existing contract tests. Suite **813/813**.
- [x] **P2 — no A/V sync correction between the WASAPI endpoint clock and the engine's video clock — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 29).** `AudioPlayer::GetPosition()` (the last decoded PTS) and `VideoPlaybackController::GetPosition()` both existed and nothing compared them: the audio is clocked by the WASAPI endpoint and the video by the engine's wall clock, so a drift between them (or a device change that moved the endpoint's clock) was invisible. **Fix:** the watchdog measures the **CHANGE** in the audio-vs-video offset, not the offset itself — both positions lead their playhead by a fixed amount (the audio ring's depth, the video scheduler's slots), so the offset carries a constant bias that must not be mistaken for an error. A new `AudioPlayer::GetPlayedPosition()` (the decoded position minus what the ring still holds) makes the comparison meaningful. The audio is re-seeked onto the video's position only when the change passes **250 ms** — far above any scheduling artefact — and never while nothing can be heard (the decode is suspended then, so the audio position is frozen BY DESIGN and would look like a huge drift). The decision is a pure function (`AvSyncNeedsCorrection`), pinned by a new unit test. Suite **815/815**.
- [x] **P3 — the A/V drift was invisible: a sync error is silent by nature — FIXED 2026-10-21 (unit 33).** The watchdog computed the drift and acted on it, but nothing *reported* it: the drift lived in two private atomics with no reader, so the only way to learn that the audio and the video had parted was to hear it. **Fix:** the drift and the correction count are now part of the telemetry — `VideoPlaybackTelemetry` / `EngineTelemetry` carry `avSyncDriftMs` and `avSyncCorrections`, the runtime's `GET_STATUS` JSON exposes them (`avSyncDriftMs`, `avSyncCorrections`), the machine-readable `runtime-telemetry.log` line carries `avDrift=…ms avCorr=…`, the engine's telemetry panel prints them, and the watchdog logs the drift every check (~4 s, debug) plus a warning when it corrects. **Measured** on the PQ ramp with an AAC track (`--unmuted`): the drift stays at **-3 ms / +7 ms** with **0 corrections** — the clocks hold. **Two log-line bugs found and fixed on the way:** the drift warning used **printf** placeholders (`%.0f`) in a `fmt`-formatted logger, so it had been printing its own format string instead of the numbers since it was written — the same was true of the decoder's "hardware frames pre-sized" line; both are fmt-style now. (A tree-wide scan found no other printf-style log lines in the engine.)
- [x] **P2 — a SECOND wallpaper engine on the same desktop collapses the video pipeline into a −3.8 s backlog — FIXED 2026-10-20 (unit 24).** Found while measuring: the Electron supervisor respawns its runtime the instant any runtime process dies, and the runtime refuses a second instance by named mutex — so a headless CLI run and the app fought over the desktop. Under that fight the telemetry showed `decFps` steady at 24.0 while `rendFps` fell 23.8 → 5.1, `effFps` 24.0 → 1.7, **`reserve` +163 ms → −3831 ms**, `schedDrops` 2 → 1616, 242 late-frame warnings (each ~1 s inside `BeginFrame`), and the wallpaper crawled at 2–3 fps for ~90 s before snapping back to a perfect 24 fps. **Three fixes:** (1) **the trigger was the housekeeping stall** — the display-topology probe blocked the render thread for 415/1084 ms, so the renderer consumed 2–3 frames/s, the ring stayed full and the decoder stayed blocked; the probe now runs on a housekeeping worker thread (unit 23), and the same clip now runs with **0 late frames and exactly 24.0 fps**. (2) **the backlog can no longer become an equilibrium**: the scheduler may present the NEWEST published frame (instead of the newest due one) when the published content is more than a second behind the wall clock (`FrameScheduler::Tick(targetPts, allowCatchUp)`, off by default so the "never present a future frame" contract holds — that contract is what made the wallpaper read as 2x before); the content jumps forward, the ring drains and the decoder unblocks. (3) **the second instance now refuses instead of fighting**: the CLI's direct mode takes the same named-mutex guard the persistent `--runtime` mode took, so a headless run against a live app exits with "another wallpaper runtime already owns the desktop layer" (exit 3) instead of both engines fighting for the WorkerW layer. Tests: 2 new scheduler tests — **811/811**.

### New discoveries (2026-10-17, full-repo quality audit)

> Full consolidated report: [`docs/bugs/AUDIT-2026-10-17-quality-audit.md`](docs/bugs/AUDIT-2026-10-17-quality-audit.md) — 10 read-only agents, **2×P0 / 14×P1 / ~30×P2 / ~55×P3**, every finding at `file:line`. A1/A2/A3 re-verified: A1 fixed (one residual), A2 partial, A3 fixed. Tooling: no `.clang-format`/`.clang-tidy`; CI clang-tidy step is a **no-op** (target doesn't exist + continue-on-error); cache has no `/W3`/`/W4`; `C:/vcpkg/.../vcpkg.cmake` is a **STUB** blocking fresh configures. **A1/A2 residual note (2026-10-19):** both residuals closed since — `SetProperty("audioMode")` dereferences `m_videoManager` under `m_mutex` (`Engine.cpp:1228-1237`), and the recovery retry-drop is fixed by the Batch 2 consume-at-attempt gate (`RecoveryBackoff.hpp` `DecideRecovery`). The audit-time characterisation is superseded.
> **Batch 1 (user-approved) FIXED 2026-10-17** — the 2×P0 + the stutter-suspect decoder item + audio EXTENSIBLE + audio-mode end-to-end (checked below; 9 new unit tests + 2 extended, RED→GREEN each, full suite 743/743 green).
> **Batch 2 DONE 2026-10-17** — all Batch-2 P1s fixed in two commits: recovery consume-at-attempt gate (+2 tests, RED C3861→GREEN), VideoManager entry-iteration locks, controller `SnapshotDecoder` report path, WorkerW `ReacquireWorkerW` capture UAF; then HDR `ResizeBuffers` refs/drain + state-commit, shared-queue fence race (mutex + per-wait events), static-image RowPitch 256-alignment, TextureCache snapshot lifetime + compositor HRESULT hardening (the last two live in currently-unwired modules — API fixed + guards added, noted per item), plus WIC 32-bit alloc overflow (P3 #14). Full suite 745/745 green. Batches 3–4 remain.
> **Batch 3 in progress (2026-10-18)** — unit 1: the Oppenheimer 4:4:4 thread-pin + a latent BT.601 conversion path for software-opened files (both fixed, see entries below). Unit 2 (FIX B): strip-parallel swscale for 4:4:4 10-bit (fixed, see entry; suite 758/758; runtime relinked twice 2026-10-18). Unit 3: shared video-fence sequence, audit #5 (fixed, see entry; suite 760/760; runtime relinked 2026-10-18 23:06). Unit 4: Engine cross-thread state — PositionClock, atomic telemetry counters, locked getters + audioMode (audit #2/#3/#4/#9; fixed, see entry; suite 766/766; runtime relinked 2026-10-18 23:31). Unit 5: Engine lifecycle guards — teardown vs render frame (#6), Shutdown re-entrancy (#7), Play/Stop transition table (#8) (fixed, see entry; suite 769/769; runtime relinked 2026-10-18 23:52). Unit 6: HDR PSO-failure desync + DXGI adapter-enum null deref (audit §5.6 #4/#5; fixed, see entry; suite 770/770; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 00:10). Unit 7: engine lock-narrowing — recovery/StopMonitor heavy work moved outside `m_mutex` (audit #14; fixed, see entry; suite 770/770; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 00:26). Unit 8: FrameScheduler `Clear()` vs racing `Tick()` lost update — consumed watermark + clear epoch packed into one atomic, forward-only commit CAS, epoch-paired burst gate (audit §5.4 #4); dead `ResetPresentationClock` deleted (PV9); SPSC lapping contract documented (PV14) (fixed, see entry; suite 773/773; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 00:57). Unit 9: playback EOS restart latch (`m_eosReached` + pure `NeedsRewindOnPlay` — a non-looping video can restart after natural EOF, audit §5.4 #5), `VideoManager::Shutdown` self-contained (monitor entries + controller under `m_monitorMutex`, `Tick` guarded — §5.4 #7), per-monitor tick target = its own published position instead of hardcoded `0.0` (§5.4 #8, latent); the unit-9 suite run's one failure (`SpeedClockTest`) diagnosed to an ambient auto-pause freezing the clock (intended fullscreen/suspend behavior — not a regression) and hardened out of the test (fixed, see entry; suite 777/777; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 10:47). Unit 10: decoder pool now builds into temporaries and commits only on full success (null-slot fast path gone — §5.2 #2), the renderer slot cache evicts stale/LRU generations instead of dying at 8 after one pool rebuild (§5.2 #3 / F1), the SW path leases slots through `PickPoolSlot()` + stamps like HW (§5.2 #4 / §5.4 #3), and partial-QI failures no longer leak video interfaces (§5.2 #6); `PickSlotOldestSafe` is now the real function under test (#15 tie-in) (fixed, see entry; suite 778/778; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 11:10). Unit 11: runtime P2s — telemetry sampler now sleeps in 250 ms slices (SHUTDOWN join no longer races Electron's 3 s kill), watchdog + sampler take a new non-blocking `TryGetTelemetry` (try-lock; skip the interval instead of stalling on the engine lock), and an oversized frame prefix now tears the session down instead of desyncing forever (zero-length still answers `malformed frame` and continues; live smoke: zero→continue→SHUTDOWN, oversized→warning→close) (fixed, see entry; suite 779/779; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 11:38). Unit 12: audio P2s — AudioCapture COM ref now tracked (`m_comInitialized`, no `CoUninitialize` underflow after `RPC_E_CHANGED_MODE`), capture thread gets its own MTA apartment, device loss (`AUDCLNT_E_DEVICE_INVALIDATED`/`SERVICE_NOT_RUNNING`) now re-opens instead of silently freezing, SILENT/null buffers feed zeros, AudioPlayer getters are atomic, and a failed `DoSeek` no longer pretends to succeed (fixed, see entry; suite 782/782; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 12:05). Unit 13 (app): quit fallback 3→5 s + cleared on exit, INIT failure drops the wedged process (no "already initialized" loop), heartbeat capability-probed and armed (miss limit 3) instead of parked at Infinity, live updates merged into `lastApplied` + debounced persist, login item default-enabled first run only (fixed, see entry; node checks 11/11 + live `verify-supervisor.cjs` incl. new arming assertion; suite unchanged 782/782). Unit 14 (graphics): compositor P2s — main-queue frame fence + bounded GPU waits (2 s) + device-removed latch; layer-texture and per-back-buffer state tracking; SRV slot recycle on layer replace; shared-handle import validated/derived from `GetDesc()` (fixed, see entry; suite 791/791; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 12:30). Remaining: **Batch-4 hygiene (§6)** — orphaned `wallpaper/renderer/` archive (git mv), dead modules (folds in §5.6 #6/#7 + §5.8 #10/#12), unbuilt tests, root clutter, stale docs. Units 15–16 closed the native + app P3 residual sweeps (own entries below).

> **Video output research DONE 2026-10-19 (four sourced tracks → [`research-video.md`](research-video.md)):** verdict — VLC 3.x is good-but-dated, not the ceiling (one fixed Hable curve, fixed 100-nit anchor, no black-point handling); quality leaders are madVR (frozen public renderer) and mpv/libplacebo gpu-next (spline/BT.2390 + black-point compensation + dithering). The "too much black" complaint is root-caused; fix designed (unit 20): **BT.2390 EETF default + BT.2408 anchors (203-nit SDR white; source peak from MaxCLL, fallback 1000)** with an exact **VLC-compatible preset**. New backlog items below (output dithering, DV-P5 detect-and-refuse, chroma upsampling, OS SDR-white query, in-repo verification harness).

- [x] **P0 — Gallery restore re-decoded every video on every launch → hundreds of MB of GPU VRAM pinned while "closed" to tray — FIXED + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-19 (built, relaunched, measured).** Of 23 video entries (22 existing files, 32 GB total), **14 had `thumbnail: ""`** and were re-decoded via `<video>` on every start, including 4K/HDR 240 fps clips. Isolated Electron 43 repro with 12 of those files: **3.9 GB dedicated VRAM peak → 2.5 GB settled**; `win.hide()` freed nothing (Chromium keeps decoder/caches and `close` only hides to tray). Live REX GPU process: **344 MB dedicated + 78 MB shared** vs **84 MB** for a blank 1440p Electron window and **148 MB** for a 27-image gallery. Fixes: `thumbnail.js` now releases the media pipeline on every exit path (`pause`, clear `src`, `load()`) and revokes the blob URL in `createThumbnail`; the restore loop persists the decoded poster (`saveThumbnail` + `galleryAdd({thumbnail})`) so the decode happens **once**, not every launch. Lint/typecheck green. **Live verification (built + relaunched):** first relaunch migrated 13/14 missing posters (now **21/23** videos cached; the two left are one missing file and the Oppenheimer HEVC 4:4:4 10-bit MKV Chromium cannot decode). Second clean launch: **no audio-service process at all**, GPU process **238→142–181 MB** private, VRAM **344→174–220 MB**, Electron total private **312→268 MB**. (`app/src/wallpapers/thumbnail.js`, `app/src/wallpapers/WallpaperGallery.jsx`)

- [x] **P1 — Engine recovery retry consumed during backoff — FIXED 2026-10-17 (A2 residual).** `m_recoveryNeeded.exchange(false)` consumed the host request at READ time: a failed attempt re-armed the flag + scheduled a backoff deadline, but the next in-window pass cleared the flag while skipping the attempt — host-only recovery (Explorer restart, no device loss) bricked until app restart. Fix: pure `DecideRecovery(hostRequested, deviceLost, readyToAttempt)` gate (`RecoveryBackoff.hpp`), request consumed only when an attempt actually runs. `RecoveryBackoffTest.RequestConsumedOnlyWhenAttemptRuns` + `FailedAttemptRetriesAfterBackoffWindow` (RED C3861 → GREEN, incl. legacy-behavior contrast). (`Engine.cpp:1836-1874`)

Both now lock `m_monitorMutex` (all other entry walkers already did; audited line-by-line).

- [x] **P1 — TextureCache raw pointers escape lock / Compositor ignores all HRESULTs — FIXED 2026-10-17 (latent: neither module has live callers today; API + guards fixed for correctness).** (a) TextureCache entries are now `shared_ptr<CachedTexture>` snapshots (`GetOrLoad`/`Reload`/`Get`); a reload publishes a NEW object instead of mutating the live one, so a held snapshot cannot dangle on hot-reload/Evict/Clear; `ImageLoader`/`StaticImageScene` signatures follow. (b) Compositor `CreateDevice`/`CreateSrvHeap`/`CreateSwapChain` HRESULT-checked end to end (factory/adapter/queue/allocator/list/heaps/GetBuffer), `Initialize` fails cleanly instead of returning true half-built. - [x] **P1 — HDR `ResizeBuffers` refs held + no drain; `CommandQueue` fence race; static-image RowPitch unaligned — FIXED 2026-10-17.** (a) `UpdateHdrSelection` drops back-buffer refs + RTVs before `ResizeBuffers`, commits selection state ONLY on success (no SDR-claim desync), re-queries the frame-latency waitable; `SyncHdrStateLocked` drains the queue first; `RefreshHdrSelection` re-creates RTVs on both paths (was: none on failed switch). (b) `CommandQueue`: `m_fenceValue` increment + Signal under a mutex (fence value can no longer go backwards on the shared queue → no hung `WaitForValue`), per-wait events in `WaitForGPU` (shared auto-reset event lost wakeups); null guards on Signal. (c) static-image upload pads rows to `D3D12_TEXTURE_DATA_PITCH_ALIGNMENT` (256) — 1366/3440-wide images previously corrupted or failed; `WICTextureLoader` now checks `GetSize` HR and does 64-bit size math (P3 #14 overflow). All three are LIVE paths (LoadTexture / HDR toggle / shared queue). (`SwapChain.cpp`; `D3D12Renderer.cpp:1406,1459-1535`; `CommandQueue.hpp/.cpp`; `WICTextureLoader.cpp`)

- [x] **P2 (performance, 2026-10-17 triage item) — rare-profile videos silently fell to 1-thread software decode — FIXED 2026-10-18.** `OPPENHEIMER_TLR-2…HEVC_yuv444p10le`: the hw attempt "opened" (D3D11VA attached) but the driver can't decode 4:4:4, so FFmpeg fell back to software INSIDE the stream while the hardware path's `thread_count=1` + `thread_type=0` pin survived → measured 92-121 ms/frame decode (~8-11 fps) vs ≈21 ms/frame at 8 threads; plus 4:4:4p10→BGRA swscale ≈ 37-47 ms/frame on top. Fix: `D3d11vaCanDecodeChroma` gate (pure, `FfmpegVideoDecoder.hpp`) before the hw attempt — D3D11VA only outputs 4:2:0 (NV12/P010), everything else goes straight to software with auto threads; logs honest now ("hardware decode skipped: … 4:2:0 only"; `decodePath=SW_BGRA8` was hardcoded `SW_RGBA16`). Tests `HwChromaGate.*` 3/3 (RED C2039→GREEN). (`FfmpegVideoDecoder.cpp` Open step 2b)
- [x] **Latent wrong-colours path for software-opened files — FIXED 2026-10-18 (found auditing the Oppenheimer colour question).** `Open()`/`SwitchStream` pre-created the swscale context with *default* colorspace (BT.601 limited) and the old cache check (format+bit depth only) counted it as a hit → `sws_setColorspaceDetails` was never called; every SDR file that software-opens (driver-profile skip, hw open fail, new chroma gate) converted with 601 — same defect class as the deadpool tint, which had only been fixed on the fresh-create path. Fix: single `EnsureSws()` creation path (all 3 sites) that ALWAYS applies the resolved matrix/range; `SwsCacheKey`/`SwsCacheHit` carry format+bit-depth+matrix+range so a stale-colorimetry context can never be reused. Tests `SwsColorPolicy.*` 4/4 (RED C2039→GREEN). Oppenheimer itself takes the fresh-create path; its live colour verdict = desktop capture (renderer segment still unverified).
- [x] **P2 follow-up (performance, 2026-10-17 triage item) — 4:4:4 10-bit → BGRA swscale ≈ 37-47 ms/frame at 4K — FIXED 2026-10-18 (FIX B).** swscale rejects mid-frame slice starts ("Slices start in the middle!"), so parallel conversion now uses **strip lanes**: each lane converts its pre-offset strip as a self-contained whole frame with its own identically configured `SwsContext` (`srcSliceY=0`; `PlanSwsSlices` remainder-to-last; joined per frame). Bit-exact vs full-frame pinned on real swscale by `SwsSliceParity.*` (444p10 + 422p10, incl. lane-context reuse across frames). `SwsParallelStripsAllowed` gates parallel to sources **without vertical chroma subsampling** — 4:2:0 strips measured NOT bit-exact (both luma rows straddling every strip boundary deviate from full-frame), those formats keep the SIMD single-context path. Folded in: `SwsCacheKey` now carries width/height (stale-size context reuse on same-format stream switch — found reviewing ae806fd). Bench `SwsStripBenchmark` (opt-in `REX_BENCH_SWS=1`): 3.80× synthetic 4K 444p10 (19.78 → 5.20 ms/frame, 8 lanes). Budget now: decode ≈21 + convert ≈5-14 + upload ≈5 ≈ 31-40 ms vs the 41.7 ms 24 fps budget — live verdict pends the Oppenheimer relink test.
- [x] **P2 (audit 2026-10-17, finding #5) — shared video-fence values restarted at 1 per decoder Open — FIXED 2026-10-18.** Every decoder (global + per-monitor) signals ONE `VideoManager`-created fence through views of the same immediate context, but each kept a private counter that `Close()` reset to 0. Two failure modes: (a) after a scene-switch reopen the new session's early frames (values 1,2,3…) were already "satisfied" by the previous session's high-water mark, so the renderer could sample a slot before its copy had executed; (b) concurrent decoders could cross-release each other. Fix: `NextSharedFenceValue()` — one process-wide atomic sequence — plus `SharedFenceLock()` and `SignalSharedFenceOrdered(enqueue)`, which runs the value draw and the signal enqueue under one lock so enqueue order equals value order and the shared fence can never move backwards (a signal enqueued after a higher one would strand every waiter in between; both `ConvertAndSignal` signal sites had exactly that race shape). Tests `SharedFenceSequence.*` 2/2 — high-water survives a simulated reopen, and two threads enqueue in strictly increasing order. (`FfmpegVideoDecoder.hpp/.cpp`, both blit paths)
- [x] **P2/P3 (audit 2026-10-17, findings #2/#3/#4/#9) — Engine cross-thread state races — FIXED 2026-10-18.** (#3, stutter-relevant) the render thread read/folded `shared::Stopwatch m_stopwatch` and wrote `m_currentPosition` unguarded while Play/Pause/Stop/GetPosition/auto-pause/occlusion-park did the same under `m_mutex` — torn positions, and worse, double-banked or rewound elapsed time around pause/resume. New engine-local `PositionClock.hpp`: seqlock-published `{base, start, running}` + transition spinlock — command and render transitions serialize (a user Pause racing an occlusion park banks the lapse exactly once), render-thread readers never block and never see a mixed pair (bounded retry, conservative base-only fallback). All 11 stopwatch/base sites + `Update` go through `Start/Reset/SetBase/Fold/Position`; `m_currentPosition` is atomic. (#9) `m_frameCount`/`m_frameTime`/`m_recoveryCount`/`m_deviceResetCount` — render-thread writers read by `DumpStats` on the command thread — are relaxed atomics (14 sites). (#4) `GetCurrentScene`/`GetCurrentMonitor` copy under the lock (the render thread rewrites `m_currentScene` via recovery-LoadScene; callers are command/telemetry only → no deadlock). (#2) `SetProperty("audioMode")` dereferenced `m_videoManager` (unique_ptr swapped by render-thread recovery) outside the lock — the call now sits inside it like SetVolume/SetMute. Tests `PositionClockTest.*` 6/6 (rate math, fold/resume continuity, concurrent-fold single-bank, bounded positions under writer stress). **766/766 ctest.** (`Engine.hpp/.cpp`, `PositionClock.hpp`)
- [x] **P3 (audit 2026-10-17, findings #6/#7/#8) — Engine lifecycle guards: frame-vs-teardown, Shutdown re-entrancy, Play/Stop transitions — FIXED 2026-10-18.** (#6) `UnloadScene`/`Stop`/`LoadScene` could `Close()` the decoder + clear frames while the render thread still held the scheduler's `Tick()` frame across the dirty-gate/`BeginFrame` window (up to a frame period) — drawing/leasing a frame whose pool handles were released. Closed two ways: the frame is consumed **by value** at the Tick seam (the ring's memory is stable, but a held slot can be wrapped by the producer), and the new `m_videoEpoch` (bumped under `m_mutex` by LoadScene/UnloadScene/Stop, snapshotted with the scene, re-checked after `BeginFrame`) drops the frame **and** the cadence re-present cache for any pass that straddled a teardown; `NotifyFrameConsumed`/`presentedNew` follow the same `hasPending && framesAlive` predicate. Residual accepted (P3, med-confidence finding): a `DrawVideoFrame` already in progress when a command-thread `Close()` lands can still race handle validity — `OpenSharedHandle` on a closed handle fails cleanly; a full wait-for-render teardown handshake was judged disproportionate. (#7) `Shutdown()` had no in-progress guard — a second caller fell through into a double `StopMonitor`/`Release` + a second join of the same `std::thread` (UB). Now the first caller owns the teardown; concurrent callers and the destructor wait on the new `m_shutdownCv` (notified under `m_mutex` when the state flips Uninitialized) and report truthful success. (#8) `Play()` gained a documented transition table — rejected from Uninitialized/ShuttingDown/Error (recovery re-plays via Initialized→Stopped, so rejecting `Error` never blocks recovery itself); `Stop()` rejects Uninitialized/ShuttingDown. Tests: `EngineStressTest.ConcurrentShutdownIsSafe` (two racing Shutdown calls), `EngineTest.StopWithoutInitializeRejected`, `EngineTest.PlayStopRejectedAfterShutdown`. **769/769 ctest**; runtime relinked 2026-10-18 23:52. (`Engine.hpp/.cpp`, `tests/wallpaper/test_engine.cpp`)
- [x] **P2 (audit 2026-10-17, §5.6 renderer findings #4/#5) — HDR PSO-failure desync + DXGI adapter-enum null deref — FIXED 2026-10-19.** (#4) `SyncHdrStateLocked` cleared `m_isHdrOutput` when HDR PSO creation failed even though the OS had already made the swapchain R16F/PQ — every following frame then bound the SDR PSO + sRGB math on a PQ back buffer (PSO/RTV format mismatch + wrong transform). The failure branch now brings flag and buffers back into agreement first: force SDR on the swapchain (`SetForceSdr(true)` — the flag now *reverts* an existing R16F format instead of early-returning) + refresh, and the cached flag may only claim SDR once `IsHDR()` actually flipped (`HdrFlagAfterPsoFailure`, pinned by `HdrSyncPlanTest.PsoFailureFlagTracksRevertedSwapchain`); if the revert itself fails the flag stays HDR (matching the real buffers) and the next refresh retries the PSO. `forceSdr` stays pinned after the revert so the format cannot oscillate (each oscillation would cost two ResizeBuffers + recreated RTVs); HDR retry needs an app restart. Belt-and-suspenders: the PSO bind site can no longer bind null — HDR-flag-without-PSO falls back to the SDR pipeline with a one-shot warning. (#5) `Device::Initialize`'s adapter loop stopped only on `DXGI_ERROR_NOT_FOUND`; any other enumeration failure left a null adapter that the next line dereferenced. It now checks HRESULT + null every iteration (zero-init desc too), warns, and falls through to the default-adapter path. Hardware/DXGI paths — no unit test reachable beyond the policy pin. **770/770 ctest** (`ctest_batch3f_1018.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 00:10. (`SwapChain.cpp`, `HdrSyncPolicy.hpp`, `D3D12Renderer.cpp/.hpp`, `Device.cpp`)
- [x] **P3 (audit 2026-10-17, finding #14) — engine `m_mutex` held across seconds-long recovery/teardown work — FIXED 2026-10-19.** Two sites stalled every command/telemetry API (GetTelemetry, Stop, GetFrameSurface…) for up to ~3 s: (a) `Shutdown()` called `StopMonitor()` — joins the Explorer monitor thread, which may sit in `ReacquireWorkerW` for seconds on window-message timeouts — while holding the lock; the stop now runs outside it (state flip + `m_running=false` stay under the lock; the monitor thread's only engine touchpoint is the atomic `m_recoveryNeeded` store, and the render thread's host accesses use WorkerWManager's separate op lock, which the join deliberately does not hold). (b) `ReinitializeRenderer()` held the lock across the entire rebuild (decode-thread joins, GPU drain, host reacquire, device/swapchain creation, VideoManager init). New detach/install contract: the dying backends are moved onto locals under a brief lock, torn down and rebuilt with the lock released, and installed under a brief lock at the end; `SetupMonitorCompositors()` + the state flip stay in the install lock (ms-scale). While detached the members are null — every command-path deref null-checks (site-audited, ~50 sites), Play/LoadScene stay rejected because the state remains Error for the whole rebuild, and the new `m_rebuildInProgress` flag rejects Stop/UnloadScene (they could otherwise flip the state and let a load run against the half-built pair); a failed rebuild clears the flag and leaves the members detached for the backoff retry, which rebuilds from scratch (state Error keeps Play/LoadScene rejected until an attempt succeeds). No unit test reachable (needs real device loss). **770/770 ctest** (`ctest_unit7_1019.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 00:26. (`Engine.cpp` Shutdown/ReinitializeRenderer/Stop/UnloadScene, `Engine.hpp`)
- [x] **P2 (audit 2026-10-17, §5.4 #4; PV9/PV14 hygiene) — FrameScheduler `Clear()` lost update: a racing `Tick()` re-armed pre-seek state — FIXED 2026-10-19.** `Clear()` (command/decode thread: seek, loop wrap, teardown) and `Tick()` (render thread) raced on separate atomics: a Tick scan straddling a Clear stored its stale consumed-watermark after the Clear's re-baseline — pre-clear frames became consumable again ("un-clear") and `m_primed` was re-set, persistently defeating the decode burst gate. Fix: the consumed watermark + clear epoch are packed into ONE atomic (bits 63..40 epoch / 39..0 watermark) so the pair has a single modification order. `Tick` commits forward-only via CAS carrying the epoch it read — a Clear that landed across the scan makes `lcNow >= selected`, so the selection is **suppressed** (`Tick` returns nullptr; post-Clear frames only). `Clear()` publishes `{epoch+1, max(wc, low)}` in one CAS (low bits can never roll back). The burst gate is epoch-paired: `IsPrimed` compares the last COMMITTED consume epoch against the watermark epoch, so the epoch bump alone disarms and no separate racy primed store exists. `m_countedThrough` is now a forward-only CAS-max in both Tick and Clear. PV9: deleted the never-read `m_lastPresentedPts` + `ResetPresentationClock()` (4 controller call sites removed; the header comment that claimed a monotonic guard corrected — there is none, by design). PV14: header now documents the real SPSC lapping contract (returned pointer stays memory-valid; content guaranteed only until kNumSlots further pushes). Tests: deterministic race via a new production-null `SetTestBeforeCommitHook` seam (the audit's fault-injection pattern; RED proven with the pre-fix store restored — `PendingCount=1`, `IsPrimed=true` — GREEN deterministic after) + a concurrent stress race test. **773/773 ctest** (`ctest_unit8_1019.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 00:57. (`FrameScheduler.hpp/.cpp`, `VideoPlaybackController.cpp`, `test_frame_scheduler.cpp`, `test_frame_scheduler_selection.cpp`)
- [x] **P2 (audit 2026-10-17, §5.4 #5/#7/#8 — PV5/PV7/PV8) — playback after EOF, VideoManager teardown and per-monitor tick target — FIXED 2026-10-19.** (PV5) `Play()` could not restart a non-looping video after a natural end of stream: the restart gate compared the published position to `duration - 0.001`, but the last PTS stops one frame short of the duration, so the comparison never fired and play stayed a no-op at EOF. The decode thread now latches `m_eosReached` explicitly at `!ok && !looping` (cleared by Open/Seek/Stop — a reopen, seek or timeline reset re-arms playback) and `Play()` ORs it in via the new pure `NeedsRewindOnPlay(eosReached, position, duration)`; tests `PlayAfterEofTest.*` (3). (PV7) `VideoManager::Shutdown` was not self-contained: it reset `m_controller` unguarded against workers reaching it through `m_monitorMutex`, and left the monitor entries (compositors + per-monitor controllers — live windows and GPU objects on a torn-down device, re-attached by the next `Reinitialize`) alive. Everything owned is now torn down and cleared under `m_monitorMutex` (controllers `Close`, compositors `Shutdown`), and `Tick` takes the same mutex for the pointer hand-off (deadlock-checked: no reverse order anywhere; `PresentToAllMonitors` already held it); test `VideoManagerShutdownTest.SelfContainedTeardownIsIdempotent`. (PV8, latent) the per-monitor tick target was hardcoded `0.0` — under the strict retain policy only the first frames were ever due; it now uses the monitor video's own published position (`monitorController->GetPosition()` — its decode-paced wall clock, which can never select ahead of its playhead). No reachable unit test (no compositors in tests). **Suite note:** the first unit-9 full run showed 1/777 failed — `SpeedClockTest.PositionAdvancesAtPlaybackRate` read 0.00078 after its 1 s sleep; diagnosed (not a regression) to the engine's intended pause behavior: an ambient fullscreen/suspend edge auto-paused right after `Play()`, freezing the position at the single `Update()` publish (780 µs at rate 1.0 — exact match); the test now disables the fullscreen detector for the measurement and asserts `Running` with the possible cause named. **777/777 ctest** (`ctest_unit9_1019.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 10:47. (`VideoPlaybackController.hpp/.cpp`, `VideoManager.cpp/.hpp`, `test_frame_scheduler.cpp`, `test_video_pipeline.cpp`, `test_engine.cpp`)
- [x] **P2 (audit 2026-10-17, §5.2 #2/#3/#4/#6, #15 tie-in) — decoder pool build atomicity, NT-handle churn vs renderer cache, SW slot lease, partial-QI leak — FIXED 2026-10-19.** (D2, §5.2 #2) `EnsurePool` set `m_poolW/H/Format` and resized the vectors BEFORE creating anything and returned false mid-loop — the fast path then permanently claimed a pool whose remaining slots were null textures (every later conversion wrote through one). It now builds the full generation into temporaries (8 textures + NT handles + video processor + staging) and commits only on complete success; every failure path releases the partial generation, the previous committed pool stays intact, and a failed `CreateSharedHandle` fails the build instead of publishing a null handle. (D3/F1, §5.2 #3) one mid-session pool rebuild (stream switch / format change) retires all 8 NT handles inside one Open — the renderer's 8-entry handle cache had no eviction, so the 9th handle returned "Video slot cache exhausted" and permanently killed rendering (the handles were documented as stable per Open). `DrawVideoFrame` now stamps an LRU tick per slot and, on the 9th distinct handle, drains the queue (`WaitForGPU`) and retires the least-recently-drawn non-active slot, reusing its index (the count only extends on append). (D4, §5.2 #4 / §5.4 #3) the SW conversion path rotated a blind `m_poolNext` cursor and never stamped `m_slotFrameIndex` — it could overwrite a slot the renderer was still reading and left the lease bookkeeping stale; it now leases through `PickPoolSlot()` + stamps exactly like the HW path (`m_poolNext` deleted). (D6, §5.2 #6) the four video-interface QIs used raw locals + one early return, leaking every already-AddRef'd interface on partial failure; ComPtr locals now publish only on full success. (#15 tie-in) `PickSlotOldestSafe` is a public static and `test_pool_slot_lease.cpp` calls the real function — the drift-prone mirror copy is deleted. Evidence: new `SoftwareDecodePoolTest.SoftwarePathLeasesPoolSlots` drives the decoder directly against the in-repo fixture and pins the deterministic lease trace f0→0, f1→0, f2→1 … f8→7 — RED pre-fix (frames 1..8 rotated 1..8), GREEN post-fix. D2/D3/D6 are latent (driver-level creation failure / two pool generations through a live swapchain — no reachable unit test; verified by inspection, commit path exercised by the SW/HW decode suites). **778/778 ctest** (`ctest_decoder_1019.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 11:10. The 5 s cadence now runs as 20 × 250 ms slices that check the stop flag (same pattern as the hang watchdog), so the join is bounded by one slice. (#2) the watchdog's `try_to_lock` guards the *runtime's* `engineGuard`, not `Engine::m_mutex`, which `GetTelemetry` takes — so the "never blocks on the engine" claim was false for the one lock that matters. (#3) `ReadFrame` consumed the 4-byte prefix, classified oversized as "malformed", answered and continued — every subsequent 4 bytes (actually body payload) was read as a new length, a permanent desync on that session. `ClassifyFrameLength` now distinguishes **Empty** (zero — consumes no body, still recoverable: answer + continue per P2-17/R12) from **Oversized** (no trustworthy body boundary: log + tear the session down). Evidence: new `FrameLengthTest.ZeroLengthIsEmptyButRecoverable` / `OversizedLengthsAreUnrecoverable`; `EngineTelemetryTest.TryGetTelemetrySucceedsWhenUncontended`; live protocol smoke against the relinked exe — `[0][SHUTDOWN]` answered `malformed frame` then `ok:true` (continued), `[0xFFFFFFFF][SHUTDOWN]` logged `body boundary unrecoverable, closing session`, wrote no response and exited in 41 ms (did not process the trailing frame). Sleep slicing is thread-level (no unit seam) — verified by inspection. **779/779 ctest** (`ctest_rt_1019.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 11:38. - [x] **P2 (audit 2026-10-17, §5.8 #3/#4/#5/#6/#7/#8/#9) — compositor sync/state/slot/import hardening — FIXED 2026-10-19.** (#3) `RenderFrame` reset the allocator/list every frame with no fence on the main queue (resetting while the previous frame executed was a race) and `Present`'s result was only logged — a main-queue fence is now created with the device, every frame signals it after submission, and the next frame waits it (bounded) before reuse; `Shutdown` drains it before releasing objects; device-removed/reset/hung failures (Present, resets, closes, waits) latch `m_deviceLost` and stop rendering instead of touching a dead device. (#9) the back buffer was assumed PRESENT — a tracked per-back-buffer state (`BackBufferStateTracker`) now feeds each transition's `StateBefore` (the exit barrier leaves PRESENT, so a failed Present can no longer desync the next frame). (#4) `UpdateLayerPixels` repeated `COPY_DEST→SRV` barriers with `StateBefore = COPY_DEST` even when the texture was already `PIXEL_SHADER_RESOURCE` — entries now carry their tracked state and a pre-copy transition to `COPY_DEST` is recorded when needed. (#5) the INFINITE fence waits (layer copy, background upload) became the 2 s bounded `WaitForGpuFence` with a device-removed check (timeout skips the frame; device loss latches). (#6) replacing a layer's texture leaked its SRV slot (and so did re-`SetBackgroundFromFile`) — the old slot is recycled into the free list before the new one is taken, and `ReturnSrvSlot` dedupes double returns. (#7) `ImportLayerTexture` forced an R8G8B8A8 SRV and ignored the caller's numbers — the resource is opened first, validated (2D, single-sample, ≥1 mip, known format) and the SRV format/dimensions are derived from `GetDesc()` (`SrvFormatForResourceFormat` maps TYPELESS→canonical typed views); a caller-size mismatch only logs. (#8) shared-handle imports carry no acquire barrier — documented as a caller-owned synchronisation contract on the API (producer must have finished writing; the compositor only samples). Evidence: 9 new CPU-only tests in `test_compositor.cpp` (compile-RED `C2039/C3861` → green): present classification incl. occluded/device-lost, wait classification incl. device-lost-wins, TYPELESS→typed mapping, slot free-list/exhaustion/recycle/double-return dedupe, back-buffer tracker lifecycle. **791/791 ctest** (`ctest_gfx_1019.log`); runtime relinked 2026-10-19 12:30 (771,584 B). (`compositor.h`, `compositor.cpp`, `test_compositor.cpp`)
