- [x] **P1 - Oppenheimer Rext on-screen colours (R/B swapped) - FIXED 2026-10-19 (unit 19).** Root cause: `rex-core/src/graphics/renderer/D3D12Renderer.cpp::DrawVideoFrame` (video-slot SRV) sampled the SW BGRA pool with a component mapping that treated the fetched components as raw bytes (R<-mem2, G<-mem1, B<-mem0) - swapping red and blue for every software-decoded wallpaper (skin tones rendered blue, red indicator lamps rendered blue) while the hardware P010 path (Odyssey) stayed correct (no swizzle). D3D12 returns fetched texel components in canonical R,G,B,A order for *every* format (the same reason BGRA swap chains sample correctly), so the mapping must be the identity. Fix: new `MakeVideoSlotSrvDescriptor()` always uses `D3D12_DEFAULT_SHADER_4_COMPONENT_MAPPING`; the per-format swizzle is gone. Evidence: (a) the paused OPP frame is n339 = t=14.125 s (the prior "60.96 s dust frame" ID was a false dark-to-dark match; the pause content was derived from the runtime telemetry: ring switched to OPP's 8-slot BGRA ring at 16:48:39, `presents` frozen at 21430 at 16:48:54 -> ~278-360 frames); (b) live capture of the pre-fix build showed the swap; (c) ODY capture (P010 HW path) vs source is identity (no swap) while the OPP capture (SW path) is swapped -> localises the defect to the SW SRV path; (d) post-fix live probe (synthetic 4K yuv444p10le, bt709 limited, flat sRGB patches) renders red(253,0,0) green(0,254,0) blue(0,0,254) white(254,254,254) - channel order correct, levels essentially identity (the earlier 0.78 slope was an aliased-vs-bilinear comparison artifact, not a real wash); (e) `ColorPipeline.VideoSlotSrvKeepsCanonicalComponentOrder` new regression test (compile-RED pre-fix, GREEN post-fix); 794/794 ctest (excl. stress/soak) green; runtime relinked 2026-10-19 20:45 (772,608 B). (`D3D12Renderer.cpp/.hpp`, `test_color_pipeline.cpp`). Offline the software pipeline is verified: engine-identical decode+swscale == ffmpeg-9 CLI reference under BT.709 (601 differs); the session log confirms the fixed path (`software decode with auto threads` → `decodePath=SW_BGRA8`, BGRA pool — 10-bit SDR → 8-bit BGRA is by design). Residual suspects: renderer/present chain or the trailer's grading differing from expectation. ### New discoveries (2026-10-15, phase-first stutter mitigation — Phase A re-baseline)

> Full 10×10 elevated-PresentMon data, method, column mapping and per-run tables: [`docs/bugs/BASELINE-2026-10-15-phaseA.md`](docs/bugs/BASELINE-2026-10-15-phaseA.md). Build `fc0695d`, tree clean, 651/651 ctest. SDR-only, graceful shutdown, zero blanks.

- [x] **Phase A re-baseline — the "defect does not reproduce at 160 Hz" caution is REFUTED.** A fresh 10-run-per-clip elevated-P PresentMon sweep (obanai 4K H.264 60 fps + Aari 4K AV1 24 fps, 30 s each) measures obanai `AE_p95` as **bimodal**: `0.43, 0.52, 4.42, 4.42, 4.55, 4.61, 4.71, 4.72, 4.77, 5.46` ms. The previously recorded tight `0.53–1.71` does not occur on this box. Two clean clusters with nothing between them is the signature of the **two startup phase-lock basins** from the 2026-10-14 campaign — so the defect **is** live at 160 Hz and **Phase B2 is justified**. (The superseded caution is struck through below.)
- [x] **NEW: systematic +0.30–0.33 ms per-frame pacing bias, rate-correctable.** Both clips present slower than nominal by a near-identical absolute per-frame amount — obanai `FrameTime` p50 **16.998 ms vs 16.6667 nominal (+0.3313)**, 58.80/60 fps (**−2.00 %**); Aari **41.970 vs 41.6667 (+0.3033)**, 23.77/24 fps (**−0.97 %**). A constant per-frame overshoot predicts the deficit to within **0.05 %** / **0.25 %**: `0.3313 ms × 58.80 = 19.48 ms/s` → predicted 58.83 (measured 58.80); `0.3033 ms × 23.77 = 7.21 ms/s` → predicted 23.83 (measured 23.77). Two frame rates, two budgets, one constant additive term ⇒ **per-sleep wake-up overshoot**, not a cadence/rounding error. Prime suspect `PreciseSleepUntil`'s `CREATE_WAITABLE_TIMER_HIGH_RESOLUTION` path (`shared/PreciseSleep.hpp:44-48`, header self-documents "~0.5 ms") — **suspect, not proven**: needs a spin-only vs waitable-timer A/B. In Phase B2 scope (closed-loop-correctable, measurable, same class as phase offset).
- [x] **NEW: a WorkerW wallpaper DOES independent-flip here — contradicts a recorded inference.** `PresentMode = Hardware Composed: Independent Flip` in **20/20 runs** across both clips. This falsifies `_research_present_pacing.md:493-497` / `wall_research.md` ("a WorkerW wallpaper is *always* Composed: Flip … can never use the independent-flip path"). **This changes the servo design premise:** the wallpaper surface is promoted to a hardware overlay plane and bypasses the DWM compositor cadence, so a `DwmGetCompositionTimingInfo` phase reference would be referencing the **DWM** clock that this surface is **not** locked to. **Action: re-verify the phase-reference API before implementing B2** — for an independent-flip surface the correct reference is the display/DXGI presentation scheduler (`SyncRefreshCount`/`SyncQPCTime` via the already-fixed `SampleDisplayCadence`), not DWM. Confirm with one `PresentMon --write_display_metadata` run or an `OverlayMinFPS` A/B before committing to either.
- [x] **NEW: GPU headroom quantified per clip.** Aari `GPUTime` p95 = **85.3 ms against a 42.0 ms budget (203 %)** — confirms the driver AV1 decode-hog mechanism and **bounds Phase D's win**: the submit side is already clean (Phase C GO), so the ceiling is the residual `AE_p95` 30–36 ms, not frame loss. obanai `GPUTime` p95 = **17.11 ms against a 17.00 ms budget (101 %)** — marginal, p95 GPU work already exceeds the whole frame budget, so **the servo must add no measurable GPU work**. Also: Aari `BetwDisplay` p50 38.4 ms ≠ `BetwPresents` p50 42.0 ms (presents do **not** land 1:1 on display changes — some coalesce); obanai the two match (16.99 vs 17.00).

### New discoveries (2026-10-15, phase-first stutter mitigation — Phase D)

- [x] **Decode-ahead reserve: timing gains PROVEN, but HELD OFF — it causes a user-visible 2x-speed defect (2026-10-15).** The shipped commit `bcdfcd6` (maxBurst 2.0) was measured to regress 60 fps content, but **that regression was environmental** (62% system load). Re-run clean at <20% load, the A/B reverses:
  - **Controlled A/B** (4 runs/arm, drops+repeats as DELTAS over a 15 s window): Aari 24 fps — OFF `AE_p95` 34.04 / drops 0.1/s / effFps 23.4 / underruns 3-8 vs **ON** `AE_p95` **3.83** / drops **0.0/s** / effFps **24.0** / underruns **0**. obanai 60 fps — OFF `AE_p95` 2.74 / effFps 59.1 / underruns 24-30 vs **ON** `AE_p95` **0.61** / effFps **60.0** / underruns **0**. The gain is **not** bought with discarded frames.
  - **BLOCKER (new, user-reported): with the reserve on, the wallpaper reads as playing at ~2x.** Root cause: `FrameScheduler::Tick` selects the **newest** due frame (`FrameScheduler.cpp:51`) and its catch-up fallback returns the newest *published* frame regardless of PTS (`:64-77`). With future frames buffered, both paths make displayed **content** advance in jumps while the playhead advances at 1x. Position still reports `1.000x` and `effFps` still reports the source rate, so **no existing telemetry detects it** — this is why position/effFps measurements looked healthy while the user saw 2x. **Fix belongs in the scheduler:** consume the frame closest to the playhead (oldest due) and stop the catch-up fallback firing merely because the ring holds future frames. Burst stays at `maxBurst=1.0` until then; `REX_DECODE_AHEAD` env flag allows A/B/rollback without a rebuild.
- [x] **Phase B2 BLOCKED — no valid phase reference exists on this surface (2026-10-15).** DWM (`DwmGetCompositionTimingInfo`) measures **138.6 Hz** on a real 160 Hz panel. The DXGI alternative was then built and measured: `SyncRefreshCount` advances at **~96/s, not 160/s** — it does not report the vblank rate either. **Neither DWM nor the DXGI scheduler provides trustworthy vblank phase for a WorkerW surface that presents as `Hardware Composed: Independent Flip` (20/20 runs).** Do not build a phase servo against either. Secondary finding: the pacing servo must steer on RATE, not phase — `deadline = frameStart + interval + corr` makes them the same knob, and at 160 Hz phase-steering trades a 2% rate deficit for a 2.4–5.9% one (`PhaseServo.hpp:20-44`).
- [x] **Full-library sweep (2026-10-15, ~55 SDR clips, GPU-monitored):** typical `AE_p50` −0.02..−0.19 ms (centred) and `AE_p95` 0.6–8.8 ms. Weakest: hall-of-legends-ahri (vp9) 8.79 / effFps 54.7, boa-hancock 8.72, blue-haired-blind-girl (vp9) 8.47 / effFps 39.9, mercy-overwatch-2 (hevc) 8.36 / effFps 47.1. GPU 3D utilisation 4.6–13.3% throughout. **`VDec` reads 0.0% on every clip — the AMD video-decode engine counter does not attribute on this GPU and is unusable as evidence.** Results are indicative only: system load varied 19–62% with `AMDRSServ` and `SmartConnect` active, so the numbers are NOT a clean bill of health and must be re-taken under controlled load.
- [x] **Speed persistence traced (2026-10-15):** `WallpaperLibrary` (`app/electron/wallpaper-library.cjs`) persists **no** playback fields at all — no `speed`, `volume`, `muted`. The 15 library entries carrying none of those keys is therefore expected, not evidence of a missing schema. The speed chain is `inspector PlaybackSection → updateWallpaperProperty → desktop.js:66 → IPC → SET_SPEED`. **The observed 2x is NOT a persisted setting** — it is the scheduler defect above. No schema change warranted.

- [x] **Decode-ahead reserve SHIPPED — measured ~7x reduction in displayed animation error on Aari (2026-10-15).** Built in three verified pieces, each committed separately:
  - **(a) Pool slot leasing** (`PickPoolSlot`): the decode thread no longer allocates pool slots by blind round-robin. Each slot records the decode-order `frameIndex` it holds; allocation picks the oldest slot ≥3 frames behind the render thread's consumed watermark (published by `Engine` right after `EndFrame`, wired `Engine` → `VideoManager` → `VideoPlaybackController` → decoder). This removes the *lifetime* hazard the fence cannot cover — a consumer that already waited on fence N would otherwise sample NEW pixels under the OLD frame identity. Degrades to strict-oldest when the pool saturates. 5 unit tests.
  - **(b) Reserve-paced decode** (`CadenceController::DecodeAheadPolicy`): the decoder thread now paces by reserve depth instead of a flat source-rate interval. Below the 100 ms low-water mark it bursts (up to 2×) to refill; at/above it, it returns to the source rate. Pure function, 5 unit tests including "never decodes slower than realtime" and the slot-cap arithmetic.
  - **(c) Honest telemetry**: `reserve=<ms> ahead=<x> under=<n>` now in the text line, plus `reserveMs` / `decodeAheadRate` / `reserveUnderruns` through `GET_STATUS`. Reports the **measured** depth, never the 200 ms nominal — the 8-slot pool caps the achievable reserve at ~291 ms for 24 fps but only ~58 ms for 120 fps, and telemetry must not overstate it.
  - **Measured result (Aari, 4K AV1, 24 fps, 160 Hz, elevated PresentMon):** `AE_p95` median **32.4 ms → 4.69 ms** (~7× better; baseline range 30.1–35.9 over 10 runs, post-change 1.59–23.47 over 5). `FT_max` tightened 42–216 ms → 42–98 ms. `GPU_max` ~107 → 56–104 ms. Live reserve holds **125 ms** (3 frames) above the low-water mark with **0 underruns** and decoder at 1.00–1.03×. 656/656 ctest.
  - **Caveat recorded:** an early sample showed `convFps=0.1` and looked like a regression — it was AV1 warm-up sampled too early, not a stall. Confirm long-run convergence before treating any single early telemetry line as a fault.
- [x] **Decode-ahead refill burst RE-ENABLED by default (`maxBurst=2.0`) — hold-off precondition resolved (2026-10-17).** The Phase D blocker (the 2x-speed defect) was fixed by `e5bfdf0` (Tick retains future frames; starvation returns nullptr; render loop re-presents — the documented precondition). A/B re-run today on the current build (harness `sustain-rt.cjs`, instrumented + clean): **Aari 24 fps** OFF `res -6 ms` / `jit 0.70` / dwell max 16 vbl / under 0 vs **ON** `res +122..163 ms` / `jit 0.27-0.32` / dwell max 6 / under 0; **obanai 60 fps** OFF `jit 5.09` / under 11 vs **ON** `jit 2.42` / under 4 (clean steady windows 0.2-0.3 ms); **astral 60 fps** ON steady `res +57..62 ms` / `jit 0.22-0.26` / under 0. Content meter read **1.00x in every ON run** — the exact 2x symptom the hold-off existed to prevent. Env semantics now: unset = default ON; `REX_DECODE_AHEAD=1` = rollback; `>1` = custom multiplier. Default-path verify (no env): Aari `res +120 ms`, under 0, content 1.00x. 733/733 ctest. (A/B recorded in `wall_research.md` P15.)
- [x] **Decode-ahead negative-reserve ABSORBING STATE — FIXED (2026-10-17). Root cause of the intermittent long stutter ("sometimes not everytime").** The 2026-10-16 one-frame hysteresis in `DecodeIntervalMs` mapped deficits of (−sourceInterval, 0) to 1x decode — behind the playhead 1x equals the playhead speed, so the deficit can never shrink, and the rescue-burst also stopped one frame short of zero. Every load enters negative (measured entries −5/−18/−40 ms across three sessions), so an unlucky session pinned negative for minutes: no jitter buffer → late-frame storms (waitable blocks 12–21 ms, gap 55.6 ms vs 41.67 target), dwell smear 4–17 vblanks, underrun growth — until an external pause/resume reset escaped (explains the "sometimes", and why a fullscreen window toggle "healed" it: auto-pause/resume rebuilt the reserve to +118/+129). Fix: every negative reserve bursts (`CadenceController.hpp:134-149`); rollback unchanged (`catchUpBurst=1.0`). Tests: `ShallowDeficitsCatchUp` + `NegativeReserveRecoversToLowWater` (fixed-point pacing sim from the measured −40 ms entry), RED→GREEN 19/19 (`test_cadence_controller.cpp`). Live-verified (user, 3–4 app relaunches + rapid-churn applies): zero negative-reserve lines since rebuild; Aari settles +118..167 ms mid-band; mid-play dip at res=61 recovered via 2.07x burst in ≤5 s (was: pinned forever); 120 fps content unchanged (+21..27 ms, under 0). Residual: sub-second catch-up on cold apply (split-second hiccup) — optional future entry-side prewarm gate, not a bug.
- [x] **Startup storm on 60 fps LOADS — NOT REPRODUCED on the current build; closed (2026-10-17).** Two fresh instrumented runs (astral 26 s, obanai 11 s; `REX_SCHED_DEBUG=1`, default config, burst default ON) produced **zero starves from PLAY through the loop wrap** and exact-1x positions (`t=10s pos=10.0`, `t=20s pos=20.0`). The earlier ~2-9 "startup drops" came from run windows that also contained wraps and system-load stalls (the environmental class below); no engine startup defect exists to fix.
- [x] **Wrap-boundary cost — measured healthy; the isolated starvation window was environmental (2026-10-17).** Instrumented wrap forensics (stage ring 256→2048 ≈ full-run CSV coverage + loop-seek timing log): demux EOF at the true stream end, loop seek **0.1 ms** (warm cache), EOF→first post-wrap frame ≈ 35 ms (full re-prime gap 73 ms in one row), post-wrap re-present ≈ 4 vblanks (~33 ms) then exact 1x; decode rows content 18.7-21.1 s steady 16.6-17.0 ms (whole-run histogram: 2018 rows 16-17 ms, 5 at 8 ms refill burst, 2 at 15 ms, one 73 ms wrap - nothing in between). The once-seen 8-starve / ~0.9 s pre-wrap drain (11:30:51) did not reproduce at 11:40/11:43 with identical content+config and belongs to the external system-load stall class (same signature as the 11:12 obanai +19/+18 burst); refill from any drain measured at a true 2x (7.7 ms cycles ≈ 130 fps). Final verification: astral 22 s crossing a wrap — **0 starves, 1 wrap, exact 1x**. Diagnostics kept permanent (2048-row ring, seek-duration log).
- [ ] **120 fps adaptive cap (Phase E, not started):** preserve real-time timeline and audio sync; sustained decode below source fps must be handled by controlled late-frame drops, never a growing backlog, and must report actual throughput. Measured ceiling today is 116.7 fps for 120 fps content (miles, hardware-bound).

- [x] **FIXED (2026-10-15): display-cadence sampler was dead BY CONSTRUCTION, and it falsified one of our own "dead route" conclusions.** `SwapChain::SampleDisplayCadence` fed DXGI's **cumulative** `PresentRefreshCount` into `DisplayCadenceStats::Sample::presentRefreshCount` (documented as "vblanks this frame occupied"), and gated it at `<= kMaxPlausibleRefreshes (64)`. Because the raw counter is a running total since swap-chain creation, it exceeds 64 within **400 ms at 160 Hz** — so the gate was closed permanently on every machine, and `dwell` telemetry was always `0/0/0/0`. **Two further defects in the same gate:** (1) `PresentCount == SyncRefreshCount` compared *images-reached-monitor* against *v-blank counters* — different units, so it could never be the intended test; (2) `DXGI_ERROR_FRAME_STATISTICS_DISJOINT` (documented recoverable — "an event, for example a power cycle, interrupted the gathering") was a bare `return false` with no re-baseline, so one power cycle killed the stream until the swap chain was recreated. Fix: track a baseline and publish the true per-frame delta, apply the `<= 64` plausibility bound to the **delta**, drop the unit conflation, and re-baseline on DISJOINT. **Verified live: `dwell=1/2/3/3 vblanks`** (was `0/0/0/0`). **CORRECTION TO `wall_research.md` P11:** the claim that base `GetFrameStatistics` "never converges" (`PresentCount=2, PresentRefreshCount=2087421`) was a **misreading of this bug**, not an environmental property — those values are exactly what a large cumulative counter looks like on a 2-present-deep swap chain. This retires one of the five "failed routes" that had blocked pacing work all session. 646/646 ctest.
- [ ] **Startup phase servo (Phase B2, not started):** `PaceLoop` (`Engine.cpp:2113-2164`) anchors `deadline = frameStart + interval` — a fixed, drift-uncorrected grid with **zero vblank awareness** (no `DwmFlush`/`WaitForVBlank`/scanline query exists anywhere in `rex-core/`). Researched candidate for a phase reference: **`DwmGetCompositionTimingInfo(nullptr, &ti)`** — supplies `qpcRefreshPeriod` (fractional, unlike the integer `m_displayHzCached` from `dmDisplayFrequency`), `qpcVBlank`, non-blocking, HDR-independent, safe at 240 Hz; `dwmapi` is **already linked** (`CMakeLists.txt:255,332,356,712`). `hwnd` must be **NULL** on Win 8.1+ (passing the WorkerW HWND returns `E_INVALIDARG`); the clock is primary-monitor-bound, which matches DXGI's own present cadence. **Rejected:** `DwmFlush` (drain barrier, not a vblank clock, blocks the render thread), `IDXGIOutput::WaitForVBlank` (deprecated, blocks, wrong cadence), `DwmQueryUnwaivedSwapChainInfo` (**does not exist** — no SDK declaration, MS Learn 404), `GetFrameStatisticsMedia` (permanent `NOT_CURRENTLY_AVAILABLE` on external panels by design).
  - ~~**CAUTION from Phase A baseline (2026-10-15): at 160 Hz, obanai-class playback is already well-locked (`AE_p50` -0.04..+0.02, `AE_p95` 0.53-1.71 over 10 runs) … A servo must not be built for a defect that does not reproduce.**~~ **REFUTED 2026-10-15 by a fresh 10-run sweep — see the Phase A re-baseline section above: `AE_p95` is bimodal `0.43…5.46 ms`, so the defect DOES reproduce at 160 Hz. B2 is justified. This caution was wrong; do not cite it.**
  - **BLOCKER — RESOLVED 2026-10-15 BY MEASUREMENT: `DwmGetCompositionTimingInfo` is the WRONG clock on this box; use the DXGI presentation scheduler instead.** A standalone probe sampling `DwmGetCompositionTimingInfo(nullptr, …)` (24 samples, `qpc*` fields converted via `QueryPerformanceFrequency`) while REX plays obanai reports `qpcRefreshPeriod` = **7.2158 ms = 138.585 Hz, constant 24/24** against the panel's true **6.2500 ms / 160 Hz** — a **15.45 % period error** (`rateRefresh=10000000/72158` confirms) — with `qpcVBlank == qpcCompose` **exactly, 24/24**, i.e. DWM runs **no distinct composition pass** for this surface. A servo locked to it would drift ~15.5 ms per second. Idle it oscillates 160↔138.6 Hz. **B2 must use `SyncRefreshCount` + `SyncQPCTime` from `IDXGISwapChain::GetFrameStatistics`** — per-swapchain, references exactly the scheduler that quantises our `Present(1,0)`, HDR-independent, non-blocking, every frame, and yields both period (`ΔQPC/Δrefresh`) and phase (`SyncQPCTime mod period`) from one call. Already implemented and working after `9437032` (verified `dwell=1/2/3/3`). Evidence: `docs/bugs/BASELINE-2026-10-15-phaseA.md` Finding 5. **The API contract research was right (hwnd must be NULL, non-blocking, HDR-independent); the clock choice was wrong for this surface on this hardware.**
  - **Also newly in scope:** the +0.30–0.33 ms per-frame pacing bias (Phase A re-baseline) is a second closed-loop-correctable error term alongside phase offset.
- [x] **§5 GATE EXPERIMENT — VERDICT: GO (2026-10-15, Phase C).** Measured on Aari (4K AV1, 24 fps, 160 Hz) over a 22 s elevated-PresentMon capture: **521 presents = 23.7/s vs 24/s source (98.75% complete)**; submit `FrameTime` p50 41.95 / max **42.69 ms** (tight — no submit stalls at all); GPU `GPUTime` p50 38.88 / p95 85.39 / **max 111.36 ms** (the ~107 ms AV1 hog, every run); REX render-thread phases `phBegin` 0.04–0.10 ms, `phDraw` 0.16–0.28 ms, `phWork` 0.17–0.86 ms, and **`phWait` = 0.00 ms in every sample**. **Conclusion: rendering and presentation continue on schedule through the AV1 decoder stalls** — the frame-latency waitable never blocks and the video decode engine and the 3D/present path overlap. This clears the pre-registered condition for Phase D. Mechanism Phase D relies on: **decode-ahead converts "GPU hogged during playback" into "GPU hogged during a pre-roll burst, then free during playback."** Note the submit side was already clean, so Phase D's win is bounded by the residual `AE_p95` 30–36 ms, not by frame loss.

### New discoveries (2026-10-14, stutter root-cause campaign — MILESTONE CLOSED)

> Elevated PresentMon (`-Verb RunAs`) was the unlock: it exposes `GPUTime`, `DisplayLatency`, `AnimationError`, `CPUBusy`. `DisplayedTime` remains UNUSABLE (196/474 deltas non-positive on Aari even elevated) — **retired, never cite it**. Safety: user hit a monitor signal-loss blank during HDR↔SDR cycling + force-kill; VBR now off, SDR-only + graceful-stop protocol used for all 30+ capture runs since, zero blanks.

- [x] **Video stutter root-caused (FOUND 2026-10-11; CLOSED 2026-10-14 after 4 display-rate experiments + 2 10× repeatability runs + per-frame correlation + a temporary decode probe).** THREE SEPARATE mechanisms, none of them cadence:
  - **(1) Driver AV1 decode hogs (Aari, 4K AV1/24fps).** 22 GPU hogs in 15 s, 80–145 ms each, on a strict **~16-frame (0.671 s) period**, with victims +28–34 ms one frame later. Temporary env-gated per-frame probe over 936 frames proved the REX path innocent: decode path mean **0.607 ms**, zero frames >5 ms, all 34 keyframes <1 ms. CPU-busy flat, `phDraw`/`phWork` ~0.3 ms. Cause is inside D3D11VA AV1 decode execution on RX 6700 XT. **NOT a REX defect** — mitigation is transcode/lower-fps, not engine work.
  - **(2) Startup phase-lock + vblank-deadline slips (obanai etc.).** 10× repeatability showed AE p95 varying 0.61→2.83→6.94→10.34→8.36 across identical runs. Model: each playback locks submit phase onto the refresh grid at startup; mid-window lock (AE p50 ≈ +0.1) leaves margin → clean run (0.5–0.7); lock 1 ms before deadline (AE p50 ≈ −1.0) → **no margin → slips every frame for the whole run (7.8–8.6, stable)**. Per-frame correlation: AE top-5% frames had **identical** mean GPU (13.23 vs 13.65 ms) and submit to the rest — **GPU saturation DISPROVED as the cause**. Loop-wrap rejected by controlled experiment (no-wrap 7.77/7.85/7.76 vs wrap 8.00/7.84/7.91 — identical). **PROPOSED, NOT IMPLEMENTED (design decision, owner call): servo submit phase to mid-window at startup.**
  - **(3) One 384 ms CPU stall (miles, 120fps) → 401 ms DisplayLatency.** Single sighting; 0 recurrences in 9 subsequent captures. Parked as a one-off OS/driver event — unassignable without recurrence.
  - **RETIRED HYPOTHESES (do not re-investigate):** 7/7/6 vblank cadence retiming — **falsified**: Aari at 120 Hz has exactly 5.0 vblanks/frame (the cleanest possible ratio) yet still measures AE p95 32.5. Cadence ratio alone does NOT explain stutter. Also refuted: AV1-inherent (30 fps H.264/HEVC judder equally), keyframe cost (keyframes all <1 ms), low-FPS-as-such (24→60 and 24→120 both bad), per-region refresh difference (**architecturally impossible** — single fullscreen quad, `D3D12Renderer.cpp:1272`, video/static textures mutually exclusive at `:1194-1208`; multi-layer compositor + per-layer scheduler are compiled but never instantiated).
  - Also delivered this campaign: drop telemetry `dropRate` (10 s window) + `dropPeak` (largest burst) — validated on a real 13-drop burst that the average had smoothed to `0.0/s`; `ColorPipeline` test suite (colour-description consistency + P010-for-HDR pool rule, 643/643 green).
- [ ] **FFmpeg decode-path defects found by the audit (FOUND 2026-10-11, `wall_research.md` §3; all real, but NONE of them caused the 2026-10-14 stutter — see campaign above). `thread_count` item is FIXED, rest open:**
  - ~~**`thread_count = 1` hardcoded**~~ — **FIXED 2026-10-10/11**: software decode now uses auto threads (hardware stays `thread_count=1`, `thread_type=0`). Benchmarked 3.23× on H.264 4K30 (114.7 → 370.1 fps). Commit `a1e1480`.
  - **`hw_frames_ctx` never set and no `get_format` callback** (`:198`) — REX never owns the D3D11VA surface pool and cannot answer "can this adapter decode this profile?" mpv uses `avcodec_get_hw_frames_parameters` + `av_hwframe_ctx_init` with a sized `initial_pool_size`.
  - **One `CopySubresourceRegion` per frame** (`:890`) — a direct consequence of the above; mpv's direct hwdec path is zero-copy. 4K NV12 ≈ 746 MB/s of GPU copy at 60 fps.
  - **`ID3D11DeviceContext::Flush()` on every frame** (`:896,1019`) — destroys FFmpeg's D3D11VA command batching; the signal does need a flush, but not one per frame.
  - **Pool slot recycled with no fence wait** (`:881-882,983-984`) — real race if the D3D12 consumer falls >133 ms behind. Also `FfmpegVideoDecoder::kPoolSize == 8` and `FrameScheduler::kNumSlots == 8` are *different* rings kept in lockstep by coincidence, not by construction.
  - **Dead `ID3D11VideoProcessor`** created at `:802-823`, never referenced ⇒ `vpMsP50/P95/P99` telemetry is **always 0** and reads as "the VP is fast". Delete it or wire it.
  - **`has_b_frames` never read** — reorder happens correctly inside FFmpeg, but REX cannot reason about startup/flush latency. mpv exposes it (`VDCTRL_GET_BFRAMES`).
  - **Explicitly NOT bugs (do not "fix"):** `AV_CODEC_FLAG_LOW_DELAY` unset (mpv also doesn't set it; would break PTS-driven selection), `best_effort_timestamp` use (**better** than mpv's raw `pts`), `IsBackwardJump` thresholds (already B-frame-aware and correct).
- [ ] **Present-pacing research gaps (2026-10-11, `_research_present_pacing.md` Appendix B):** 10 items NOT VERIFIED — notably (i) runtime behaviour of `FRAME_LATENCY_WAITABLE_OBJECT` when never waited on (upstream docs silent; **moot for REX** — `D3D12Renderer.cpp:1141` does wait), (ii) whether one app's present rate changes the VRR interval another app receives on the same output, (iii) Chromium `viz` `FrameRateMatcher` cadence algorithm (source path 404s on current `main`). StackOverflow 64328187 and Chromium issue bodies 360354541 / 40890504 are sign-in-gated or 403 — cited by URL only, content not relied on.

### New discoveries (2026-10-10, FPS telemetry audit)

- [ ] **FFmpeg 8.1.2 CBS-AV1 assert kills the runtime on blk4K (FOUND 2026-10-10; BISECTED 2026-10-10, P0.4 — owner: parallel audio track):** PLAY of AV1+HDR+AAC file dies at variable <1–5s with `cbs.c:597 width>0&&width<=32`, exit 0xC0000005, HW+SW. Isolation matrix on final code: original+audio crashes 5/5 · remux+audio crashes · remux audio-free plays 12s clean (dec 59/out 58) · sysffmpeg 9.0.1 clean · Oct-9/Aug binaries clean. My Engine hunks exonerated (stash-rebuild still crashes 2/2); loopWraps fetch_add never executes pre-wrap. Prime suspect: f8d895f audio-thread changes (MMCSS + Start/Stop) — supported by same-tree WER `c0000374` heap loop (12×/~100s) + `MonitorAudioTest` fastfail (untouched per safeguards). Sibling: Dolby-Vision entry below (second file, same parser family — suggest they also try an audio-stripped remux). Workaround stands: blk4k out, mindblow covers AV1-HDR. Evidence: `%TEMP%\opencode\rex-verify\` (`blk4k_noaudio.mp4` clean / `blk4k_remux.mp4` crashes).
- [ ] **120fps steady-state shortfall ~4% (FOUND 2026-10-10, Ralph FPS loop; DIAGNOSED 2026-10-10, needs owner call):** skyscraper 1440p120 split on the canonical binary (wrap isolated): decode 118.0/s → consume 117.7–117.9/s → presented 116.3–116.6/s vs 120 source. Loss attribution: ~2.0/s VCN throughput + ~0.3/s scheduler skips + ~1.2/s present/cadence; nothing saturated (Video 58.8% / 3D 22% / CPU 7%). Verdict: distributed small losses, no single fixable bottleneck — 116.6 (97.1%) is the measured platform ceiling on the RX 6700 XT (1 fps under the 117.6 bar; chasing it means silicon gains, out of scope). See `fps.md` finding 5.
- [x] **Frozen video frame covers newly applied static image (FOUND 2026-10-10, user report; FIXED 2026-10-10, P0.1):** root cause was the missing hide transition — render window shown on acquire, no `SW_HIDE` anywhere, dark-clear requires Running. Fix: `IDesktopHost::SetVisible` pure virtual + WorkerW/Progman/MultiMonitor impls (window-thread affine) + hide on `UnloadScene` + show on any `LoadScene`; `Stop` keeps the snap (user decision). Proven live: video visible=True → image LOAD visible=False → PLAY-after-image ok:false (expected) → video reload visible=True → STOP visible=True. Host tests 13/13 (incl. live hide/show round-trip), rex-core-tests 41/41, wallpaper suite 184 + 2 env-skips (1 foreign audio crasher from parallel MMCSS track, untouched). Bonus fix in same unit: `wallpaper:set` no longer fails persistence when post-image PLAY reports empty scene. Flowchart: `docs/wallpaper-engine-architecture.md` §8.
- [ ] **Supervisor wiring (P0.2, code complete + headless-proven 2026-10-10, LIVE verification pending binary link):** `supervisor.cjs` wired through `main.cjs` as the single exit-recovery driver (old inline `recoverRuntime` removed — no double-reapply); renderer watchdog guarded until first ping (would otherwise reload-loop); heartbeat parked (`missLimit: Infinity`) until native PING lands; `resetRecovery()` on deliberate applies preserves un-park semantics; `supervisor.stop()` on will-quit. Proven headless 12/12 against REAL classes with a fake native (exit→reapply→restore, 3-fails→park+gave-up, quit-blocks, no-ping→no-reload, ping-then-stale→reload, hung→stop→recover, parked-heartbeat→untouched). Native `PING` command added (`cli_main`, ok:true, side-effect-free) + arch IPC count 21→22. Remaining: link blocked by 3 elevated orphans (need reboot/admin kill) → then live intentional-crash + unexpected-exit test in the real app.
- [ ] **App-state + gallery persistence wiring (P0.3, code complete + headless-proven 2026-10-10, LIVE in-app verification pending):** `AppStateManager` + `WallpaperLibrary` instantiated in `whenReady` (library self-registers `gallery.*`); `app-state:get/set` + `gallery.read-file` (atomic, 150MB cap, `not-found`/`too-large` signals) handlers; preload `appState` + `gallery` bridges; new renderer `gallery.js` (wrappers + `buildFileRecord` shared by import + restore + `galleryRestoreFile`); gallery import persists paths, persisted-path dedup authoritative in library (`gallery.get` check; `splitNewFiles` stays as same-session exact-bytes preflight; edited-file re-import bumps `lastUsed`, no duplicate); startup restore effect rebuilds Files (cap 100, missing skipped-never-pruned); removal drops library entry. Proven headless 20/20 vs REAL classes (round-trip, backups, corrupt+quarantine recovery, restart persistence, case-insensitive dedup, preload channel names) + `vite build` 1.49s + lint clean (2 pre-existing warnings only). Remaining: live import→restart→duplicate→remove→missing-file pass in the real app.
- [ ] **Occlusion pause — engine burns full video cost while wallpaper is hidden (FOUND 2026-10-10, hardware-usage research; SHIPPED as safety net 2026-10-10, needs owner call on scope):** occluded presents return `DXGI_STATUS_OCCLUDED`, which `EndFrame` treated as no-op success with log throttling only. Measured visible cost of HEVC 4K60: 4.2% CPU / 13.2% 3D / 54% VCN-codec. Shipped (TDD 16/16 `test_occlusion_policy.cpp`, suite 220 green): per-swapchain `OcclusionTracker` (only the documented DXGI code counts; device failures never park) + `AllPathsOccluded` aggregation + `OcclusionPolicy` (1s threshold, disarmed-until-LOAD/Play grace, atomics for command/render threads) + Engine park/probe/resume (Pause/fold/Play mirror of AutoPause, state stays Running, 4Hz probe, Reset/Arm on all playback transitions with auto-pause ownership handoff) + `occlusionParked` in GET_STATUS. Vendor-neutral by construction (one DXGI HRESULT by value, no vendor APIs; silent no-op where OCCLUDED never fires). CORRECTED SCOPE 2026-10-10 live A/B: a maximized window does NOT trigger it — DXGI occlusion needs FULL coverage and the taskbar strip breaks it (30s maximized notepad: zero OCCLUDED lines, parked=false throughout, behavior otherwise nominal). Real triggers are whole-desktop-hidden cases (lock screen, UAC, RDP-minimized, fullscreen-Start) — largely overlapping the suspend/auto-pause lattice, so this is a safety net, not the maximized-window saver. Park action itself is still unwitnessed live (needs a lock-screen test); maximized-window coverage would need a foreground-rect trigger (separate design, not started). Harness lesson: `Start-Job` runtimes die at bash-call shell teardown (two false "crash" alarms tonight, zero WER) — single-call orchestration only (`occ/occ_ab.cjs` pattern).

- [x] **Launch-at-login missing (FOUND 2026-10-08, strategy review; FIXED 2026-10-08, Ralph-1):** the app supports --background launch and replays the last wallpaper after crashes, and per-monitor assignments persist EDID-keyed - but nothing registers REX for Windows startup (no setLoginItemSettings, Run key, or Startup shortcut). Fixed: file-less wallpapers send no bridge call (`desktop.js` early return, proven 0 calls headless); boot re-apply re-saves to disk (self-healing). Needs one real apply to re-seed persistence.]**
- [x] **UI advertises unimplemented wallpaper types (FOUND 2026-10-08, strategy review; FIXED 2026-10-08, T1-T5):** gallery classified/selected WEB/SHADER/SCENE/AUDIO and applying any of them silently STOPPED the current wallpaper (destructive no-op). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-08-ui-honesty-supported-types.md` — ordered steps: (1) `SUPPORTED_WALLPAPER_TYPES` + `isSupportedWallpaperType()` in `registry.js`; (2) picker accept → image/video only; (3) tiles show Coming later, clicks never apply; (4) `loadWallpaperFile` returns null for unsupported; (5) apply path warns instead of STOP; (6) verify (`pnpm lint`, `vite build`, 5-row manual matrix), record, commit. No engine/protocol/deps changes. Verified 2026-10-08: `pnpm lint` clean, `vite build` 5.71s, headless node matrix (bridge-call counts + loader refusals) all green.

- [x] **Silent-success LOAD trap (FOUND 2026-10-08, engine+UI survey; FIXED 2026-10-08, Ralph-2):** LOAD with type web/shader/scene/audio-file returns ok:true yet presents dark clear color (falls into else -> UnsupportedAdapter; only image/static get branches). The UI lets users select all 6 types but apply silently stops for 4 of them. Fix: reject unknown types with ok:false unsupported-type (and gate the UI apply path). Fix: skip incoming files matching an existing record (name+size+lastModified) and select the existing wallpaper instead of re-importing. **[FIXED: `splitNewFiles`/`fileIdentity` in `registry.js` (4/4 node assertions: dupe skipped, edited file imports, same-batch deduped, empty-existing); `handleFiles` imports fresh-only and selects the existing wallpaper when all-dupes. `pnpm lint` clean, vite build ok]**
- [x] **Login item launched bare Electron in dev (FOUND 2026-10-09, user report; FIXED 2026-10-09):** the Run entry was `"...electron.exe" --background` with no app path, so Windows booted a default Electron info window instead of REX. Fix: dev-aware registration — packaged keeps `args: [--background]`, dev passes `path: execPath + args: [appPath, --background]` via new `applyLoginItem()` used by boot/IPC/tray. Verified: descriptor shapes + stub-harness boot call (7/7 green). Heals on next app start (re-registers over the broken entry).
- [x] **Engine Tile fill mode rejected while advertised (FOUND 2026-10-08, survey; FIXED 2026-10-08, Ralph-3):** ParseFillMode accepted tile and the CLI advertised it, but Engine::SetFillMode rejected Tile-not-implemented and ComputeFillViewport no-oped it into silent Stretch (LoadScene pushed scene.fillMode straight to ApplyFillMode, bypassing the SetFillMode guard). Fix (refuse, not implement — true tiling needs quad/instancing work the spec never gave; OS Path A tiles natively via WallpaperStyle): `Engine::LoadScene` rejects Tile with InvalidArgument before state mutation. Verified: new `EngineTest.LoadTileFillModeFailsLoudly` (RED: loaded silently; GREEN after); neighbors green; live protocol LOAD video+mode=tile pending (user app holds the layer).
- [ ] **Video covers newly applied static image (FOUND 2026-10-09, user-verified; FIXED pending live re-test):** applying an image while a video ran showed the image ~1s, then the last video came back over it (`wallpaper:set` logged 19:54:35/47, no later video IPC — resurrection came from inside the runtime). Root cause: image LOAD skipped `UnloadScene` unless state was Running/Paused, so after a stall (Error) the video backend survived and recovery reloaded it; plus a pending recovery request could race the unload. Fix: unconditional `UnloadScene` on the image path + disarm recovery/backoff inside `UnloadScene`. Needs: rebuild + restart + user re-test (video → image → 10s watch).
- [ ] **Wallpaper video audio silent (FOUND 2026-10-10, user report; FIXED pending ears test):** video played unmuted with WASAPI endpoint ready, thread rendering, zero errors — yet no sound. Root cause: `IAudioClient::Start()` was never called anywhere (AudioCapture calls it; the render player didn't), so the engine never pulled buffers. Fix: ThreadLoop owns Start/Stop (starts on play, stops on pause/endpoint-loss/exit; Start failure re-enumerates). Verified: Debug builds, 11/11 audio/mute/volume tests green. Needs: Release rebuild + restart + user listens.
- [ ] **Wallpaper audio crackle (FOUND 2026-10-10, user report: constant crackle on laptop speakers; FIXED pending ears test):** conversion clamps correctly and the file is plain AAC stereo, so the crackle is deadline misses, not clipping — the render thread had no MMCSS priority (its capture sibling does) and got preempted by 4K decode past the ~20 ms buffer deadlines. Fix: `AvSetMmThreadCharacteristics(L"Audio")` + revert in `ThreadLoop` (valid task-index var per the AVRT contract). Verified: Debug builds, 8/8 audio tests green, Release rebuilt + app restarted. Needs: user listens.
- [ ] **Heap crash on Dolby-Vision AV1 file (FOUND 2026-10-10, crash loop; PROTECTED, fix upstream):** `Mind Blowing OLED Dolby Vision - 4K HDR 240fps.mp4` (actually AV1 59.94fps + dvcC box, HW d3d11va P010 3840x2176) kills the runtime ~20s into PLAY — 0xc0000374 heap + 0xC0000005 AV at 0x639C68E5, crash loop every ~25s until recovery parks. Same class as the parallel track's CBS-AV1 assert finding (blk4k, upstream ffmpeg 8.1.2) — second file confirming the parser family, not one bad file. No REX-side fix (upstream); protection: persistence repointed to ahri SDR (safe), user must not apply the Dolby file until upstream lands. Repro: headless INIT/LOAD/PLAY, dies before GET_STATUS (~25s). See everything.md

Fix: thread the real path library → record (`buildFileRecord(f, pathHint)`, `galleryRestoreFile` returns path) → `loadWallpaperFile(file, pathHint)`; string inputs resolve too. Verified headless 4/4 (hint resolves, live path kept, string resolves, deferred refused); lint/build clean. Needs: reopen app, click a restored tile.
- [x] **🐛 FIXED (2026-10-10) Switching away from a PLAYING video crashed the runtime (heap corruption):** root cause was a **heap buffer overflow in the audio ring buffer** (`AudioPlayer.cpp`) — NOT a render/GPU race. The ring is circular (`m_writePos`/`m_readPos` advance with `% m_ringFrames`), but `PushFrameToRing()` indexed it linearly: the full-guard checked only `m_bufferedFrames + frames > m_ringFrames`, never `m_writePos + frames > m_ringFrames`. Once the consumer advanced `m_readPos` past 0 — i.e. as soon as audio actually played — the producer wrote past the end of `m_ring`, corrupting the process heap (`0xC0000374`), which surfaced later at an unrelated `free()`. `RenderStep()`'s normal-speed path had the mirror-image out-of-bounds read; the speed path already wrapped correctly, which is why only playback triggered it.
  - **Isolation (decisive):** same codec H.264, only the audio track differs — `ghost-cod` (no audio) survived 3/3 while `spiderman` (audio) crashed 3/3; never sending PLAY exits 0. Two prior hypotheses tested and REJECTED first: render/teardown CPU race on the shared fence (shared_mutex barrier, still crashed 4/4) and GPU wait ordering (`UnloadScene` closes the video manager before `ClearVideoFrame()`'s `WaitForGPU()`; hoisting it still crashed 4/4). Both reverted.
  - **Fix:** S16 fast path, generic per-sample loop, and the consumer's normal-speed copy all split at the ring wrap.
  - **Verified:** 600/600 ctest green; repro 6/6 clean exit (video→image and video→video across two audio files). Regression caught in-flight: my first version added `+ done` to the ring pointer (valid only for the linear WASAPI destination) and segfaulted `MonitorAudioTest.SecondMonitorVideoStartsMuted` — corrected to `wp*ringCh` only.
  - May also explain the reported "rub-rub" distortion (the overflow produced garbage samples) — needs the user's ears to confirm.
  - Crash dumper now tags frames with `module+offset` (`ModuleTag` in `wallpaper_cli_main.cpp`); that is what proved the faulting frames were our own exe, not a driver/FFmpeg DLL.
- [x] **🐛 FIXED (2026-10-11) Dolby Digital / Plus audio crashed the runtime and 5.1 sounded wrong:** two independent defects, both found with generated AC-3/E-AC-3 5.1 fixtures (distinct per-channel sine tones: FL 440 / FR 880 / FC 1760 / LFE 80 / SL 220 / SR 330 Hz).
  - **(a) Device layout (the crash).** `OpenEndpoint()` requested the SOURCE channel count from the shared-mode WASAPI endpoint — 6ch for AC-3/E-AC-3. The engine period then describes a format the endpoint never hands back, so `RenderStep()` wrote `framesAvail * 6` samples into a stereo-sized buffer: an overrun that faulted the runtime (0xC0000005) ~1s after `WASAPI stream started`. Fix: the device layout is **always stereo**; the source layout is kept separately (`m_srcChannels`) and mixed down.
  - **(b) Downmix was "drop the extras".** `PushFrameToRing` read only the first `m_channels` of a 6-channel frame, so centre and surrounds were discarded. Fix: explicit Dolby / ITU-R BS.775 coefficient matrix (`GetDownmixCoefficients`), with LFE deliberately excluded.
  - Also per spec: correct `avcodec_send_packet`/`receive_frame` state machine (retry the same packet on send-`EAGAIN` instead of unreffing it; drain until `EAGAIN`), NULL-packet EOF drain for the AC-3/E-AC-3 frame delay, decoder errors logged with codec + `av_strerror` instead of a silent permanent stop, and a bounded ~150 ms ring prefill before `IAudioClient::Start()`.
  - **Honest scope note:** the legacy one-receive-per-packet loop recovers the SAME frame count on these fixtures (measured: 288512 both ways) — the state-machine defect is a real spec violation but not the cause of the Dolby failure. The crash was (a). The test asserts non-regression rather than claiming a frame-count win.
  - Side finding: `av_err2str()` is a **GNU statement expression** `({...})` that MSVC rejects (C4576) — it was unusable anywhere in this codebase; replaced with an explicit `av_strerror` helper.
  - **Verified:** 607/607 ctest (7 new); AC-3 and E-AC-3 fixtures play end-to-end and survive a scene switch (exit 0); downmix proven per-channel by Goertzel (FL→L, FR→R, FC→both, LFE absent).
- [x] **🐛 FIXED (2026-10-11) Dolby/audio acceptance via sustained live test:** protocol loads default to `mute=true`, so every headless audio test was silent — the 5-minute Aari soak now unmutes via `SET_MUTE` and the audio thread reports a 10 s health line: `audio out: ~44100 fps rendered, 0 underruns`. Proven on the real Aari file (AV1+DV 4K, AAC 44.1kHz stereo): full 227.86 s played, **looped cleanly** (`225s -> 12.37s`), 300 s survived, clean SHUTDOWN exit. Decoded frame rate tracks render rate the whole way.
  - Note: the Aari file is NOT Dolby-audio — its track is plain AAC LC. It cannot test AC-3/E-AC-3 (the generated tone fixtures cover those). Its AV1+DV video now plays without the old crash; the original "poison file" attribution was the heap/ring corruption, already fixed.
- [x] **🐛 FIXED (2026-10-11) present-jitter blindness + send-EAGAIN packet loss.** Root cause research in `wall_research.md`. Added inter-present-interval telemetry (`gapP50/P95/P99/Max/Jit`) because `effFps` is a MEAN and cannot express jitter — measured `effFps=23.9` while `gapP99=62ms` against a 41.67ms nominal. Also fixed `avcodec_send_packet` returning EAGAIN: the packet was `av_packet_unref`'d and treated as accepted, permanently punching a hole in the compressed stream (bounded retry added after an unbounded retry was proven to hang the decode thread). 612/612 ctest.
  - **REJECTED after measurement:** mpv/Chromium display-sync (present per-vblank). Median gap did become a clean 6.25ms, but 4K x 160 presents/s exceeds this GPU — 346 late frames/45s, `gapMax` 98ms, effectiveFps FELL 24.0 -> 22.2. Reverted; source-rate pacing keeps `gapP50=41.87ms`/`gapP95=42.40ms`.
  - **Exonerated by measurement:** Electron UI GPU load (backdrop-filter/rAF removal changed nothing; stutter reproduced with zero Electron processes), the FFmpeg decoder (decFps 23.9, schedDrops 0), the ring scheduler (`begin+wake` = 0.19 ms), and `%120` display-topology housekeeping (< 4 ms).
- [x] **FULLSCREEN AUTO-PAUSE FIXED (2026-11).** `IsTrueFullscreenWindow(style, isZoomed)` in `PlaybackPolicy.hpp` distinguishes true fullscreen from an ordinary MAXIMIZED window (caption + non-popup, or IsZoomed => not fullscreen). `CheckFullScreenPause` applies the style check after the coverage test. 5 new tests in `test_playback_policy.cpp`. This false positive froze playback at position 2.4 s and corrupted 4 of 6 headless timing measurements.
- [x] **P0 R1 FFmpeg send/receive contract - fixed AND tested.** `avcodec_send_packet` EAGAIN no longer unrefs the packet (which punched a permanent hole in the compressed stream). `ShouldRetrySendPacket` is a pure tested policy; `sendEagainStalls` exposed via `GET_STATUS` + telemetry. 5 new tests in `test_decoder_packet_contract.cpp` run a real H.264 stream (12 KB fixture, `rex-core/tests/fixtures/packet_contract.h264`) and assert frame conservation + monotonic PTS.
- [x] **P0 R4 frame-latency waitable - AUDIT ERRATUM: already correct, now measured.** The original audit wrongly claimed the waitable was never awaited. It IS awaited in `D3D12Renderer::BeginFrame` (D3D12Renderer.cpp:1141), before rendering, first frame included, bounded 1000 ms timeout. Added `phWait` phase timing which measures **0.00 ms** - the wait never blocks. Nothing to fix.
- [x] **P1 phase timings shipped** (`phBegin`/`phDraw`/`phWork`/`phWait` + frame index) so a present stall can be attributed, not guessed. Undisturbed run: phBegin=0.06ms phDraw=0.27ms phWork=0.25ms phWait=0.00ms, gapJit=0.31ms.
- [x] **STUTTER TAIL - rare ~17 ms draw/present spike (ROOT-CAUSED 2026-10-14):** this and the "Remaining stutter tail" entry below are the SAME defect as the 2026-10-14 campaign above — resolved as driver AV1 decode hogs + startup phase-lock slips. `m_frameLatencyWaitable` was already exonerated (it IS awaited, `D3D12Renderer.cpp:1141`, `phWait=0.00 ms`). See the campaign entry for the full causal inventory and the retired cadence hypothesis.
- [ ] **AV sync is wall-clock driven, peers drive from audio.** Video position comes from a stopwatch (Engine.cpp:716-722) while audio plays independently. mpv's default is audio-master. Risk: long-session drift between the wall clock and the audio hardware clock, and no IAudioClock anywhere. See `_research_audio.md` A-axes. **Partially closed 2026-10-14:** `_research_audio.md` now has a full root-cause report (no `IAudioClock`, polling WASAPI vs event-driven, MMCSS "Audio" vs "Pro Audio", no `GetDevicePeriod`, `AUTOCONVERTPCM` risk). Audio-master clocking itself is **OPEN — real design work, not started.**

WER faulted module pointed straight at it. Fixed with stored HPOWERNOTIFY (+ unregister-before-destroy in Reacquire). Lesson: Unregister* APIs do not all take HWNDs - verify handle types.

Fixed with a real DWORD + revert on thread exit; 20 s audio scene now clean. Unblocked P2-16 verification.

- [ ] **Playback slow-motion + stutter on 4K60 (2026-10-07 report; DIAGNOSED 2026-10-09, Release runtime in app):** decode is healthy (decFps 57-59 steady) but render sags progressively (rendFps 58.7 → ~49 over ~10 min, presents ~54/s on 60fps content = literally sub-1x). Box is 3840x2160 @ 160Hz (RX 6700 XT); cadence target math is correct (min(60,160,60)=60) so the sag is in execution, with stable memory (~415MB, no leak). Suspects: 160Hz-vsync quantization vs 16.67ms PaceLoop sleep, GPU contention/thermal, or clock-Phase-4 catch-up debt. Yesterday's 32fps content held lockstep indefinitely. Next: A/B with app closed (Release vs Debug, same file+params) + watch whether fresh baseline (58.0 at 00:02) sags again. Evidence: `%TEMP%\REX\runtime-telemetry.log`.
- [x] **120fps content hard-capped at 60 (FOUND 2026-10-09 via new schedDrops counter; FIXED 2026-10-09):** Electron hardcoded `fps: 60` and PaceLoop mapped maxFps≤0 to 60, so 120fps-on-160Hz presented 60 with ~93 drops/s (effFps 14.8). Fix: maxFps passes through as uncapped (min(source×rate, displayHz)); desktop/main/runtime default fps 0. Verified headless (app closed, zero G5 risk): miles 120fps file → effFps 116.1, drops 9, repeat 7, queue 0 (was 14.8/1400+/600+). The "240fps" Dolby file reports srcFps 59.94 (mislabeled; plays 58.5). 60fps-and-below behavior unchanged by construction (source/display-limited as before). See everything.md **[2026-10-09: telemetry line extended (srcFps/convFps/schedDrops/repeat/queue/effFps/dispHz); Release rebuilt + app restarted 19:41 — fresh baseline decFps 59.0/rendFps 57.5/effFps 59.0 on the LG 4K-HDR file, dispHz=160 confirmed live, early drops 82/3495 (~2%). Login Run entry verified fixed (`electron.exe "D:\REX_doc\app" --background`). Watching for sag with full counters]** — `blk4k.mp4` (AV1 3840x2160 59.94fps, AAC) plays at **0.55x with judder**: decFps/rendFps lock at ~33 while the source is 59.94. Evidence, not guessing: `r_frame_rate`/`avg_frame_rate` are correct 60000/1001 and `m_frameIntervalMs` is the correct 16.68 ms (`VideoPlaybackController::Open :41-42`); per-frame CPU is ~1.1 ms (proc 48.6 s / ~42k frames) so it is NOT software-bound; fixed-function `Video Decode 1` engine sits at 0% while AMD VCN `Video Codec 0` runs 51–60% — the fixed-function AV1 path is the throughput ceiling for this content on the RX 6700 XT. Architecture fault (user chose fix direction): the decoder is the playback clock — `DecoderThread` paces to source rate, `FrameScheduler::Tick` ignores `targetPts` and returns latest, the render loop presents only fresh frames — so a decoder shortfall slows **time** instead of dropping frames. Fix = real-time wall clock + PTS-aware latest-frame selection + stale-frame drops + threaded SW decode (`thread_count = 1` at `FfmpegVideoDecoder.cpp:146,393,411` throttles the SW fallback: system-ffmpeg SW-1thread does 22.8 fps vs 61.9 threaded on the same file). Aggravating stutter sources found live: two stray `bench-media\mpeg4_320x240.mp4` runtimes (17:55, 18:16, ringBytes 2457600 = 320x240x8 slots) and a brief double-spawn from the soak harness itself, all presenting to the same WorkerW — second-runtime guard still missing (G5). Plan: `docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-07-realtime-playback-clock.md` (v2 clean architecture: source/decoded/present FPS separated, CadenceController, PTS scheduling). Status 2026-10-07: Phase 0 shipped (R1 hot-reload deadlock + R2 static render-once, both checked above); Phase 1 shipped (per-stage telemetry: sourceFps/convertFps/schedulerDrops/presentFps/repeatedFrames/queueDepth/displayHz live in GET_STATUS — AV1 4K60 Case-B printout: source 59.94, decode/convert/render/present all ~32.6, drops 3, queue 0, displayHz 160, decodeMsP50 0.43 ms; Phases 2–4 pending. Soak data: 34-min HDR run, CPU 3.1% avg, GPU-3D 9% avg, VideoCodec ~55%, priv 377→416 MB (+38 MB — watch item), IO 2.5 MB/s, 68387 decoded / 455 dropped / 67931 presented. NOT captured: GPU clock/power (ADL Overdrive5 dead rc=-1, HWiNFO blocked, LibreHardwareMonitor v0.9.6 located but not yet wired), P95/P99 frame times (harness didn't sample `decodeMsP50/95/99`), startup latency (runtime.log rotated by stray instances). UPDATE 2026-10-07 — ROOT CAUSE FOUND + FIXED in Phase 2: the VCN-ceiling theory is REFUTED (AV1 4K60 now decodes at 59.1 fps, decodeMsP50 0.3 ms). Actual cause: Windows sleep quantization — sleep_for(16.7 ms) wakes after ~31 ms on the 15.6 ms timer quantum, halving every 60 fps source to ~32 fps. Decode pacing now uses shared::PreciseSleepUntil (PreciseSleepTest pins 10x16.67 ms ~= 166 ms); H.264 1440p60 + AV1 4K60 both verified live at ~59 fps, 1x, drops ~0.

- [x] **B1 · CRITICAL** — `rex.settings` dereferenced a null service → startup access violation — **fixed 2026-10-07**: `ModuleOrchestrator::initialize` now publishes `EventBus` + `ConfigurationStore` into the `ServiceContainer` (as non-owning views; ownership stays with the orchestrator), and `SettingsModule::initialize` fails loudly instead of dereferencing null. Verified: `rex-core` builds, module/core suites green
- [x] **B3 · CRITICAL** — `std::stoi` on the profile auto-switch thread → `std::terminate`; plus a double `unlock()` UB — **fixed 2026-10-07**: numeric parse is validated and wrapped (a bad value is logged and skipped), matches are collected under the lock and applied outside it, and every apply is exception-guarded so a failing profile can never abort the host (`ProfileManager.cpp`)
- [x] **B2 · CRITICAL** — shutdown wiped all settings — **fixed 2026-10-07**: shutdown() now merges with the on-disk config, carrying settingsJson and restartCount through (`ModuleOrchestrator.cpp`). Verified: settings suites green
- [x] **B4 · CRITICAL** — DLL unloaded under live objects — **fixed 2026-10-07**: reloadModule() now calls mod->shutdown() (exception-guarded), destroys the old instance before FreeLibrary, serializes reload against all snapshot paths via reloadMutex_, and re-inserts the id into startupOrder_ (`ModuleOrchestrator.cpp/hpp`). Verified: builds, module/core suites green
- [x] **B5 · CRITICAL** — detached worker capturing `this` — **fixed 2026-10-07**: workers are tracked and joined in the destructor (with cancellation first); a cancelled command no longer reports success; finished workers are reaped (`CommandExecutor.cpp/hpp`). Verified: command suite 82/82
- [x] **B6–B8 · MAJOR** — shortcut self-deadlock, unsynchronized ServiceContainer, non-atomic config writes — **fixed 2026-10-07**: events/callbacks fire after the lock is released (`ShortcutManager`); `ServiceContainer` is mutex-guarded; `ConfigurationStore::saveModule` holds the lock across read-modify-write and writes via temp-file + atomic `MoveFileExA` replace (`ConfigurationStore.cpp`). Caught my own re-lock bug in loadModule via the round-trip test. Verified: settings 40/40, command 82/82
