## 🎯 Forward plan — features by stage, prioritized (2026-10-15)

> Built from the REX-vs-Wallpaper-Engine stage comparison + everything measured
> this session. Each item states WHAT, WHY, PRECAUTIONS (from hard-won
> experience: every one of these precautions corresponds to a real mistake or
> false conclusion made during this campaign), and ARCHITECTURE (where it lands,
> file:line). Priority is correctness → reliability → compatibility → policy →
> new types. Deferred scope per AGENTS.md banner is listed, not scheduled.

### P0 — correctness and safety (do these first, one at a time)

- [x] **P0-1 Scheduler live A/B — CLOSED 2026-10-16 (NEW verified; OLD fails loudly).** Control = worktree at `64f7938` + telemetry + rate fix (isolates the scheduler; clean rebuild required — stale objects from a failed `bcdfcd6` attempt poisoned incrementals, `0xC0000139` then `0xC0000005`, lesson recorded). Results on marvel, alternating arms: (1) production config (burst OFF): NEW plays 60 s clean — `content=0.94–1.03` never >1.05, `effFps≈25.7`, queue 0–1, drops frozen at startup-only, real loop wrap survives with a 1-sample dip; (2) burst=2: NEW never shows `content>1.05` (invariant holds — freezes per retain policy instead, effAvg ~1.6–3.3); OLD shows **`contentMax=1.20` (future frames presented — the defect, live)** and **crashes with `0xC0000005` on 2/3 burst-2 runs** (old fallback + racing decode = torn slot read). So: the fix holds its invariant under pressure that kills the old code. NOT re-running OLD at burst-OFF (low power: with correct rate both arms face bounded lead; null result expected; time better spent on P0-4).
- [x] **P0-2 Content-rate check (the telemetry blind spot) — BUILT, TESTED, LIVE-VALIDATED.** `ContentRateMeter` (`rex-core/src/wallpaper/playback/ContentRateMeter.hpp`, max-watermark semantics) fed from `Tick`, folded in `GetTelemetry`, plumbed to text (`content=`) + JSON (`contentRate`). 12/12 unit tests (`test_content_rate.cpp`). Live: marvel reads 0.94–1.03 while playing 1x; read 2.16–2.41 during genuine 2.4x racing (flagged ✓); 0.00 pre-first-window by design. Two self-caught errors: (1) first version summed signed PTS deltas and rebased on any backward step — read 0.01 on perfect 1x playback because pushes arrive in decode order with B-frame jitter (the hpp comment knew; the meter didn't); (2) first version had no epoch reset at decoder-side Clear. Commits `86959f1` + `97ca4fa`.
- [x] **P0-3 Wire `decodeMsP50/P95/P99` into the text telemetry — DONE.** Text line now carries `decP50/decP95/decP99` (verified live: `decP50=0.35ms` on marvel); JSON already had them. Pure plumbing, no behaviour change. Commit `86959f1`.
- [x] **P0-1b Source rate from measured timestamps (ROOT CAUSE of the marvel 2x) — DONE, live-verified.** `marvel-spiderman-miles-morales.1920x1080.mp4` declares `r_frame_rate=60/1` but carries **1222 video samples / 47.554 s = 25.69 fps** (proven by parsing mp4 stsz/mvhd; audio 2048×1024/44100 = 47.553 s cross-confirms). `ResolveFrameRate()` (`FfmpegVideoDecoder.hpp`, pure + 7 tests) prefers `nb_frames/duration` with fallback + bounds, wired at both Open and SelectStream sites with a WARN trail (`frame rate 25.70 fps from 1222 frames / 47.55 s, not the declared 60.00 fps`). Before: decode paced 2.3x too fast → ring full of future frames → 37 s permanent starve (NEW scheduler) / ~2.4x content (OLD fallback). After: 60 s of 25.7 fps playback at `content≈1.0`. 708/708 ctest. Commit `7250c9b`.
- [ ] **P0-1c Behind-phase racing (NEW defect found 2026-10-16, during P0-1).** When the ring is ENTIRELY overdue (decoder restarted behind the wall playhead), newest-due selection presents at decode speed: measured `content=2.41x` on the NEW build at burst=2 (decoder racing 2.4x). Playhead-paced consumption is the real fix — among overdue frames, present nearest the target / cap consumption at ~1 frame per interval — plus P0-4 backpressure so decode can't sustain a multi-second lead. NOTE: with burst held OFF (production default) and the rate fix in, lead stays bounded and this path is unreachable in practice; do NOT build playhead pacing until the OLD-arm A/B confirms the scheduler separation.
- [x] **P0-4 Reserve-aware decode backpressure — DONE 2026-10-16 (third attempt, stateless).** Root finding: `m_reserveMs` was NEVER written (always 0.0), so pacing was flat source-rate with no high-water throttle. Fix (`19dec3d`, 719/719): decode thread differences published position against the render thread's loop-relative wall target (`m_lastTargetPts`) → playhead-relative lead → `DecodeIntervalMs` gains park above high-water (4x interval) + burst-never-park when behind + NaN guard. Stateless per-iteration recompute (no latch → cannot deadlock like attempt 1; lead-gated not occupancy-gated → cannot throttle a healthy reserve like attempt 2). Unit: 4 new policy tests + updated 2 stale ones. Live: marvel burst-2 lead 80–118 ms bounded (was 25 s → freeze), content 1.00, under=0; obanai burst-2 lead 41–56 ms, content 1.00; both burst-OFF unchanged (marvel 25.7 @ ~1.0, obanai 59.7 @ 0.98). Preconditions met (P0-3 numbers used: none needed beyond lead; A/B covered 25.7 fps H.264 + 60 fps H.264 — no working AV1 file exists, see P2-2).
- [x] **P0-1c Behind-phase racing — CLOSED as designed-behavior 2026-10-16.** With lead-park bounding overshoot, the behind-phase (decoder loop restart up to a full duration behind) lasts ~2 s of catch-up before the band re-captures; sustained racing is impossible because decode can no longer bank a multi-second lead. Not building playhead-paced consumption: the cure would be slow-mo (wrong-er). The `content=` meter reports catch-up windows honestly.

### P1 — reliability (the app must survive real use)

- [x] **P1-1 WorkerW hide-on-UNLOAD — headless VERIFIED 2026-10-16, visibility interactive-remainder.** `tools/verify/p11_unload.cjs` (new, all green): INIT → LOAD video → PLAY 10 s (effFps>0) → UNLOAD (ok + UNLOADED event) → alive, status sane → LOAD image → alive → STOP → SHUTDOWN, no crash. Engine path was already code-verified (research Track 2). REMAINS for an interactive pass (needs the app session): eyeball the video layer hiding + OS image showing on video→image (10 s watch), plus image→video→image, video→video, PAUSE→PLAY.
- [x] **P1-2 Supervisor + app-state/library reconciliation — DONE + LIVE VERIFIED 2026-10-26.** Recovery, `AppStateManager`, the wallpaper library and saved playback state now share one replay path: `app/electron/recovery.cjs` (pure, 6 tests) hydrates a persisted `{kind, params}` entry from the gallery library's per-file settings (`mergeRecoveryEntry`) and defines `desktopLayerReady`; `main.cjs` `replayStoredEntry()` is used by **both** the boot replay and `reapplyLastWallpaper()`, so a recovered scene restores fill mode, loop/mute/volume/speed, audio mode and brightness/contrast/saturation, not just the file. Images are proven by the `LOAD` response; videos must answer `GET_STATUS` with a running/paused scene and a decoded duration > 0 before the supervisor may report `recovery-ok` (payload now carries `{ attempt, path, kind }`), and the scheduler is reconciled to the verified path. `reapplyLastWallpaper()` only replays **this session's** `lastApplied` — a user who turned restore-at-launch off must not have an old file resurrected by a crash. The renderer adopts the recovered path (no second `LOAD`). **Live:** killing the runtime PID produced `runtime:recovery start` → `runtime:recovery: video applied` → `runtime:recovery desktop layer verified` → `runtime:recovery ok (attempt 1)`, and `GET_STATUS` reported state 3 (Running) with the position advancing. Full suite 95/95.
- [ ] **P1-3 Gallery persistence live verification (headless subset GREEN 2026-10-16).** p03 (bridge + restart persistence) + verify-p05p06 (21/21: add/list/get/update/remove/readFile/thumbnail/duplicate/missing) both green on the current tree. REMAINS for an interactive pass (needs the app session): real file-picker import → tile poster visible → restart → tiles restored with posters → duplicate import → remove → missing-file. WHY: closes the restore path users actually touch.

### P2 — compatibility (Wallpaper Engine's real lesson: fall back gracefully)

- [x] **P2-1 Capability-aware decode fallback — RE-LANDED stepwise 2026-10-16, user-verified each step.** Revert (`17ff569`) restored colors (deadpool HW, `decP50=0.63ms`). Step 1 (`fccfff1`): latch+telemetry+SW-context fix only — deadpool HW @0.99x, AV1 unexpectedly plays on HW at 23.9fps (driver decodes AV1; only SW is broken). Step 2 (`8338a84`): corrected GUID probe — HEVC/VP9/AV1 all match driver profiles, all on hardware. Step 3: string-parsed test hardening — the new pinning test immediately caught a bad MPEG2 string (table bytes were right). 720/720. Lesson locked in: test data needs an independent source; plus driver-GUID logging on probe miss so the next mismatch is diagnosable from runtime.log alone.
- [x] **🐛 BUG SW-decode tint (found + fixed 2026-10-16, eyeball verify pending).** SW path emitted `SWS_CS_DEFAULT` (=ITU601) limited-range RGB while the shader consumes full-range sRGB (`SrgbToLinear`, no range expansion) — washed + hue-shifted SDR. Fixed `FfmpegVideoDecoder.cpp`: `dstCsp=srcCsp`, `dstRange=1` (matches HDR branch + HW behavior). Build clean, 720/720. Verify on a real SW fallback (needs user go-ahead — any runtime run paints the live WorkerW).
- [x] **4K120 catch-up burst (fixed 2026-10-16).** Root cause measured: decode pacing sleep caps throughput at ~107 fps (8.33 ms + ~1 ms work < 119.9 needed), so reserve drained to −3 s with burst held at 1.0. Fix: `catchUpBurst` (default 2.0) applies ONLY when behind the playhead; refill/park behavior untouched; `REX_CATCHUP_BURST` overrides (`1` = off). 4K120 H.264: convFps 101→120.6, reserve −3187 ms→+5 ms, gapJit 3.88→0.55, gapMax 50→15 ms, repeats 436→125. Cost: skip-counted drops rise (accounting, newest-due selection; content speed correct). Deadpool regression: none (content 1.00x). 721/721 ctest (new `CatchUpBurstAppliesOnlyBehindPlayhead` test).
- [x] **Catch-up hysteresis (fixed 2026-10-16).** Default-on burst limit-cycled on 4K60 (live session: drops 4.4/s, repeats 4.3/s, effFps 56.5 vs 59.9 baseline ~59.3): sub-frame deficits are pacing noise, but burst overshot → ahead → newest-due skips → behind again. Fix: hold 1x within one source frame of the playhead; rescue-burst only deeper behind. 4K120 rescue unaffected (reserve was −3000 ms). 722/722 ctest (new `CatchUpHasOneFrameHysteresis` test). Live verify pending user app restart (their runtime predates the build).
- [x] **Per-frame stage timing shipped (2026-10-16).** New `StageTimer.hpp` rings: decode side (demux/send/recv/convert/arrival/queueDepth per produced frame), present side (beginWait/drawSubmit/endFrame incl. Present/gap/target/dwell/new-vs-repeat/deadlineMiss per pass). Dumps to `%TEMP%\REX\perframe_*.csv` (256 rows each) when `REX_PERFRAME_CSV=1`; verified live on deadpool (send ~0.4 ms dominates decode; Present call ~0.15 ms; 0 deadline misses headless). Two bugs found by the smoke test: `ccs=UTF-8` fopen mode silently dropped all narrow writes (BOM-only files) — fixed to plain narrow; plus one transient wedged run (1 present in 15 s, cleared on retry — no code cause found, both threads' new code is wait-free). 727/727 ctest (new `test_stage_timer.cpp`: ring order/wrap, percentiles, CSV column pins).
- [x] **Prime-gated burst (fixed 2026-10-16, verified live).** At LOAD/wrap the ring is empty with the playhead at zero, yet lead reads hugely negative → catch-up burst fired into nothing → newest-due skips = the startup storm (historic dropPeaks 26–369). Fix: scheduler `IsPrimed()` (set on first Tick consume, cleared on Clear/Seek/Open); decode paces 1x until primed (wallpaper has no lip-sync; wall alignment irrelevant). Headless deadpool: **0 drops/0 repeats at +12 s** (was dozens–hundreds), effFps 29.9→30.0, content 1.00x. Residual: ~10 drops once when priming re-arms burst at reserve −20 ms. 732/732 ctest (3 new prime tests).
- [x] **Resume disarms burst gate (fixed 2026-10-16).** VLC-fullscreen auto-pause flapping (pause/resume every ~60 s) re-armed the startup storm on every resume: primed stayed true across pause. Fix: `DisarmPrime()` on controller `Play()` (covers auto-resume) — holds 1x until the next consume WITHOUT dropping buffered pre-pause frames (unlike Clear). Verified: no-tick starvation logic changed (ticks don't run while Paused, so resume was never seamless-burst-safe). 733/733 ctest. Exe link pending (live session holds the file).
- [x] **Load fast-forward fixed (2026-10-17, verified live).** Every apply played ~2x for seconds: `LoadScene` reset `positionBase` but NOT the wall stopwatch, so `GetPosition()` opened hundreds of seconds in (fmod duration → arbitrary loop point) while content restarted at 0 → lead hugely negative → catch-up sprint. Fix: `m_stopwatch.Reset()+Start()` in `LoadScene` (recovery path at :412 untouched — it preserves position deliberately). Fresh LOAD now: 1 drop total, `ahead=1.00x`, `convFps==srcFps` from the first seconds, `content=1.00x`. 733/733 ctest.
- [x] **Thread naming P7 (done 2026-10-16).** New `shared/ThreadName.hpp` (`SetCurrentThreadName`/`GetCurrentThreadName`); decode thread → `REX-Decode`, render thread → `REX-Render` (present rides on render). Round-trip unit-tested. 729/729 ctest.
- [ ] **🐛 Packaged app ships a stale runtime (found 2026-10-16).** `app/dist-exe/win-unpacked/resources/bin/rex-wallpaper-runtime.exe` is dated 2026-08-24 (0.6 MB vs 0.7 MB current); `getWallpaperCliPath()` (`app/electron/main.cjs:73-82`) prefers it whenever it exists, so a packaged launch silently runs August code with NONE of the October engine fixes. Decide: re-pack after engine changes, copy the fresh binary into resources on build, or retire dist-exe. NOTE: recent live sessions show catch-up behavior (convFps > source), so the user has been launching via dev (`.build`, current) — the shortcut question matters for which path they use.
- [ ] **Decode pacing servo (in progress 2026-10-16).** Open-loop sleep overshoots ~+0.5 ms/iter (convFps 3–6% under source at 60/120 fps) so the reserve pins negative and catch-up cycles forever. Reusing `PacingServo` (moved engine → `shared/`, generic/pure) on the decode thread + `decodeServoCorrMs` telemetry (text `dcorr=`, JSON) + `REX_DECODE_SERVO=0` rollback. Code written, 729/729 green; runtime-exe link blocked (LNK1104 — live session holds the file), commit pending successful link.
- [x] **Decode pacing servo shipped + verified live (2026-10-16).** `dcorr=-0.16→-0.22ms` converging on the live app session (dev binary, Gorgeous-Eyes 59.94): reserve back positive (+56 ms), underruns frozen (2), gapJit 0.25 ms (tightest in-app cadence measured), effFps 59.0/59.9, drops 2.6/s concentrated at loop wraps. Committed with full exe link + 729/729.
- [x] **Wrap-lab measured (2026-10-16, Gorgeous-Eyes 10 s file, wraps every 10.19 s).** PresentMon (~30 s, 1726 presents): base cadence tight, but 18 `Composed: Flip` presents carry 115–183 ms GPU-bound hitches (IF↔Composed mode-switch cost, ~1% of presents, ~22 vblanks each) — the mid-loop stutter. Wrap storms are CORRECT recovery (seek stall → Clear → catch-up burst → newest-due skips keep content at 1x; counted drops are accounting). Seek-stall duration still unmeasured (needs env-gated perframe/sched data). content=0.00x recurs on short-loop files (meter blind across wraps — cosmetic, nothing acts on it).
- [ ] **Diagnostic instrumentation gaps (found 2026-10-16 while assembling `decode_raw.md`):** per-stage split still missing — demux/send/recv latencies, D3D11→D3D12 copy + fence-wait durations, submit/Present/fence durations, per-frame CSV export, per-frame deadline flag, `SetThreadDescription` thread roles. Without these the 24 ms/present GPUTime attribution stays open.
- [x] **P2-2 SW AV1 failure — ROOT-CAUSED live 2026-10-16 (build limitation, not REX).** The library's Aari file (mp4, 3840x2160, measured 24.0 fps) drives the native `av1` decoder: HW correctly skipped by the P2-1 probe (driver exposes 24 profiles, none for AV1), SW opens (`pixelFormat=yuv420p`) then emits ZERO frames — `avcodec_send_packet failed (-40)` ×8 → escalation, `no frame decoded after 240 attempts` → latch code=1 → `unusable=1` in telemetry. Raw-FFmpeg benchmark harness (no REX code) also yields 0 frames (`Failed to get pixel format` + `Get current frame error`) — definitive: the shipped native AV1 decoder cannot negotiate this stream; dav1d would be the fix but that is a build/vendor decision, out of scope per "don't add backends indiscriminately". REX-side handling complete: detect → latch → report → supervisor-parkable. NOTE: this file never decoded on this box, so the campaign's "Aari 24/24" numbers refer to a different file or environment — do not cite them for this specimen.
- [ ] **P2-3 Poison-file guardrails.** CBS-AV1 assert (blk4k) + DV-HEVC-Atmos crash-loop + Mind-Blowing DV heap crash: upstream parser family, no REX-side fix. Keep the supervisor `poisonStreak` park; verify the live poison-file acceptance test (apply → bounded crashes → park). WHY: infinite crash loops are the worst user experience in the file. PRECAUTION: never auto-retry a file that kills the runtime; manual apply resets the streak by design.
- [ ] **P2-4 HDR/vendor validation matrix.** NVIDIA + HDR-on + multi-GPU combinations unvalidated; HDR present bug kept open for an eyeball check. WHY: all colour work this session was SDR-desktop only. PRECAUTION: **do not touch colour code** without a live HDR reference frame and an A/B vs a reference player; the reverted `e95072b` taught that latent-HDR fixes can't be validated on SDR.

### P3 — product policy (needed, not urgent)

- [ ] **P3-1 Unified playback-policy layer.** One place deciding play/pause/mute/unload from app state + monitor state + power conditions (per-app rules, fullscreen behaviour, profiles). WHY: lifecycle pieces exist scattered (battery saver, suspend, session lock, display-off, occlusion park, auto-pause) with no single owner. PRECAUTION: the occlusion park taught that DXGI occlusion needs FULL coverage (taskbar breaks it) — policy triggers must be validated live, not assumed from API docs.
- [ ] **P3-2 Multi-monitor independent selection + profiles.** Prove assignment, reconnect, and profile behaviour end-to-end (compositor foundations + EDID identity exist). WHY: Wallpaper Engine's clearest product lead. PRECAUTION: per-monitor controllers are independent (`VideoManager.cpp:535-567`) — any reserve/policy work must stay per-controller, never global.
- [ ] **P3-3 Small completions.** FPS counter un-hardcode (inspector shows "60"); audio-on-vs-muted benchmark; audio A/V sync (`IAudioClock`, audio-master clocking — wall-clock drift is real, design work, not started); `StopTimelineTest` flake watch (failed once under load, passes isolated).
- [ ] **P3-4 Performance modes (Power saving / Balanced / Performance) — SPEC'D 2026-10-20, implementation deferred.** Four-way selection (`auto | power | balanced | performance`; `pinned` is derived, not a fifth state); hardware-tier Auto baseline (weak→power, mid→balanced, strong→performance; tier from the adapter REX actually uses, incl. `CheckVideoDecoderFormat(P010)`); Auto downshifts **only** on battery or Windows Energy Saver (fullscreen stays an independent pause policy and is reported as a *pause*, never a mode); each mode is a preset of user-editable, removable levers + "Reset to preset"; live levers apply immediately with **no playback restart**, HDR + stream-resolution are *pending* until the next load. Not a Windows power-plan change, no elevation — only the runtime's own priority class is touched. SPEC [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-performance-modes-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-performance-modes-design.md) · PLAN [`docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-20-performance-modes.md`](docs/superpowers/plans/2026-10-20-performance-modes.md). WHY: Wallpaper Engine's nearest feature is a renderer switch + an FPS cap with no hardware awareness and no explanation; a tiered, per-lever, honest system is the engine's clearest maturity win — and it is the concrete form of P3-1's "power conditions" owner. PRECAUTIONS: (a) **nothing stacks on a red or dirty tree** — Phase 0 = green ctest baseline, then the two P3 VRAM fixes above (format-alternation re-pin; VEH fake-crash blocks); (b) the lever measurement table (Phase 0.5) lands **before** any UI or savings claim, and the FPS cap must never be described as a decoder throttle (`CadenceController.hpp:9-13`); (c) the mode must be re-sent through `INIT` on every spawn path or a crash silently loses it; (d) `perf:get` must not spawn a runtime to read persisted state (`wallpaperRuntime.cjs:172`); (e) new app-state key `performance` is unrelated to the per-wallpaper `wallpaper.performance` registry metadata (`PerformanceSection.jsx`) — keep the UI copy distinct.
- [x] **P3-5 Gallery layout + playlist dock (VS Code-style resizable panels) — PART 1 SHIPPED 2026-10-20** (the resize/layout half; the selection/favorites half is P3-5b/c/d below). SPEC [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-gallery-layout-playlist-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-gallery-layout-playlist-design.md). Shipped: (a) the app shell became a **content column** — page area on top, a **global bottom dock** below it, with the nav width reduced to one constant set (`SIDEBAR_W`/`SIDEBAR_CLOSED_W`/`CONTENT_GAP`, `SidebarLayout.jsx`) so the 240/51 and 260/71 numbers can no longer drift; (b) the dock is **resizable by its top edge** (120 px … 70 % of the window) with `-` minimize / `[]` maximize / `X` close, all persisted in `app-state.json` → `ui.*`; (c) the **gallery ↔ inspector split** got a draggable 18 px handle (the panels' old gutter) clamped to 280 px / 320 px, persisted as `ui.gallerySplitPx`; (d) the **playlist dock**: header = icon + name + picker (select / new / rename / icon / remove; "None" excluded) + `config` (the existing `PlaylistEngineDialog`) + the three window controls, body = a **grid of thumbnail-only cubes** (130 px, the name in the tooltip; click = select, double click = apply, right click = Apply / Open in Explorer / Set as active playlist / Remove from playlist); (e) **playlists own their members**, keyed by **file path** (the store's `file-N` ids are reassigned every restart) and persisted in `playlistEngine.playlists`; (f) **drag a gallery tile into the strip** to add it (`application/x-rex-wallpaper-paths`); (g) the gallery's **playlist cube retired** — one playlist manager, in the dock header; (h) **a playlist's members are now its pool** in the playlist engine (`filterPool`), so the strip and the engine agree (an empty playlist keeps the old filter-only behaviour); (i) the dock's edges are **rounded like the gallery panels** (18 px, all four corners) and the gap between the panels and the dock is **10 px** (the old 24 px page padding + 28 px section margin are gone) — both user requests during the live pass; (j) the **dock's width == gallery + inspector width** (the 24 px gutter moved from the page area to the content column, so the dock's edges line up with the panels') and the dock **floats 14 px above the window's bottom edge** — user requests; (k) the `config` entry is a **grey word** (not an icon) and the playlist settings dialog is **ported to `document.body`** — the dock clips its overflow, so a dialog mounted inside it was cut off — user request; (l) **the persisted split is clamped at render time** to the width that actually fits (`gridWidth - 320 - 18`) — found live: a saved split wider than the window pushed the panels off-screen (verified at 1000 px and 1400 px viewport emulations: no overflow, the dock stays aligned, the saved value is kept so the layout returns to it when the window grows); (m) **the app now loads the `dark` theme** (`<html class="dark">`) — it was rendering the *light* shadcn theme on entirely dark surfaces, so every shadcn component was **near-black on black**: the Inspector's text, the playlist settings dialog, the dropdown menus (the pop-up was "invisible" because of this, not because of the dock's clipping — the portal fix was still needed) — found live while checking the pop-up's contrast; (n) the **default gallery/inspector split is the measured one** — gallery **1723 px (77.1 %)** / inspector **511 px (22.9 %)**, stored as a **ratio** (not pixels) so a fresh install starts there and it holds at any window size; verified by clearing the saved split and re-measuring at a 2252 px grid → 1722 / 512. A saved pixel split still wins for an existing install; (o) **the layout store no longer rolls back a change made while the persisted state is still loading** — the async load used to overwrite the in-memory `ui` wholesale, so a click that landed in that window (e.g. *minimize*) was silently undone and then persisted as undone; the touched keys are now kept (found live: the minimize button appeared dead); (p) the **minimized dock is no longer cropped** — the 6 px resize handle is dropped while minimized so the 36 px header owns the whole strip and the icon/name, `config` and `- [] X` sit centred in it instead of being cut off (the button trio is always `- [] X`, in both states); (q) the strip is now a **grid of thumbnail-only cubes** (130 px, `repeat(auto-fill, 130px)`, name in the tooltip) with a **right-click menu** — Apply / Open in Explorer / Set as active playlist / Remove from playlist — and a **renamed built-in playlist with no members is now persisted** (the old filter only wrote playlists that had members, so a rename was silently lost); (r) the dock has **one inset** (`--dock-pad: 10px`) shared by the header and the strip, so the picker, the `config` word, the window controls and the cubes all line up (the cubes were hanging 8 px to the left of the picker) — the gaps are now 10 px to the panels above, 8 px under the header, and 14 px under the dock; (s) each cube now has its own **options**: a **remove** button that fades in on hover (a `span[role=button]`, never a `<button>` inside the cube's button) and **drag to move** — the cube reorders inside the playlist, the drop target lights up, and the reorder uses its own mime type (`application/x-rex-playlist-reorder`) so it can never be confused with the gallery's "add to playlist" payload (the dragged index is kept in a ref so the drop handler knows the source before React re-renders). Verified live: a drag moved a member from slot 0 → slot 2 and back, the order persisted, and the remove button reached opacity 1 on a real mouse hover; (t) the **app shell's window controls were swapped** — the title bar's traffic lights now run `- [] X` (yellow minimize → green maximize → red close) instead of the macOS-ish `[] - X`, so the shell and the dock's panel controls use the same order, with their colors and functions unchanged.. **Live-verified in the Electron app** (CDP-driven): dock drag persisted a fractional height (432.00006 → 478), split drag moved the gallery 1129 → 1329 px and persisted, the picker listed Favorites/Nature/Gaming/Mixed + New/Rename/Icon/Remove, a real tile dragged into the strip produced a chip with the file's name **and poster**, the drop persisted the membership by path, maximize hid the page area, close set `panelOpen:false`, and the gallery's *Playlist* button reopened the dock with the remembered playlist and height. `pnpm lint` 0 errors, `pnpm build` clean. PRECAUTIONS carried forward: the engine change is live (it applies wallpapers to the desktop) so it needs a real-device check, not just a unit test; `pnpm dev` is browser-only (no `rexDesktop` ⇒ no paths) so membership/favorites are inert there; `pnpm typecheck` does not cover `app/src` at all.
- [x] **P3-5 Sidebar rail polish — 2026-10-21.** (a) The nav groups got real breathing room: `.sidebar-group-title` margin `18px 14px 7px` → `20px 14px 14px`, so "DESKTOP" no longer crowds the Wallpapers pill. (b) The collapsed rail's hover/selected highlighters are now **true circles** (39×38 with `border-radius: 999px`, icon centred by `padding-left: 10.5px`, nav's 4 px rail dropped) instead of 12 px rounded squares. (c) The **collapse animation was rebuilt** because the old one snapped: `border-radius: 50%` turned the wide pill into an **ellipse** mid-spring, the label/group eyebrow were unmounted the instant `open` flipped (leaving an empty wide champagne bar), `justify-content` jumped the icon, and `whileHover={{ scale: 1.03 }}` scaled the pill mid-morph. Now: radius stays 999px (a shrinking pill *becomes* a circle), the label wipes out via an animated `max-width` while the link's `overflow: hidden` clips it, eyebrows collapse via max-height/opacity/margin, padding+gap animate on the same 0.42 s curve as the width spring, and the hover scale is gone (hover is a fill change — no sub-pixel text blur). Verified over CDP: radius `999px` at every sample (w 167→91→58→51) and the settled link is 39×38. (d) Build gotcha worth remembering: Tailwind v4 rejects a rule whose body parses to an empty declaration, and a **lone `\r`** (CR not followed by LF) counts as declaration content — one had been injected into `SidebarLayout.css` by a scripted edit, breaking `pnpm build` with `CssSyntaxError: Invalid declaration: ''` at that line. Fixed by normalising all 8 CSS files (`\r(?!\n)` → CRLF); if the build fails that way again, scan for lone CRs first.
- [x] **P3-6 App colour palette — SHIPPED 2026-10-20** (researched from the Windows 11 / Fluent token system and implemented). The app's own CSS carried **136 distinct colour values across 447 uses** in 8 files — 22 alpha steps of "white" for text (one of them, 0.45, = **4.41:1 → fails WCAG AA**), two spellings of the accent plus a violet second accent, two danger reds (one at 4.35:1, also failing), and panels that differed by **1.04:1** (the nav and the dock were indistinguishable by fill — only a hairline separated them). Worse, the shadcn token set was dark and correct while **zero** of the app's own rules used it: two parallel colour systems that never met. Now: **75 tokens** in `index.css` (Windows' two-layer model — base = nav + dock, content = gallery + inspector, raised = menus/dialog; four text steps at 21 / 14.4 / 7.7 : 1 with the disabled one exempt as inactive UI; the Windows dark-mode control ramp 3/5/8/10 %; one accent hue; three status colours that never ship without an icon), **257 declarations** migrated by a **role-aware** mapping (the same literal is a text colour in one property and a fill in another — `app/tools/migrate-palette.cjs`, idempotent), the **content layer became an occluding surface** (the gallery title was invisible on the dark panel; the page title was invisible on a light wallpaper — it now carries its own shadow), the title bar's controls got a surface (they float over the wallpaper), the failing text step was raised 0.45 → 0.62 and the failing danger red replaced. **Guard:** `pnpm --dir app check:palette` fails on any raw colour literal outside the palette and on any palette text step under 4.5:1. Live-verified over CDP against the *rendered* backgrounds: nav 19.6, page title 21.0, gallery title 17.99 (was 1.17), inspector 17.4, dock 19.2, dialog 15.96–16.5, menu 12.03 — all ✓. SPEC [`docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-app-color-palette-design.md`](docs/superpowers/specs/2026-10-20-app-color-palette-design.md) (with the research: Fluent 2 color, Windows color/layering/acrylic, WCAG G18, the Fluent dark-mode token values).
- [x] **P3-7 Inspector v1 - rebuilt around the six live properties; the inert surface is gone - SHIPPED 2026-10-21.**   Research: [`research-inspector.md`](research-inspector.md). The panel used to mount Appearance (17 controls with **zero**   consumers) and Renderer (codec / hardware-decode / subtitle / Ken Burns - also unconsumed: a codec cannot change per   file, HW decode is process-wide, the runtime has no subtitle track), while **none** of the six properties the runtime can   change live was reachable. It now mounts: poster + identity (real file size, the registry defaults are placeholders) ->   **Apply / On desktop + Pause-Resume** -> Playback (mute, volume, speed, loop, output; engine-backed only) -> Display   (fill/fit/stretch/center, tile for images only) -> File (path, copy, open location) -> Playlist (add to playlist).   `AppearanceSection`, `RendererSection` and the broken `ActionsSection` are deleted; Duplicate/Delete left the inspector   because they only mutated the store (the tile and the library entry survived) and the gallery tile menu already owns them.   **Persistence:** the six live settings are written per file into the library entry (debounced) and re-hydrated on load, so   volume/fit/mute/speed/loop/output survive a restart (`wallpaperSettings.js`, 10 node:test cases).   **Boot state:** main re-applies `last-wallpaper.json` at startup, so the renderer now adopts that scene as "on desktop"   (`wallpaper:last-applied` IPC + `externallyApplied` flag) instead of offering "Apply" for the wallpaper that is already up -   and without re-loading it. **Audio truth:** a container probe (`app/electron/mediaInfo.cjs`, 8 node:test cases) reads the   file header and tells the panel whether the file has an audio track; silent clips show "This file has no audio track." and   the mute/volume/output controls are hidden. It is a container-level parse on purpose: Chromium cannot decode the AC-3 /   DTS-HD MA audio in these files, so a decoder-based probe would have called the movies silent.   **Fit modes:** Tile is only offered for images - the engine rejects it ("Tile fill mode is not implemented"), which used   to raise a user-facing error toast.
- [ ] **P3-5b Multi-select in the gallery (Explorer semantics) — SPEC'D, not started.** Rubber-band marquee + Ctrl/click toggle + Shift/click range over the sorted list; bulk actions (add to playlist / remove from library) on the selection; the Inspector follows the **focused** item. Needs a path-keyed selection set in the store (`selectedPaths` + `selectedId`) — the store still has a single `selectedId`. PRECAUTION: the marquee's scroll container is `.gallery-grid` (not `.wallpaper-grid`), and the overlay must live outside the framer-motion-animated tiles. SPEC §4.1.
- [ ] **P3-5c Favorite heart + Favorites filter — SPEC'D, not started.** A heart at the tile's top-right toggling the file's favorite, plus a Favorites filter in the gallery toolbar with favorites sorting first. It also carries the **favorite-flag cleanup**: today `general.favorite` (read by the engine) and `info.favorite` (written by the unmounted `InfoSection`) are two flags with the same name, **both defaulting to `true`** — so every wallpaper is a "favorite" and the engine's Favorites-Only filter is a no-op. The durable home is the **persisted gallery library's** per-path `favorite` field (`wallpaper-library.cjs:132,154` — already there, with an `update` IPC); `general.favorite` becomes the in-memory mirror and its default flips to `false`. Until this lands, "Favorites" behaves as an ordinary container. PRECAUTION: the heart must be a `span[role=button]` overlay, **not** a `<button>` inside the tile's `<button>` (Chromium auto-closes the outer button). SPEC §3.2/§4.2.
- [ ] **P3-5d Inspector poster SHIPPED 2026-10-21; cube-drop gesture still open.** The Inspector now renders the focused file's poster as a **full-width block above the name** (`insp-poster`, using the shared `thumbCache.js` entry) — the old 44 px chip beside the name is gone, the block is **450 px tall** (44 → 220 → 300 → 450 on request) and the close X moved into the name row below it. Live-verified over CDP: `poster {w:275,h:450}` with the name/close row directly below it (poster ends at y 684). **Still open:** **dropping gallery files onto a specific cube** (insert before it — they are appended today). SPEC §4.5/§4.6.

### P4 — deferred per scope banner (listed, not scheduled)

- Web / Scene / Shader / AudioVisualizer apply paths; playlist runtime IPC + persistence; thumbnail persistence/favorites/search; SDK; marketplace; automation; themes/i18n/a11y; widgets/taskbar/start-menu (other session active; do not touch `app/src/widgets/`).

### Global precautions (every item above)

1. **Validate every metric before trusting it.** This session retired `DisplayedTime` (non-monotonic), the AMD `VDec` counter (always 0.0%), cumulative `PresentRefreshCount` gated as per-frame, and `effFps`-means-jitter. New telemetry gets a known-good/known-bad calibration before use.
2. **Controlled conditions.** Load <20%, SDR clips only (post-blank protocol), graceful stops, short runs, drops+repeats as window deltas, 5–10 runs minimum (variance is bimodal).
3. **One change per commit, verify before next.** 689/689 gate on every commit; live A/B on the problem file before calling behaviour done.
4. **Do not touch:** colour code (`D3D12Renderer` shader, `ResolveColorInfo`), `app/src/widgets/`, the 8-slot pool size (needs memory measurements first).
5. **Feature flags for rollback** (`REX_DECODE_AHEAD` pattern); never re-enable a held-off behaviour without its A/B.

- _Reference data (2026-10-26 / 2026-10-28): measured cost of a 4K60 video wallpaper on this box, same clip in both engines (VP9 8-bit SDR webm) — **Lively: 19.2–20.0 % GPU 3D, 98.1 % video-decode, 0.54–0.75 % CPU, ~305 MB WS (tray), 481 MB VRAM** vs **Paiint: 18.1 % 3D, 99.5 % decode, 0.39 % CPU, 108 MB renderer WS, 449 MB VRAM clean-process**; Paiint on a true 10-bit HDR10 AV1 clip: **27.9 % 3D, 76–78 % decode, 989 MB VRAM clean-process**. (Earlier 641 / 1,252 MB figures were measured after a format switch and include a pinned previous pool generation — see the P3 entry.) Logs: `lively_hw_usage_2026-10-26_rendering.csv`, `lively_hw_2026-10-28_samefile.csv`, `paiint_hw_2026-10-28_samefile.csv`, `paiint_hw_2026-10-28_hdr_clean.csv` (summary + method: `everything.md` → Tooling & diagnostics). Shell-open HDR (2026-10-28, 60 s each, 4 Electron procs + engine): **LG OLED HDR10 27.3 % 3D / 993 MB VRAM / 491 MB stack**, **Mind Blowing Dolby Vision 28.6 % 3D / 844 MB VRAM / 500 MB stack** — the 149 MB VRAM gap between two same-size HDR clips is per-file (imported-slot count), so treat HDR VRAM as a range, not a constant. Lively on the identical clips: **37.8 / 38.4 % 3D, 822 / 704 MB engine VRAM (987 / 854 MB stack), 525 / 524 MB stack RAM, 4.26 / 4.11 % stack CPU** — Paiint ~28 % cheaper on 3D, ~150 MB heavier on engine VRAM, and far lighter in the shell (Lively's WinUI costs 136–151 MB VRAM + ~3 % CPU; Paiint's Electron is 0 MB / 0.12-0.19 % with HW accel off). Use as the comparison baseline when the resource monitors land. **Full report — all 11 runs (T1–T11), per-process and stack tables, cross-engine synthesis, invalidated measurements, reproduction commands: [`test.md`](test.md).**_

### Legend

`[ ]` not started · see `🟡` items in `#project_status.md` for partial work · move completed items to `everything.md` ✅
- [x] **A15 · MAJOR** — fullscreen flash (~1 s XamlWindow overlay) froze the wallpaper and corrupted the position readout (20 s → 6 s) — **fixed 2026-10-07**: the pause now fires only after the fullscreen state persists 1.5 s, and pause/resume no longer rewind the clock — elapsed time is folded into a position base (`m_positionBase`) since Stopwatch restarts from zero, so resume continues instead of rewinding while the decoder keeps its own PTS. Proven live: PAUSE 3 s froze pos at 6.06, PLAY resumed to 10.08 (not 0)

### Playlist engine - change modes (2026-10-21)

- [x] **P2 - the "Loop playlist" toggle was dead: `options.loopPlaylist` was read NOWHERE.** `pickNext` always wrapped (`(currentIndex + 1) % pool.length`), so a playlist could only ever loop; the config's "Repeat Playlist" switch (and "1 run") did nothing. **Fixed:** `pickNext` now honours it - with the loop off, every file plays once (the played set lives in the settings, like the shuffle order, so it is per playlist) and the playlist then stops: `pickNext` returns null and `fireChange` leaves the wallpaper on the last file.
- [x] **P2 - the config dialog edited a playlist the engine never ticks.** The engine rotates through the ACTIVE playlist (`engine.js: activePlaylistId()` resolves it by NAME from the wallpaper on the desktop), while the dialog edited the DOCK's playlist - so "Change Wallpaper" in the config looked dead. The dialog now edits the active playlist, and its playlist picker switches the active playlist (writing the same `playlist.currentPlaylist` property the strip's "Set as active playlist" writes). The dock's new **apply** button does the same and puts the playlist's first file on the desktop; its label follows the state (`apply` -> gold `applied`).
- [x] **P1 - "When Current Ends" / "Video Ends" / "Audio Ends" can never fire.** `evaluateMode` read `getMedia(snapshot.selectedId)`, but `mediaBridge.registerMedia` is **never called anywhere in the app**, so `getMedia()` always returned null and `ended` was always false. The app had no visibility into the native playback either (the runtime emitted no scene-ended event and the app never polled `GET_STATUS`). **Fixed (native-side event, 2026-10-21):** the native runtime now **emits an `ENDED` event** when the current scene's content reaches its end. The playback controller latches it (`VideoPlaybackTelemetry::sceneEnded`, filled in `GetTelemetry()`, copied by `Engine::GetTelemetryLocked` into `EngineTelemetry::sceneEnded`); the latch is set by the **decode** thread from the published PTS (the *presented* PTS never reaches the duration - the scheduler clears the tail frames when the decoder wraps, measured: a 54 s clip looped without ever reporting its end) and is cleared by `Open`/`Seek`/`Stop`/`Close`, so a seek - the app's own playlist change re-seeks the controller - can never look like "the video ended" (that would run the change loop away: change -> seek -> ENDED -> change -> ...). The CLI's telemetry sampler samples every 250 ms and emits `ENDED {path, session}` once per scene end. The app consumes it in `engine.js` (`ensureRuntimeSubscription` -> `window.rexDesktop.onWallpaperEvent`): the signal is scoped to the wallpaper ON THE DESKTOP (the store's `appliedId` + the file path), a stale end is dropped, and it is consumed only when a change really happens - a change that cannot happen yet (no active playlist, the playlist ran out) keeps the end pending instead of throwing it away. **Proven live:** the native runtime logged `scene ended: pts=54.117 duration=54.125` + `Scene ended: ...miles-morales-walking-skyscraper...mp4`, the renderer's console showed `[runtime] ENDED`, and the app changed the wallpaper **on its own** (`runtime.log`: `Scene ended: ...miles-morales...mp4` -> 0.35 s later `Loading scene: ...obanai-x-kaburamaru-demon-slayer...mp4`, the next file of the active playlist). The latch is now a testable `SceneEndLatch` (set once from the published PTS, re-armed by `Open`/`Seek`/`Stop`/`Close`), and the CLI pairs each telemetry sample with a scene generation so an ENDED can never name the file that replaced it. 9 new unit tests (5 `SceneEndTest` + 4 `SceneEndLatchTest`) cover the decision and the latch; ctest **824/825 executed, 100% passed** (1 disabled soak). **Re-verified live 2026-10-22 on a fresh Release build + app:** `scene ended: pts=54.117 duration=54.125` -> `[Runtime] Scene ended: ...miles...mp4` -> 0.09 s later `Loading scene: ...obanai...mp4` (app log `wallpaper:video` in the same second), then the 28.3 s obanai clip ended -> `Loading scene: ...TheOdyssey...mkv`. Two consecutive ENDED-driven changes, no change loop; the active playlist was found by membership because the runtime name had reset to `'None'`.
- [x] **P3 - the playlist dock's ground is Steel Obsidian** (`#5E5E5E` = rgb 94, 94, 94) at the shell's 92 % translucency, as a local `--dock-surface` token so the rest of the shell stays warm obsidian.
